# Seam 3 — HTTP routes

Request/response shapes per route. Errors are always `{error, message, ...context}` with the
codes listed. Everything under `/api/v1` except the Open group. Auth: every route under `/api/v1`
requires a session, and a request without a valid one — a session minted through another issuer
than `CANYON_OIDC_ISSUER` is not valid (amended 2026-10-17, #2306) — is
`401 {error:"unauthenticated", message:"sign in to Canyon"}` before the handler runs, with no
session row and no audit row, including a path under `/api/v1` that no route serves: the session
stage answers before the catch-all. A session acts on an app only as far as its **level** on that
app allows (*App teams*, below); the org-wide writes are admin-only, registering this host's GitHub App and setting its front door among them. Sessions are minted only by the flows in the Open group below. A
host running `CANYON_AUTH=off` or `demo` serves a caller without a session as actor
`person / anonymous` instead, storing no session and setting no cookie, and is the only configuration in
which that happens. Such a host serves only a request whose `Host` names this machine — `localhost`, a
name under it (an app's `<app>.<env>.localhost`) or a loopback address, with or without a port — and
answers any other `421 {error:"misdirected", message:"sign-in is off, so this Canyon answers only at localhost"}`
before any route, app surfaces included: with every caller an admin, a page a browser loaded from another name could otherwise be
rebound by DNS to the loopback port and act as admin (amended 2026-10-23, #2447; its `/ready` exception removed
2026-10-26, #2449). Before any HTTP, its listener closes at accept every connection whose peer is neither loopback
nor, under `demo` with a loopback `CANYON_PUBLISHED_ADDR`, the gateway of the network the connection arrived on,
so no request, status or session exists for it (amended 2026-10-26, #2449). That gateway is served only once the
executor's `GET /published` shows this running container's port on loopback alone (contracts/executor.md; amended
2026-10-26, #2491). A session's `flow` (`browser | device`) is recorded and grants nothing and withholds nothing —
whatever runs under a person's session acts as that person — but for one org setting, off by
default: while `prod_approval` is on (Platform, the preferences row), a promotion asked under a
session that is not a browser's waits for a person to approve it in the dashboard (*Production
approvals*, Deploy & promote) — and for one override: on a host with sign-in, `promote_anyway`, which
promotes a version whose preview answered an error, counts only from a browser session (the promotions
row, amended 2026-10-08; with sign-in off no session tells them apart, and it counts from any caller) — and for one deploy: a pull request's version goes to preview only from a browser session (the deploys row, #1947; with sign-in off, from any caller) — and for one approval: a blocking finding is approved only from a browser session (the approve rows, #2204; with sign-in off, from any caller) — and, while `prod_approval` is on, for opening a prod write database session (*Database sessions*). All five rest on the session's flow, never on proving a person: whoever drives a signed-in
browser holds a browser session.
There is no push transport (decided 2026-09-04, `canyonv2-kaq`): clients **poll**
`GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id` for run progress; the run's timeline rows are seam 2. The database relay
(*Database sessions*) carries a person's bytes, never Canyon's state.

**App teams.** Levels and what each may do: design/PERMISSIONS.md, which wins on conflict; this file
states each route's minimum level and its refusals. An app's team is its grants, each
`{kind, grantee, level, roles[]}`: `email` (a person; `grantee` the lower-cased address) or `group`
(`grantee` a group name, read from the sign-in through Preferences' `groups_claim`: the claim's strings
when it is an array, its keys when it is an object — Zitadel's project roles,
`urn:zitadel:iam:org:project:roles`, map each role to where it was granted — at most 200, any other shape
none; amended 2026-10-05, #1662) at any level, and
`everyone` (the org's membership lists, never anyone the identity provider signs in; `grantee` `""`)
at `viewer` only. A caller's level on an app is the highest of the grants matching their email, their
identity-provider subject or a group their session carries — an `everyone` grant matches every member —
else `none` (an `email` grant names its address only while its `subject` is `""`; once bound — at a
sign-in whose `email` claim the provider asserts verified, `email_verified: true`, which binds every
unbound grant to that address and writes one `bind grants` row, `detail{email, issuer, projects[], rebound?}`,
`rebound` there only when it bound a grant bound under another issuer before, each such grant's
`{project, subject, issuer}` as it was — it names
that subject alone, never another person who holds the address later; a grant records the issuer it was
bound under, `issuer` that row's — an empty one, a server's from before issuers mid-upgrade, read as
`CANYON_OIDC_ISSUER` — and one bound under any other issuer is read as not yet bound — by its address, until that sign-in binds it again, `PUT …/owner`
answering `409 grant_bound` at its address meanwhile; with sign-in off a grant is bound under `none`,
which is never an issuer; the server records the issuer on a grant's and a pending request's subject
with none — `none` on the anonymous admin's at its start, before it serves, and `CANYON_OIDC_ISSUER` on
every other in the first `GET /auth/callback` each server process verifies under it, never with sign-in
off and never on discovery alone; a grant or request with
no subject records no issuer (amended 2026-10-17, #2306); an address is compared as stored,
lower-cased, and an empty address or subject names no grant; every path that writes a grant's subject —
that sign-in, `POST /api/v1/projects` for its creator, `PUT …/owner`, and a share granting an access
request, which carries the subject the request recorded — writes it only for a session whose address
the provider asserted verified, the sign-in's rule, and an empty one otherwise; a claim never rebinds a
bound grant); their app roles are the union. **Admins** pass every check on every app and are never grants; on a host with auth off every
caller is the anonymous admin, so nothing here is refused. A new app is private — `POST /api/v1/projects`
writes its creator as `owner`, and nobody becomes an owner by deploying. An app's name, owners and
summary are readable by every signed-in member **on purpose**, not by oversight: it is how a colleague
finds an app to ask for access (the no-access screen, `POST …/access-requests`). A **responder**
(design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Security responders*) is an org role, never a level: their access to an app
route is their grant's, as anyone's, and only the fleet row, the hold routes (*The hold*) and the
approver's routes (the level table's `contributor, or a responder` row) admit them without one.

| Level | The minimum for |
|---|---|
| admin, never a level | `PUT` and `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id/repository`, linking an app to a repository (#1917): which repository feeds an app is the org's choice, as which repositories Canyon's Apps see is; a person at any level below admin is `403 {error:"admin_required"}`, after an unknown or deleted app's `404` |
| `owner` | `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id` · `PUT …/name` (#1909) · `PUT …/auto-deploy` (#1923) · `PUT …/repository/keeps` (#1938) · the Access group but `POST …/access-requests` · `PUT` and `DELETE …/policies/:rule`, the app's additions · `PUT …/owner` on an app with a grant at `owner`, a person's or a group's |
| `contributor`, or a responder with no grant | what approving a blocking finding needs (amended 2026-10-13, the owner; #2204; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Security responders*): `GET …/gate-runs` · `GET …/status`, the receipt at rest · `GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id` and `GET …/sarif` · the two approve routes — a responder on an app not shared with them reads these as a contributor does and approves under the approve rows' rules; every other app route answers them `team_required` |
| `contributor` | every other route under `/api/v1/projects/:id/` and every other `/api/v1/gate-runs/:id` route: upload, check, deploy, promote, undeploy, approve, cancel, logs, secrets, and every read of the app's packages, runs, findings, deploys, status and audit rows. **Contributors promote** (the owner, 2026-09-25) **and approve advisory findings; a blocking finding is an admin's or a responder's to approve, never its run's author's** (amended 2026-10-12, #2204: the approve rows) |
| `viewer` | opening the app (*App surfaces*, below), with its roles; every read answers it the reduced row, as `none`, with the app's live addresses added (`envs`, the project read) · `POST …/access-requests`, to ask for more (amended 2026-10-05, #1706) |
| `none` | `GET /api/v1/projects`, `GET /api/v1/projects/:id` and the fleet, as the **reduced row** — `{id, name, owners[], owner_groups[], summary, access, claimable}` and nothing else · `POST …/access-requests` · `PUT …/owner` on an app with no grant at `owner` of any kind, no secret row, nothing deployed, no link to a repository and no package from GitHub — an ownerless app holding any of them is an admin's to adopt |

Two refusals, after the session stage and before the handler reads anything of the app but its team
— one `denied` row once answered (the audit read), one log line; an unknown or deleted project is `404` before either:
`403 {error:"team_required", message:"Ask <who> to add you to <app>", owners[], owner_groups[]}` when the
caller's level is below a `contributor` route's — `owners[]` the project read's, `owner_groups[]` the project read's, and `<who>` whom to ask: the first owner's display, else `a member of the <group> group`
for the first of `owner_groups[]`, else `an admin`; a read-only caller's (`viewer`) message is `Ask <who> to add you to
<app>'s team: on its page in Canyon, use Ask to join its team.`, the button that asks them (amended 2026-11-12, the
v0.9.0 walkthrough) · `403 {error:"owner_required", message:"only the owners of <app> and admins can do this: ask <who>"}`
when the route's minimum is `owner` and the caller is not one, `<who>` as team_required's (amended 2026-10-12: a
refusal says whom to ask).

An org-wide route a member may not use answers `403 {error:"admin_required", message:"only an admin can do this: ask <admin>"}`,
`<admin>` the first address, sorted, of `admin_emails` and `bootstrap_admins` together (the preferences read answers both to
every member), `an admin` when both are empty — every row below naming `admin_required` answers this message (amended 2026-10-12).

**The setup state** (design/ONBOARDING.md §2). While the org's `onboarded_at` is `null` under `oidc`
(`GET /api/v1/preferences`, Platform), every route under `/api/v1` except `GET` and
`PUT /api/v1/preferences` answers `409 {error:"setup_required"}` — after the session stage, before
the handler: no audit row, one log line — and the dashboard renders onboarding rather than the
fleet. `/ready` is explicitly unaffected and still answers `200`: the process is up, migrated and
connected, and what is missing is a person's decision. A `503` would make a load balancer pull the
host out of rotation, and then nobody could reach the onboarding screen to fix it.

## Open

Served at the root, **outside the session, audit, and rate-limit stages** of the request pipeline (the sign-in routes excepted, below)
(`recovery → logging → session → handler`, the audit row inside the handler's transaction; no CORS stage: the dashboard is served from the same origin as the API, in development through the Vite proxy): no
session is issued, no audit row is written, no rate limit applies. The auth routes below are the exception: `GET /auth/callback` and `POST /auth/device/token` mint a session and write the `sign in` audit row, `GET /auth/start`, `GET /auth/callback` and `POST /auth/device` are bounded per source address (one bucket, `429 {error:"rate_limited", message, retry_after_s}` past it), `POST /auth/device/token` is bounded per grant by its interval (`slow_down`) and never per address, `GET /auth/app` per Canyon session (its row, below) and never per address; the four sign-in routes and `POST /webhooks/github` are the only unauthenticated routes that write, and `GET /auth/app` writes only for a signed-in caller. Recovery and structured logging still run — every Open request
produces a log line. `POST /webhooks/github` is Open too: authenticated by its signature, not by
a session; the request records the delivery it accepted and writes no audit row (the steps its
work completes write theirs, as any workflow's do); never bounded per address — GitHub delivers
from a handful of addresses, so a bucket keyed on the peer would throttle a busy org and protect
nothing. With the three hand-off routes below, authenticated by a token the org holds (design/HAND-OFF.md), these are the only routes reachable without a session — everything else under
`/api/v1` requires one.

**Every unsafe method under `/api/v1` is same-origin only.** A `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` reports an origin other than this one — or, when the browser sent none, whose `Origin` is not the host's configured public URL — is refused `403 {error:"cross_origin", message:"this request did not come from Canyon"}` before the session stage reads or writes anything: no session, no audit row, one log line. A client that sends neither header is unaffected: the CLI is not a browser. Previews are served under the dashboard's registrable domain, so without this a preview's page script could act as the signed-in admin with the Lax session cookie (canyonv2-cox).

One thing that is *not* Open, stated so nobody decides it by omission:

- **App surfaces.** Apps are host-routed: `http://<app>.preview.<host>` and `http://<app>.prod.<host>`,
  `<app>` the app's id, never its display name
  (decided 2026-09-11: one rule on the demo host and on real infra, one image serves every
  environment, no path prefix compiled into the app). The server routes on the first two labels
  of the request's Host and proxies to the app's container (M4); they are not seam-3 routes. On
  the demo host that is `http://claims-portal.preview.localhost:7777` — Chrome and Firefox resolve
  `*.localhost` to the machine; Safari and command-line tools need a hosts line or a public
  wildcard name that points at the machine. **Every app is behind company sign-in and private
  until shared** (*App teams*, above): a browser without the app's cookie is sent through
  `GET /auth/app` (below) and comes back with one; a signed-in person at level `none` is sent to the
  dashboard's `/#/no-access/<app>` screen — the app's name, its owner and *Ask for access*
  (`POST …/access-requests`) — never the app and never a bare `403`; anyone else reaches the app with
  the identity token design/PERMISSIONS.md describes, their level checked again on every request.
  With auth off every visitor is the anonymous admin and reaches the app. **On Kubernetes** none of
  this paragraph's routing is the server's: an app's address is the front door's
  (`/api/v1/front-door`, Platform), the org's gateway or Canyon's oauth2-proxy signs people in, and
  the sidecar in the app's pod decides (contracts/executor.md, *On Kubernetes*).

| Route | Response |
|---|---|
| `GET /health` | `200 {status:"ok"}` — the process is up; answers even when Postgres is down |
| `GET /ready` | `200 {status:"ready"}` · **`503 {error:"not_ready", message:"not ready", waiting_on:[...]}`**. Fail closed: Postgres or the durable-run store unreachable or unmigrated ⇒ not ready. `waiting_on[]` values come from the closed set `postgres \| durable_runs \| oidc` — `oidc` for a host whose OIDC discovery has not yet succeeded — **never** driver or error text; `message` is the fixed literal shown; failure detail goes to the server's own log, not the response. Readiness reports cached probe state (background interval), not a per-request dependency query. The model backend is never a readiness input — a model outage fails the affected run closed (D10), it does not mark the host down. Neither is the setup state (the paragraph above this group): a host whose org has not finished onboarding is ready and answers `200` here |
| `GET /tls/ask?domain=` | **whether the TLS proxy in front of Canyon may get a certificate for `domain`** (Caddy's on-demand `ask`; docs: self-host/docker.md; **added 2026-10-24**, #2458) · `200`, no body, when `domain`, lower-cased and without a final dot, is the host of `CANYON_PUBLIC_URL`, `<app>.<env>.<that host>` for an app that exists and is not deleted, `env` `preview` or `prod`, or `canyon-probe.preview.<that host>` or `canyon-probe.prod.<that host>`, the two addresses the Kubernetes front-door check dials whether or not an app has that name (contracts/executor.md, `certificate`; **added 2026-11-04**, #2463: the k3s guide's Caddy asks here, and without them the front door's certificate check failed) · `404`, no body, for any other name, an app id out of pattern, a deeper name, or a name with any byte past ASCII (refused before it is lower-cased, which would fold the Kelvin sign to `k`; the host of `CANYON_PUBLIC_URL` is compared lower-cased; #2668's review) · `403 {error:"not_the_proxy", message:"only the proxy in front of Canyon asks this"}` unless the request carries no `X-Forwarded-For` (nor `Forwarded`) **and** either the connection comes from loopback or from an address `CANYON_TRUSTED_PROXIES` names, or the query carries `token=` equal to `CANYON_TLS_ASK_TOKEN`, compared in constant time: the proxy's own ask carries no `X-Forwarded-For`, and every request it forwards from outside carries one, so nobody outside can list apps through it. The token is for a proxy with no address to trust, the Helm chart's Caddy, a pod on any network, which the chart hands the token it generates (**added 2026-11-05**, #2672); with `CANYON_TLS_ASK_TOKEN` unset no token is accepted, and a missing or wrong one from any other address is this `403`. Canyon never logs the token: its request log records the path alone, never this route's query. The proxy may: Caddy can't send a header with its ask, so the token rides in the URL, and Caddy's own error log can carry that URL when an ask is refused (certmagic logs a failed renewal's ask at ERROR). So the token grants nothing past this route: it lets a caller ask which app names exist, no more; rotate it by changing `CANYON_TLS_ASK_TOKEN` on the server and the proxy together (#2674's review) · outside session, audit and rate limit; under `off` and `demo` the `421` Host rule at the top of this file applies first |
| `GET /{$}` · `GET /assets/{path}` | the built dashboard, served from the binary: `/{$}` (the exact root, never the
  mux's catch-all `/`) is its `index.html`, `/assets/{path}` its content-hashed files, both cacheable;
  `/{$}` is answered with `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`,
  so no page frames the dashboard: a preview is served under its registrable domain, where the session
  cookie and the same-origin rule both hold, and could otherwise put the device or approval page under a
  decoy for a signed-in person to click (amended 2026-10-07, #1820);
  the bundle carries no configuration, org data, or credentials — everything it shows it reads from
  `/api/v1`. Client routing is hash-based (`/#/…`), so there is no server-side fallback: any other
  unrouted path answers `404 {error:"not_found", message}` like every other route |
| CLI download | the CLI binary per OS/arch, served from the same host — byte-identical to the signed release, checksum published beside it; path pinned when the CLI ships (M6) |
| `GET /auth/config` | `200 {canyon: true, auth: "oidc" \| "off", org, device, cli, public, setup}` — what the CLI reads to learn whether this host authenticates and how. `setup` is `true` while the host is in the setup state (`onboarded_at` null under `oidc`, above), when nobody can approve a CLI sign-in yet, so a CLI whose code expired can say so, and only then; `false` otherwise and when auth is off. The only route that proves a host is Canyon. `cli` is the CLI version this host was released with, so a CLI can tell the person it is older than its host; `public` is the host's configured public URL, the same string the skill manifest hands out, so a CLI can name the session cookie the way this host set it. `device` is whether this host serves the device flow: `true` under `oidc`, `false` when auth is off; `org` is the org's display name. |
| `GET /auth/start?prompt=` | `302` to the issuer's authorization endpoint, with PKCE (`S256`), a state value and a nonce in one short-lived cookie built by the session cookie's builder: `__Host-` prefixed and `Secure` on https, `HttpOnly`, no `Domain`, `SameSite=Lax` · `prompt` is absent or `select_account`, passed on to the issuer as OIDC's `prompt` (the dashboard sends it after a sign-out), else `400 {error:"invalid_prompt"}` · `404` when `CANYON_AUTH` is off. |
| `GET /auth/callback?code=&state=` | `302` to `/` with the session cookie set · **a refusal answers a `302` too, never a body**: the caller is a browser following a redirect, and a page of machine output is not something to show a person. `302` to `/#/signin` on a state or nonce mismatch, a code exchange failure, an ID token that does not validate, an `email_verified` that is present and false, an address whose part before the `@` is not ASCII (no case folding Canyon and the provider might do apart), a `sub`, an address or an `hd` over 200 characters (an identifier is never cut to another's; only the display name is cut), or an email on neither membership list (`CANYON_OIDC_ALLOWED_DOMAINS`, `CANYON_OIDC_ALLOWED_EMAILS`) · `302` to `/#/setup-required` when the refusal is that the host is still being set up (the setup state, the paragraph above this group): the email is not an admin's, so it is not this person's turn yet, and the screen says so. Both are ordinary hash routes of the dashboard, so the redirect carries no query and no fragment of its own beyond the route: the screen renders from its own reads. No detail is echoed; the reason goes to the log, and a person the provider vouched for and Canyon turned away is a `sign in refused` row too (the audit read). `404` when `CANYON_AUTH` is off. |
| `GET /auth/app?to=&flow=` | where an app surface sends a browser without the app's cookie (*App surfaces*, above) · reads the Canyon session cookie itself — outside the session stage, so never a `401` — and without a live session sets a short-lived `canyon_app_to` cookie, built as the sign-in state cookie is, and `302`s to `/auth/start`; `GET /auth/callback` then `302`s here instead of to `/` while that cookie is set, never through a query `next` · `to` must be exactly `<app>.<env>.<public host>` — an app that exists, `env` ∈ `preview, prod`, the host's configured public host — and `flow` the lower-case hex sha256 of the app's flow cookie, else `400 {error:"invalid_to"}` · a caller at level `none`: `302` to `/#/no-access/<app>` · else `302` to `<public scheme>://<to>/.canyon/callback?code=`, the code single use, good for 60 seconds and bound to `flow` · bounded per Canyon session, never per address — behind an ingress every browser shares one address, and an app page whose app session has lapsed sends its every request here: 60 at once, then one a minute, so a session writes at most 60 rows and one a minute after them; past it `429 {error:"rate_limited", message, retry_after_s}` · a caller without a session is sent to `/auth/start` uncounted, under that route's own bound · each code is one app session row: the app's callback spends the code the first time it is presented, and deletes the row when that presentation fails (another browser's flow, another app, past its 60 seconds); a row whose code nobody presents, and every app session, is deleted with the Canyon session it was issued under, when the expired session is swept (design/AUTH.md §5) · no audit row · `404` when `CANYON_AUTH` is off |
| `POST /auth/device` | `200 {device_code, user_code, verification_uri, verification_uri_complete, expires_in, interval}` (RFC 8628; `user_code` is 8 Crockford base32 characters from `crypto/rand`, `expires_in` is 600) · `404` when `CANYON_AUTH` is off |
| `POST /auth/device/token` | `{device_code}` → `200 {session, expires_at, subject, display, org}` · `400 {error:"authorization_pending" \| "slow_down" \| "expired_token" \| "access_denied"}`. A device code is single use: consumed on first success, and dead after `expired_token` or `access_denied`. `404` when `CANYON_AUTH` is off. |
| `POST /webhooks/github` | GitHub's deliveries to this host's Canyon App (M8) · judged in this order: `413 {error:"too_large"}` past 5 MiB of body · `503 {error:"not_configured"}` when no App is registered · **the App is the one whose id `X-GitHub-Hook-Installation-Target-ID` names** (#1914: one App per organization) · `401 {error:"bad_signature"}` when no registered App has that id, or unless `X-Hub-Signature-256` is the HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body under that App's webhook secret · `204` an event not served — served are a `push` to the repository's default branch, a `pull_request` `opened` or `synchronize` whose `head.repo.full_name` is the repository's own (**a fork's is `204`**, nothing written), and `installation` `created` and `deleted`, and a push or pull request from an installation with no live row of that App is `204` too · **served too, writing only links and installations, but for the import below** (#1917): `repository` `renamed` and `transferred` (each link to that repository id takes its new `full_name`), `deleted` (each link's `state` becomes `deleted`; the app keeps every version) and `archived` (each `linked` link's becomes `archived`) and `unarchived` (`linked` again), and `created`, served only in an organization whose App's `imports_new` is true and `204` otherwise (#1923); `installation_repositories` `removed` (each link to a removed repository becomes `removed`) and `added` (a `removed` link to an added one is `linked` again) · each link whose `state` changes is one audit row `repository changed` on its app, `detail{repo_id, full_name, state}`, by `github:<login>` of the delivery's sender · **in an organization whose App's `imports_new` is true** (#1923), a `repository` `created`, and each repository an `installation_repositories` `added` names, that no link names — of any state, at any folder, by `repo_id` — becomes an app as the import makes one — its name the repository's, its id the host's, no owner, linked at its root — by the agent `github:<login>` of the delivery's sender, with the import's audit row; it is reviewed at its next push, not on arrival · the check is written with that App's key · **a pull request is gate-only**: its head is ingested and checked as a push is, stored as `source.kind: "repo-pr"` with the pull request's `number` and head `branch` recorded on the package (#1947), moves no `main`, writes no `declare resource` row, and is reviewed and reported exactly as a push is, but **is never the project's current package** — not pulled, not listed, not a run's default, not `latest`, its declarations not the project's, its runs never the project's `gate_state`, `last_run`, receipt, fleet tiles or policy preview (a blocked pull request is a failed check, not a blocked app — though its run takes the project's one run slot and is listed with its runs), and it is never named to a deploy (`404`) but by a browser session once its review passed (the deploys row, #1947), though a gate run may name it; a later upload of the same tree makes it current as that upload; a later push of the same tree (the merge) makes that package current as `repo`, writing the declaration rows then · `202 {}` accepted, and a redelivery of an accepted delivery id is `202 {}` again with nothing started twice · **a push to a repository Canyon keeps** (*Kept in GitHub*, below; #1926) moves its link's `kept_commit` to the push's head, and a push whose head is the commit Canyon itself kept starts no check and makes no package · an accepted push becomes the project's current package exactly as an upload does — the same ingest and `[resources]` check, `source.kind: "repo"`, `source.ref` the pushed commit, `source.actor` `github:<login>` — with the `declare resource` audit rows an upload writes for a first declaration — and is reviewed by a gate run, never a deploy, started as `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/gate-runs` starts one (`started_by` the agent `github:<login>`, which can never approve a finding — the run keeps the sender's numeric GitHub id too, and every person who linked that account is its author (`GET /api/v1/github/link`, #2228) — and one audit row `gate run from github` `detail{package}`) — **unless the app's `auto_deploy` is on** (#1923): then the push's package is deployed to preview as `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/deploys` deploys one, by the same agent, reviewed first as every deploy is, and the audit row is `deploy from github` `detail{package}`; GitHub never deploys a pull request (a person may, the deploys row, #1947), and nothing from GitHub ever reaches production; either way the check below ends on the run's verdict — a deploy's check ends when its verdict row is written, as a gate run's would, and never waits on or reports the build or the deploy, whose outcome is the app page's —, and reported as a GitHub check named `Canyon gate`, which always ends: `failure` naming the refusal when the tree is refused · `success` when the run ends `clean`, `success` titled *Every blocking finding approved* when it ends `blocked` with each blocking finding covered by an unexpired approval of the project's — the run a deploy goes on from (the deploys row; **amended 2026-10-04**, #996) — `failure` with its counts when `blocked` otherwise, `failure` titled *The review didn't finish* with its `failure_reason` in words when `failed` (*A failure reason in words*, amended 2026-10-04, #1512), `failure` "The review did not finish" when it has not ended 35 minutes on · while another run of the project is `running` the start is tried again every 15 s, 40 times, then the check is `failure` "Another Canyon run for `<app>` was in progress; push again" — a review that was due and not done never passes· a start past the gate-runs rate limit is not tried again: the check is `failure` "Canyon's limit of N reviews an hour for this app is reached. Push again after HH:MM UTC." (the pusher's login in place of "this app" past the per-actor limit) · each step (GitHub's calls, the database's) is tried three more times, backing off, and a check that still cannot finish is `failure` "Canyon could not finish this review", never left in progress · each finding of the stored verdict — and nothing else is read — is an annotation: `path` and `start_line`/`end_line` from its location, no columns, `annotation_level` `failure` when it blocks, `warning` at severity `high` or above, else `notice`, `title`/`message`/`raw_details` its `title`/`problem`/`fix` and `required_end_state` · at most 50 annotations per request, each request appending, at most 200, the summary ending "…and N more findings in Canyon." past them · **a repository maps to the apps linked to it, by its repository id and a folder, never by its name** (**amended 2026-10-09**, #1917: a repository named like an app made that app's current package): every `linked` link to `repository.id` is one app, and each is checked on its own — its own package, run and check · a link's `folder` is `""`, the repository's root, or a path under it: a root link packages the commit's whole tree, as before; a folder link packages only the entries under its folder, with the ingest's caps counted on the folder alone, and reads the rest of the archive past them up to 4 GiB of file bytes and 1,000,000 entries in all, past which the check is `failure` titled *Canyon refused this commit* naming the bound, and nothing is packaged and `canyon.toml` read at the folder's root, and **only when the folder changed**: Canyon reads the folder's git tree id at the commit from GitHub's trees API, and when it is the tree id of the link's last push whose review let it through — `clean`, or `blocked` with every blocking finding approved; a pull request's never moves it, nor a review that did not finish or blocked, so the push that merges a pull request is packaged and a blocked folder is reviewed again — nothing is packaged or reviewed and the check is `neutral` titled *No change under <folder>* · a folder the commit does not have is `failure` titled *<folder> isn't in this commit*, its summary *Canyon packages this app from <folder>. Move it back, or an admin links the app to its new folder.*, and nothing is packaged · a commit whose `canyon.toml`, at the link's folder, has an `app` line naming another id than the linked app's is `failure` titled *canyon.toml names <id>, not <app>*, its summary *This repository feeds <app>. Change the app line to <app>, or delete it.*, and nothing is packaged (#1938): an `app` line is optional, and one that is there says which app the code is · the check is named `Canyon gate` for a root link, so existing required-check rules hold, and `Canyon gate · <folder>` for a folder link · a push or pull request of a repository with no `linked` link is one check, `Canyon gate`, `neutral`, titled *This repository isn't linked to a Canyon app*, its summary *An admin links it in Settings › GitHub.*; nothing is packaged · a link that is `deleted`, `archived` or `removed` is not checked |
| `POST /releases/:id/report` | **the org's platform reports on a release** (design/HAND-OFF.md §4; the shapes are `release.schema.json`'s `report_*`): `Authorization: Bearer <host token>`, the one the platform-webhook row's PUT answered or the platform-repo row's first save did (each row keeps its own; #2470), compared as its SHA-256 · `{status: "deployed", address, digest, resources?}` · `{status: "failed", reason}` (plain text, 1–1024 characters) · `{status: "removed"}` → `200 {}`, recorded in one transaction with its audit row, actor `{kind: "agent", subject: "platform", display: "<org>'s platform"}`: `deployed` writes the environment — `url` the `address`, `image_digest`, `package`, `gate_run`, `deployed_by` the run's actor, `promoted_by` on `prod`, `run_by` `org` — and the run's `deploy.completed` row (events.md); `failed` and `removed` write theirs — `action` `platform reported deployed` `detail{release, env, digest, url}`, `platform reported failed` `detail{release, env, reason}`, `platform reported removed` `detail{release, env}` — and `removed` takes the environment off the project read · **`409 {error:"held", message:"production is held"}`** (*The hold*) for a `deployed` on production while it is held, late or raced — no reason and no admins: the token is the platform's — nothing is written, and Canyon sends another `remove` naming the release's package and image, so the org's platform takes down what it just started; the hold reads `pending` on that `remove` until the org reports `removed` — and every `deployed` report on production while it is held sends one, a superseded release's too (`409 superseded`), whatever run still waits on it and whatever remove is open: the platform says it runs it now · **`409 {error:"digest_mismatch", expected}`** for a `deployed` whose `digest` is not the release's image's: nothing goes live, one audit row `platform reported another digest` · **`409 {error:"resources_missing", missing, message}`** (#2452) for a `deployed` whose `resources` leaves out a resource the release lists — the key absent, or `[]`, on a release that lists one among it — after every other refusal and never over `409 held`: `missing` the names left out, sorted, `message` `the report has no endpoint for <names>: send one for every resource the release lists`, `<names>` joined with `, `; nothing goes live and no endpoint is written, the release keeps the names for the environment's pending line (`pending.missing`, the project read), the run waiting on it gets one `build.log` line (events.md) and keeps waiting, its 30 minutes as they were, and one audit row `platform reported deployed without resources` `detail{release, env, missing}` per refused report, which the bound per token bounds; an entry the release does not list, one twice, or more entries than it lists is still `400 invalid_report` · **`resources`** (#2432), `[{name, endpoint}]`: where each resource the platform provisioned for the release is, **one entry for each resource the release lists** (amended 2026-10-23, #2452: it was optional) — the key absent, or `[]`, only on a release that lists none — `name` one of the release's package resources (`release.schema.json`'s `deploy.resources`), each at most once, so never more entries than the release has resources; `endpoint` a place and never a credential, parsed with Go's `net/url`, never a pattern: printable ASCII (`0x21`–`0x7e`), at most 255 bytes, none of `%` `\` `@` `?` `#`; its scheme the resource type's — `postgres` takes `postgres` or `postgresql`, `s3` takes `s3` or `https` — no userinfo, no opaque part, no query (not even an empty `?`), no fragment; its host lower case — a domain of one label or more (a cluster's service name, a bucket; each label at most 63 characters, the whole at most 253) whose last label is not all digits, or an IP address, an IPv6 one in lower case too; a port, when there is one, 1–65535 with no leading zero; its path empty or one `/[a-z0-9_-][a-z0-9._-]{0,62}` segment, the database or the bucket, so never `.` or `..`; and it prints back exactly as sent · the endpoints are written on the write's own path alone — never on a `409` nor on the duplicate's `200 {}` — one row per name for the release's app and environment: every `deployed` that is written replaces what the environment's earlier release reported, a report without the key as `[]` does, so a release that lists none clears them (amended 2026-10-23, #2452: one without the key left them as they were); they go when a project delete's `remove` reports `removed` (the delete row) · **`address`** is parsed the same way, `https` alone, its path any `/`-separated segments of unreserved characters (`[A-Za-z0-9._~-]`), none of dots alone (`.`, `..`), with or without a trailing slash (amended 2026-10-23, #2432: `https://h/?token=x` passed before; userinfo, a query, a fragment, a percent-escape, a backslash, an upper-case host, a dot segment, an all-digit last label such as `https://2130706433/` and a port with a leading zero are refused now) · any of these, or any other body, `400 {error:"invalid_report"}`, checked after the release is found · a report after the run's 30 minutes is recorded the same and the run's state stays · the same status again is `200 {}` and writes nothing · a `deployed` after `removed`, or any report — `deployed`, `failed` or `removed` — on a release a newer release of the same environment superseded, or that a deploy Canyon has run there since followed (the environment switched back to `run_by: canyon`, design/HAND-OFF.md §1 *Switching*; recorded on the release as the access row says, so it stands after Canyon undeploys its own deploy, #2469), is `409 {error:"superseded", release}` — `release` the newer release, else the reported one itself; a `deployed` on a `deploy` release an undeploy's `remove` superseded, the environment still offline, has Canyon send another `remove` and write its audit row (*The brake*, which says when) · `401 {error:"bad_token"}` — a replaced token's too · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown release · bounded per token, 60 a minute, then `429 {error:"rate_limited", message, retry_after_s}` · a `deployed` on a `remove` release — an undeploy's, a hold's, the brake's or a delete's — is **`409 {error:"not_a_deploy", message:"this release is a remove: report removed or failed"}`** (#2432), before anything is written, a hold's remove among it, held or not · **a `deployed` on a release of a project being deleted or deleted** (#2432) is **`409 {error:"project_deleted", message:"this app is deleted or being deleted: Canyon asks your platform to remove it"}`**, writes nothing, and sends one `remove` with `delete: true` for the release's environment — none while one is open there, so the same report again sends nothing — whatever release it names, superseded, undelivered or failed · **a `delivery: repo` release** (#2470) takes reports as any does — a platform's `failed` lands, and a `deployed` goes through the same path, though the check-in (the access row) is what usually settles it · **refusal order:** `401`, `404`, `400`, `409 not_a_deploy`, `409 project_deleted`, `409 superseded`, `409 digest_mismatch`, then the duplicate's `200 {}` that writes nothing, then `409 held`, then `409 resources_missing`, then the write (amended 2026-10-23, #2452: `held` was missing from this list) |
| `GET /access/:app/:env` | **the app's access file, for the sidecar in the org's pod** (design/HAND-OFF.md §5): `Authorization: Bearer <access token>`, the one `GET /releases/:id/access-token` answered for this app and environment · `200` the access file exactly as `PUT /access`'s `access` object (contracts/executor.md), the same producer · **a stopped release admits nobody** (amended 2026-10-24, #2450): a token that passes the check is answered `200` the **deny-all file** while its environment is stopped — production held (*The hold*; `prod` alone), or the project deleting or deleted (`deleting_since`, then `deleted_at`) — or its own release is: the release the token was minted for is itself a `remove`, a `remove` of its environment was recorded at or after it — from the moment Canyon records it, in the transaction of the undeploy, the hold, the brake or the delete that sends it, whatever the platform answered or reported on it since — or a deploy Canyon ran there has followed it (a switch back to `run_by: canyon`: the org's pods are no longer the environment's) — recorded on the release itself when Canyon's deploy is written while the org's setting is `run_by: canyon`, so it holds after Canyon undeploys its own deploy and its deploy row is gone, and a write of Canyon's under `run_by: org` — a patch, a deploy that started before the switch — stops no release (amended 2026-10-27, #2469: the deploy row alone reopened the token, and stopped the org's newer release while it stood). The release's own conditions are read per token, in the one lookup of the token check, so a newer release recorded since never gives an older one's pods the list back: the access file's shape, `updated_at` now, `groups_claim` and `subject_issuer` `""`, `members` `{domains: [], emails: []}`, `admins` `{emails: [], claim: "", value: ""}`, `roles` `{}` and `entries` `[]` (release.schema.json's `access_deny_all`), so every sidecar of the environment refuses everyone at its next pull, a minute, with no grace — never a `401`, which the sidecar reads as Canyon unreachable and rides out for `access_grace`; the token check is the same either way. A release a `remove` followed stays stopped: its pods admit nobody again, whatever is deployed after, and only a newer release's own token pulls the access file; a lifted hold has sent its `remove`, so production's pods stay stopped until a new release of it runs · `401 {error:"bad_token"}` on any other token, a dead one or another environment's, and on an unknown app or environment — the same answer, so nothing enumerates apps · **never bounded**: the sidecar of every handed-off app pulls it every minute, and a bucket would throttle the fleet's own sign-in · **a pod's check-in** (#2452): once the `200` is certain, Canyon records `seen_at`, now, on the release the token was minted for — in a statement of its own after the token check, which it never changes; at most once per 30 seconds per release, a release seen in the last 30 seconds or locked by another pull skipped, never waited on; best effort, so a write that fails is logged and the pull still answers the file, never late and never refused for it; a refused pull writes nothing — read as the project read's `envs.<env>.seen_at`; a deny-all pull checks in as any `200` does: a pod still pulling is one the platform has not taken down, which an environment *removing · waiting on your platform* says beside it, in amber (`seen_at`'s rule; a deleted project's writes nothing) · **`Canyon-App: answered`** (#2470): the sidecar sends it on a pull once the app accepted a connection on its port just before; no other value is read, and without it a check-in is a check-in and nothing more · **a check-in settles a `delivery: repo` release** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3b, *Deployed*): when the check-in is written on a release whose `via` is `repo`, still `accepted`, a `deploy`, and the pull carried `Canyon-App: answered`, Canyon — after the answer, never delaying or changing it, one at a time per release and backing off per release on any error — first reads, before any git or API call, whether the release is still the environment's newest, not removed since and, on production, not held: if it is not, it goes straight to the decision path below, which answers it `superseded` or held with their removes, never `release tree mismatch`; else it fetches the branch the release was committed to and needs the tree at `<root>/<app>/<env>` to be the one it rendered, else the release is `refused`, one `build.log` line and one audit row `release tree mismatch` `detail{release, env, branch, expected, found}`; for a release that opened a pull request, it reads the pull request — open is a no-op until a later check-in, closed unmerged is `refused` (`pull request closed`), merged with a head other than Canyon's commit, into a branch other than production's, or from a branch not this repo's own is `refused` (`pull request changed`), each one line and one audit row `detail{release, env, pull_request}` — and an error reading it settles nothing; then, under the ledger lock, the report row's decision path, as a `deployed` report carrying the release's own digest, the `address` and the endpoints Canyon rendered (checked as the report row checks them), with its every refusal — `not_a_deploy`, `project_deleted` and its remove, `superseded` and its remove, the hold and its remove — answered to nobody: the audit row is **`release live by check-in`** `detail{release, env, digest, url, commit, proof: "check-in"}`, actor `{kind: "canyon", subject: "check-in", display: "Canyon"}`, via `canyon` · a read: no audit row |
| `GET /releases/:id/access-token` | **the org's pipeline fetches the sidecar's token**, under a host token (either row's) or the **fetch-only token** (*The deploy repo*, below; #2470: kept on the platform-repo row, External Secrets' store holds it, and it opens this route and nothing else, compared as its SHA-256; it is not bound to an app — design/HAND-OFF.md §10's accepted residual risk), and puts it in its own secret store — never Canyon, never a webhook, never a commit: `200 {name: "CANYON_ACCESS_TOKEN", value}`, the same value every time while the release is that environment's newest; minted per release, it dies when a newer release of the environment reports `deployed` · `401 {error:"bad_token"}` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or superseded release · **never bounded**: it is behind the host token, whose reports are bounded (the report row) · no audit row |

`slow_down` is RFC 8628's own value and belongs in the union: without it a CLI polling too fast
gets an error it has no rule for, and every conformant client already knows to back off on it.

The seam-3 contract test covers `/health`, `/ready`, `/auth/config`'s shape and the error shape of
an unrouted path (`/nope`) — `cmd/canyon-server`, `TestAuthConfigIsRoutedAndErrorsCarryTheContractShape`:
with `CANYON_AUTH` off, `/auth/config` answers `{canyon: true, auth: "off", org, device: false, cli, public, setup: false}`
and the four flow routes answer the `404`; the routes themselves join the check as they land. The
check's table is pinned by transcription, nothing reads this file: a new Open row is a test change too.
`GET /{$}` and `GET /assets/{path}` join it with the bundle (M3); the download row joins when its
path is pinned — pinning a path is itself a seam change (rule 5). `POST /webhooks/github` is in it
with its `503` on a host with no App registered; `github-app/hook_test.go` holds the rest of its row.

## Projects & packages

All routes in this group and below are under `/api/v1`.

| Route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/v1/projects` | `{name}` or `{id}`, never both (**amended 2026-10-09**, #1911: a first deploy never fails on a name) · **`{name}`** — the app's display name: one line of plain text, 1 to 100 characters, with no character that can hide or reorder text (a control or format character, or a line or paragraph separator), and never unique, so a hundred people can each have a `claims-app`; **the server picks the id**: `<word>-<word>-<suffix>`, two words from Canyon's fixed, curated list and three lowercase letters or digits from a cryptographic source, e.g. `funny-snake-4k2`, never the id of any project, live or deleted, so the create never answers `exists` or `deleted` · **`{id}`** — exactly that id, as `canyon deploy --app` and an existing `canyon.toml` name it: it must match `^[a-z]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$`, at most 55 characters (a DNS label with room for its container's suffix: the id is the first label of the app's hostname, App surfaces above, and `<id>-preview` — the container's name on the app network, contracts/executor.md — must be a label too, so 55 + 8 = 63); it is refused, never slugified; its display name is the id · either way the id never changes, is the app's address, and is what every other route and `canyon.toml`'s `app` name; the display name is for people alone | `201 {project}` — the id the server picked is the answer's `id` · the creator is written as the app's `owner`: an `email` grant of the session's address, subject and display (*App teams*) · `400 {error:"invalid_name"}` for a `name` refused above · `400 {error:"invalid_id"}` for an `id` refused above · `400 {error:"bad_request"}` for a body with both or neither · `409 {error:"exists"}` · `409 {error:"deleted", message:"An app named <id> was deleted, and its name is not reused: name the app something else"}` for the id of a deleted project, whose ledger stays under it, so a create never reads as the app existing while every route answers it `404` (**amended 2026-10-04**, #864) — both for an `{id}` alone |
| `GET /api/v1/projects` · `GET /api/v1/projects/:id` | — | `{project(s)}` — project = `{id, name, owners[], owner_groups[], summary, access, claimable, stack?, gate_state, envs{preview?, prod?}}` · **`owner_groups[]` is the names of the groups holding a grant at `owner`, sorted** — `[]` when none — on every shape, so a screen names the group to ask where no person owns the app (amended 2026-10-12) · **`claimable` is whether this caller's claim (`PUT …/owner`, below) would be accepted now**: `true` for an admin and an owner, and for anyone on an app open to claim, by the claim row's own test — on this read, the list and the fleet, whole and reduced (amended 2026-10-12): the dashboard offers *Claim* only where it is `true`, since `owners[]` names email grants alone and says nothing of a group's, a secret, a deploy or a repository · `name` is the app's display name, as `POST` set it, and need not be unique (an app made before 2026-10-09 has its id as its name); `id` is unique and is its address (#1911) · **the single read adds, at `contributor` and above, `auto_deploy`**, whether a push deploys to preview (#1923), and **`repository`** (#1917), the app's link, or `null` without one: `{repo_id, full_name, folder, state, keeps, kept_commit, kept_error}` (*Kept in GitHub*, below; #1926), `state` ∈ `pending \| linked \| deleted \| archived \| removed` — `pending` a kept repository not made yet — — `deleted` the repository was deleted on GitHub, `archived` it is read-only there, `removed` Canyon's App can no longer see it — at `contributor` and above; the reduced row carries none · **below `contributor` the row is `{id, name, owners[], owner_groups[], summary, access, claimable}` and nothing else, but at `viewer` it adds `envs{preview?, prod?}`, each `{url, state?, seen_at?}` and nothing else** — on this read, the list and the fleet (*App teams*) · **`hold?`** (*The hold*) while production is held, `{by, at, reason, version}`, at `contributor` and above, to a responder and to an admin; to a responder below `contributor` the reduced row adds `envs{prod?}`, `{url, state?, seen_at?}`, and `hold?`, and nothing of preview but a viewer's; to anyone else below `contributor` a held production reads exactly as one with nothing deployed · a viewer's `envs.<env>` is there only when something is deployed there and Canyon runs it, or the org runs it with a `url` (never one waiting on its first release, `{run_by, pending}`), so a person shared the app can open it; its `state` is the one below, asked in the same executor calls, on the fleet row and on this read (the list carries none); `envs` is `{}` at `viewer` when neither qualifies and absent at `none` (#1521) · `summary` is the current package's `description` (the upload row), `""` when it has none · `access` is `{level}` below `contributor` — `level` ∈ `viewer \| none`, the caller's own on this app — and from `contributor` `{level, everyone, people, groups, requests?}`: `level` ∈ `owner \| contributor \| viewer \| none` (an admin's is whatever their grants make it); `everyone` whether it is shared with everyone; `people` its `email` grants below `owner` and `groups` its `group` grants, so an app is private exactly when `everyone` is false and both are `0`; `requests` the count of its pending access requests, answered to its owners and admins only · `gate_state` ∈ `never_reviewed \| pending \| running \| clean \| blocked \| failed` (the latest run's state, never a patch run's — *Patch runs*; `never_reviewed` when there is none) · `envs.preview` / `envs.prod` present only when something is deployed there, or a release is open there (`run_by: org`, design/HAND-OFF.md): `{url, package, label?, image_digest, deployed_at, gate_run, run_by, pending?, pull_request?, patch?}` — `patch` the environment's patch state, at contributor and above (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-18, #2086) — `pull_request` `{number, branch}` while preview runs a pull request's version (#1947), absent otherwise, its `label` absent: a screen names it by its pull request, or, while a release is open there and nothing is deployed there yet, `{run_by, pending}` alone (`release.schema.json`'s `env_pending`) — `run_by` ∈ `canyon \| org`, who runs it, recorded when the row is written (a row written before the column answers `canyon`); `pending` `{release, event, sent_at, state, via, missing?, pull_request?}` while a release of this environment is open — `event` ∈ `deploy \| remove`, `state` ∈ `sent \| accepted \| undelivered \| refused`, `via` ∈ `webhook \| repo` how it reaches the platform, the release's own (#2470: a screen says a repo release's state in the deploy repo's words, D5), `pull_request` `{url, number}` the pull request a `delivery: repo` release opened in the deploy repo while it waits on it, absent otherwise — like the rest of `pending` at `contributor` and above, so its link, which names the deploy repo, reaches no viewer — `missing` (#2452) the names of the resources the platform's last `deployed` report on it left out (the report row's `409 resources_missing`), sorted, on a `deploy` while `sent` or `accepted`, absent otherwise — and after `undelivered` or `refused` until the environment's next release is sent or it is undeployed; absent otherwise; the screen and `canyon status` draw it as *waiting on your platform since <sent_at>* (`deploy`, `sent`/`accepted`), *removing · waiting on your platform* (`remove`), *Your platform did not accept the release* (`undelivered`), *Your platform refused the release* (`refused`) and, with `missing`, *Your platform's report left out where <names> is* in place of the first — `are` for more than one, `<names>` joined with `, ` (#2452); **`answered` is what the app itself answered once it was deployed** (**amended 2026-10-05**, #1740: a stock Django answering `400 DisallowedHost` to every request read as a clean Live, readiness being a TCP connect), on an environment Canyon runs, on the status read alone and on the run's `deploy.completed` row (events.md), which the CLI prints: after each deploy step that starts a container, Canyon asks the app **one** `GET /` past Canyon's sign-in, from where the app runs — `Host` the environment's address as its `url` names it, port included, no cookie, no credential, no redirect followed, 5 s, a line read from at most the body's first 64 KiB: on Docker the server, straight to `<app>-<env>:3000` on the app's network as its proxy reaches it, on Kubernetes the executor through the app pod's `canyon-identity`, in `/run`'s answer (executor.md, *What the app answered*) — since a visitor's request would meet the sign-in, not the app — and records `{status, said}`, the HTTP status and, when the answer's `Content-Type` is `text/plain` or `application/json`, the first non-empty line of the body, `""` for any other type — a page's first line is its markup, `<!DOCTYPE html>` on the test cluster (amended 2026-10-05) — and the line masked by the app's runtime values as `stopped`'s `said` is, then each Cc, Cf, Zl and Zp character removed, then cut to 200 characters, by the server when it records it, `""` when there is none; or `{error}` when nothing answered, `error` exactly one of `no answer in 5 s`, `refused` (nothing listens), `unreachable` (no such address, no route) and `not an HTTP answer`; never asked again until the next deploy, never polled, and never a judgement: a `401`, `404` or `302` at `/` can be right, the environment's `state` stays what the executor says, and the one thing it stops is a promotion of that preview row from any session but a browser's (the promotions row, **amended 2026-10-08**); **an answer to say beside readiness** — the screen's headline, `canyon deploy`'s step to production — is one where nothing answered or the status is outside `2xx` and `3xx` but `401` and `404`, the two an app's own sign-in or an API with no page at its root answer (amended 2026-10-05), every surface by this one rule; and `canyon deploy` says *answered <status> <reason phrase> at /*, the reason phrase the standard one for the code and none for a code with none, never the app's own, and for a `401` followed by ` (Canyon's check sends no identity token, so an app that checks it answers 401 here)` (**amended 2026-10-08**, the QA rerun: an app that verified Canyon's identity token read as broken at /), beside it and the screen's environment card *Answered <status> at /*, or *No answer at /: <error>*, with `said` under it; absent on a row deployed before, on a promotion's retry that found the container already up (nothing was started), and on Kubernetes when the executor could not ask (its log says why); `state` ∈ `running \| restarting \| stopped \| unknown`, **on the status read and the fleet row alone** (this read carries none, but on a viewer's reduced row, above), on an environment Canyon runs (`run_by: canyon`) with something deployed — whether its container runs now, asked of the executor on that read (executor.md, `POST /running`: one call for every 400 environments the read asks of, the most one call asks, never one per app, the calls at once under the one 2 s bound and the environments in a fixed order (app, then environment), so a poll reads the same ones unread as the poll before (**amended 2026-11-09**); **amended 2026-11-08**, #2724: the fleet read asks of every app its reader reads, for what needs a person): `restarting` when it has been started again since it was deployed and is up or waiting to start, whether or not it is up at that instant — once is enough, until its next deploy (executor.md, *Restarting*; **amended 2026-10-05**, #1722: an app up 40 s in every 5½ min read as running in those 40 s), `stopped` when it does not run now and is not restarting — it exited past its restarts, or it was stopped — and `unknown` when the executor did not answer in time or could not tell, so a read that could not ask never says `running` (#1241) — and on the status read a `stopped` one says why, `stopped: {exit_code, said}` (its row, #1597), and a `restarting` one says how often, `restarting: {restarts, exit_code?, said?}`, the times it was started again since `deployed_at`, and how it last exited when the executor said so — `exit_code` and `said` together, as `stopped`'s, under the same mask and the same rules (the status row; amended 2026-10-10, #1597); absent on `run_by: org`, which the org's platform runs; the screen and `canyon status` draw only a `running` environment as live, and say *keeps restarting (once since <deployed_at>)* or *(<n> times since <deployed_at>)* on the status read and *keeps restarting* where the row carries no count, *stopped* and *couldn't check whether it runs* for the other three, capitalised in the screen's cells, and `canyon fleet`'s ENV column `<env> restarting`, `<env> stopped` and `<env> couldn't check`; **`seen_at`** (amended 2026-10-23, #2452) is when a pod holding the access token of the environment's newest release reported `deployed` — the one that put it live — last pulled its access list (the access row, *a pod's check-in*): a timestamp, on an environment the org runs (`run_by: org`, its deploy row the org's) with a `url`, absent until a pod of that release has pulled, and never on one Canyon runs, which says `state` — a field of its own, never `state`: it is the pod's sign-in sidecar checking in, not the app answering, and never a gate, since nothing refuses on it; it rides every read that carries `envs` — this read, the list, the fleet row and the status read — at exactly the levels `state` does and with the same reductions (a viewer's `{url, state?, seen_at?}`, a responder's production, a held production reading as nothing deployed to whoever cannot read its hold), and costs no executor call; **every surface reads it by one rule**, against the reader's clock, a `seen_at` ahead of it read as now, with two named constants, *stale after* **5 minutes** and *never after* **10 minutes**: seen within *stale after*, *a pod checked in <age>*, neutral, never green — but while the environment is stopped, its `pending` a `remove` (an undeploy's, a hold's or a delete's: each records one at once), *a pod still checked in <age>*, amber: its pods pull the deny-all, and one still pulling is one the platform has not taken down (amended 2026-10-24, #2450); seen longer ago, *no pod has checked in since <time>*, the day before the time when it is not today, amber; never seen and deployed more than *never after* ago, *reported deployed, but no pod has checked in*, amber; never seen within *never after* of the deploy, nothing more than before, since a pod may still be starting — capitalised in the screen's cells, and under `canyon status --format ai` the same sentence, never the timestamp, its amber ones ending `(your platform's pods: nothing to do in Canyon)`; an environment with no pod — scaled to zero, or run without the sidecar — reads the never line, as it should, and a Canyon outage longer than *stale after* reads every handed-off environment stale until one pull, a minute, after Canyon is back; `url` the host-routed surface, or the address the org reported, `label` the label of the package this environment runs, absent when it has none, so the screen names what is running without going looking (*Naming a version* below), `image_digest` the `sha256:…` half (the full image reference stays server-side: promote ships that exact image, executor invariant 4) — and `prod` adds `promoted_by` · `owners[]` are the app's `email` grants at `owner`, oldest first, each the actor triple `{kind: "person", subject, display}` — `subject` the identity-provider subject the grant holds, `""` for someone who has never signed in, `display` the grant's, else its address; a `group` at `owner` manages access but is not on `owners[]`, which is what `owner.required` and the `unowned` tile count; it is on `owner_groups[]` · every env carries `deployed_by`, the actor of the run that produced what it runs — on `prod` that is the preview deploy whose exact image the promotion shipped (no rebuild, executor invariant 4), which is why `promoted_by`, the person who clicked promote, is a second name and not the same one · both are **recorded when the row is written**: `deployed_by` onto the deploy row from the run's actor, `promoted_by` onto the promotion from the actor who confirmed it — a deploy row written before that column existed answers `null`, and there is no backfill (nobody knows who it was) · `latest: {package, label?, stage, since}` is the newest package and where it has got to: `package` is `null` and `label` absent exactly when `stage` is `created`, `stage` is one of the nine below, `since` is the instant the package entered that stage (the fleet's staleness sort, row 75) · `gate_state` stays the governance fact it has always been · **an app has no stage — it runs environments and it has a latest package** · `latest` is on both reads of this row and on the fleet row — one shape, everywhere a project is listed · **the single read alone adds `resources[]`** (the list and the fleet row do not), at `contributor` and above — technical detail (design/PERMISSIONS.md): the reduced row carries none, so no endpoint reaches a viewer or anyone with no access: the project's resource records, one per `(env, name)` as `{name, type, env, state}` with `state` ∈ `pending \| ready \| failed \| deleting`, **and each resource the org's platform reported provisioning (#2432), as `{name, type, env, state: "provided", endpoint}`** — `type` the reporting release's package's declaration, `endpoint` the report's; where a record of Canyon's (made before the switch to `org`) and a platform's row name the same `(env, name)`, both, the record first: the data was never moved, the platform's starts empty, and a delete destroys both — and every resource the current package declares (its newest, as `packages/current`) that neither names yet, as `{name, type, state: "declared"}` with no `env` · by `name`, then the declaration before `preview` before `prod` · a resource a newer package no longer declares stays listed while a record of it exists: its data is kept until the project is deleted · `[]` when there is neither |
| `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id?confirm=` | — | `204` — **deleting a project destroys its resources and their data immediately** (undeploy keeps them): first, under the ledger lock a run's start takes, the project is marked deleting (`409 run_in_progress` if a run is `pending` or `running`, but a patch run before its `/run`, which the delete ends `failed`/`interrupted` and goes ahead: *Patch runs*); from then no run starts and no promotion lands (`409 {error:"deleting"}`) until the delete finishes, and the mark stays through a `202` or `502`, so the project is frozen until a retry completes. Then, once a recreation of the app's network under way has ended (Egress control: the delete waits on the app's undeploy lock), both envs' containers stop (`POST /stop`) and the deploys rows go (`/status` shows nothing running), every resource record is marked `deleting` (a deploy never ensures one again), its driver deletes each, newest first, and a record goes only once the driver answers gone, with an audit row `delete resource` `detail{name, type, env}` in the same transaction; then the app's images and build caches are removed (`POST /forget`; a failure is the `502 teardown_failed` below, and the same request again resumes); then — under the ledger lock, an environment whose newest project delete's `remove` has not reported `removed`, open or failed, answering `202 {pending[]}` with it in place of finishing, the same request again sending it anew (#2432) — the project's secret rows (both envs) go, and its link to a repository (#1917), so the repository and folder can be linked again, the project leaves the list and the single read, and one audit row is written, in one transaction. Its ledger and runs stay · `confirm` must equal the project id, else **`409 {error:"confirm_required", resources[]}`** listing the records to be destroyed (`{name, type, env, state}`, newest first), then what the org's platform reported provisioning, `state` `provided` and no endpoint (#2432), a record and a provided row of the same name both — the platform deletes those — and nothing changes · a project whose prod is live — deployed, or holding resources or secrets there: `403 {error:"person_required"}` for an agent while `prod.human-approval` is blocking, as undeploy · `202 {pending[]}` while a driver is still deleting (those records stay; the same request again resumes), and — **under hand-off the delete sends a `remove` release carrying `delete: true` to each environment that is a target** (#2432): one the org runs now (a deploy row `run_by: org`), one with a `deploy` release whose package declared resources, one the platform reported provisioning for (`provided` rows), one with a `deploy` release still open (`sent` or `accepted`: the platform may be starting it now, and the delete's remove supersedes it), and one whose newest project delete's `remove` has not reported `removed` — open, `failed`, `undelivered` or `refused`: a cleanup that failed stays a target until a later one is removed — none of it read from `run_by`, so switching it back and forth never moves an environment in or out — undeployed or not, so the platform deletes what it provisioned there; the set is read once, in the transaction that marks the project deleting, the project's row locked, and every check below and every remove the request sends go by it; production, when it is a target, is live for the `person_required` rule above; with a target whose last deploy went through the webhook and no platform webhook saved, or one whose last deploy went through the deploy repo and no deploy repo saved — `409 {error:"repo_required", message:"Save your deploy repo first."}` (#2470) — **`409 {error:"webhook_required", message:"this app's data is on your platform, and no platform webhook is set to tell it to delete it: set one in Settings › App hosting first"}`**, after `confirm` and before the project is marked deleting, so the delete never waits on a remove it cannot send; a project Canyon alone ran sends none; an environment the org runs gets it in place of `POST /stop`; an open `remove` without `delete` is never taken for it — a new one is sent — while an open one with it is answered again, one reported `removed` is done, and one `undelivered` or `refused` is sent again by the same request again; a `deployed` report on any release of a project being deleted or deleted is `409 project_deleted` (the report row), writes nothing, and has Canyon send one more `remove` with `delete: true` for that environment — none while one is open there — so the delete waits on it; only a project delete's removes carry `delete`, never an undeploy's, a hold's or the brake's — while one has not reported `removed`, `pending[]` then listing `{release, env}` rows beside the records; its `removed` takes that environment's provided resources off the project read · `502 {error:"teardown_failed"}` naming the resource a driver could not delete: its record, the secrets and the project stay, and the same request again resumes, never deleting twice · each record is deleted by the driver that made it, by the name the record keeps (`builtin`, or the adapter's URL), never by whichever serves its type today; a record whose driver is no longer configured is that `502`, `"the driver that made <name> is no longer configured: its data is kept"` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project · **`409 held`** (*The hold*) while production is held, read under the ledger lock the mark takes, after the level and the actor: the app is evidence until the hold is lifted · every refusal before the confirmed one reaches neither the executor nor a driver · a wait on the app's undeploy lock — behind an undeploy, a hold, a delete or a recreation of its network (Egress control) — is bounded at 20 s: past it `409 {error:"busy", message}` and nothing is done, so no waiter holds a database connection without end (**amended 2026-10-15**, #2254) |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/owner` | `{display}` | `200 {owners[]}` — **a claim**: the caller becomes an `owner` of the app, an `email` grant of their address and subject under the given display; claiming again updates the display, nothing is removed · anyone may claim an app with no grant at `owner` of any kind — a person's or a group's — **while it holds no secret row, in either environment, nothing is deployed in either, its production is not held (*The hold*), and it is not linked to a repository and holds no package from GitHub** (`source.kind` `repo` or `repo-pr`, unlinked since or not: an app made from a repository is claimable neither before its first package lands nor after an unlink): claiming one that does would hand over live credentials, a running service, or a repository's code and the power to turn its check green by approving its findings (#1917, design/PERMISSIONS.md), so an admin adopts it; on any other app a caller who is neither an owner nor an admin is `403 {error:"owner_required"}` · a claim at an address whose grant is bound to another person (*App teams*) is `409 {error:"grant_bound"}` and changes nothing: an owner or an admin shares it to the claimer, never a silent promotion · audited with the actor and `detail.display` · `400 {error:"invalid_display"}` when `display` is empty or over 128 characters · `404 {error:"not_found"}` |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/name` | `{name}` — the app's new display name, refused as `POST`'s `{name}` is (#1909) | `200 {project}`, the single read as the caller reads it · **only the display name changes** (`projects.name`): the id stays the app's address, its audit subject, `canyon.toml`'s `app`, the CLI's `--app` and every route's `:id`, so no address, session, certificate or checkout moves (#1911); an app made before #1911, whose name is its id, is renamed the same way · an owner's or an admin's, as managing access is: anyone else is `403 {error:"owner_required"}`, naming whom to ask, one `denied` row and nothing changed · one audit row `rename app` `detail{from, to}` on the app, none when the name is already that · `400 {error:"invalid_name"}` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for an unknown or deleted app |
| `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/packages` | tar stream · headers: `Canyon-Base-Package: pkg_x` (optional) · `Canyon-Package-Label: v1.2` (optional: a friendly name for this package, `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$`, unique within the project) · `?force=true` · `?check=true` | `201 {package: {id, label?, tree_hash, commit, source: {kind, ref, actor}}}` · `400 {error:"invalid_label"}` · `409 {error:"label_taken", package}` when the label names another package of the project (the same tree again keeps its first label and answers it; a new label on an existing tree is `label_taken` too — labels never move) · **`409 {error:"stale_base", head_package, moved_by, moved_at, moved_from}`** — the two exits are `pull` or `force` · `moved_from` is the head's `source` `{kind, ref}`: a pushed head's `ref` is the commit GitHub delivered, which a copy that already holds it in its git history is built on (cli.md §3.6; #1938) · `moved_by` is the display of whoever's upload made the head current, never an identity provider's subject (amended 2026-10-01, `canyonv2-e69b`) · `source.actor` is the actor's subject · the server applies the snapshot's exclusions to every upload, whatever the CLI sent · a base header on a project with no current package (none, or only a pull request's or a check's) is accepted, the base unread: nothing is current, so nothing is replaced — a copy deployed to a host that has no version of the app, after a reinstall or a move, uploads as a first one (amended 2026-10-05, #1678) · **no base header on a project that has a current package is the same `409 stale_base`, naming the head** (amended 2026-10-04, #1503): a copy that records no base — a fresh clone, since `.canyon/` is never committed — never silently replaces the app's current package; it is checked before the tree is read, so a fresh clone holding exactly the current tree is refused too; the exits are the same, pull or force · only the exact `force=true` is force, and a forced upload's audit row carries `detail.forced: true` (an upload that overrode nothing carries no `forced` key) · a tar ingest refuses (an entry escaping the tree, over 512 MiB of file bytes, over 100,000 entries) is `400 {error:"bad_request"}` · **the server reads the `[resources]` section of the package's root `canyon.toml`**, only lines `name = { type = "<type>" }`, and refuses the upload with `400 {error:"invalid_resources", message}` — nothing is committed and no package is made — when the file is over 64 KiB (`canyon.toml is larger than 64 KiB`), it is not TOML, `resources` is anything but one `[resources]` section of those lines (no `[resources.db]` table, dotted key or `resources = {…}` value), a name does not match `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`, a declaration holds anything but one string `type`, the type is not in the snapshot's `component_menu` (the message names the menu's types), the type is one this host does not provision yet (`GET /component-types`' `status` `planned`: a deploy would fail at its first resource, so it is said at the upload, the message naming the type; **amended 2026-10-04**, #1255), or two declarations' types give the app the same variable (`GET /component-types`' `gives`: two `postgres` both give `DATABASE_URL`; the message names both) · in the same pass and under the same cap it reads the top-level `description`, one line of plain text saying what the app does — the project's `summary` while this package is current — refused `400 {error:"invalid_description", message}` when it is not a string, is over 200 characters, or contains a character that can hide or reorder text (a control or format character, or a line or paragraph separator);
