and the `[roles]` section, the app roles an owner may grant (the Access group), only lines `name = "<one line>"`, refused `400 {error:"invalid_roles", message}` when `roles` is anything but one `[roles]` section of those lines, a name does not match `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`, a role's line is over 120 characters or contains a character that can hide or reorder text (a control or format character, or a line or paragraph separator), or there are more than 16 · a package with no `canyon.toml`, or none of a section, declares nothing · the check runs only when the upload makes a new package: the same tree again answers the existing package · the declarations are part of the package: fixed with it, changed only by a newer one · each `(name, type)` the project had not declared in any package before writes one more audit row, `action` `declare resource`, `detail{name, type, package}` · the same tree uploaded again answers the existing package with its first label and source `kind` and `ref`, and **makes it current again** — its `created_at` and `source.actor` become the upload's, so a version names who last uploaded it and when, and the packages list shows it first and `packages/current` serves it, while its stage stays what its own runs and deploys made it (the same tree, the same answers) — so the base a client records from this answer is the project's head, and a person redeploying their own earlier version is never told it moved under them (amended 2026-10-01, `canyonv2-o49o`); a package only a pull request or a check packaged becomes this upload's and current · **`?check=true` is a check's upload** (`canyon check`, amended 2026-10-03, #1251): its package is reviewed and **never made current by the check**, as a pull request's is never made current by the pull request (`POST /webhooks/github`) — not pulled, not listed, not a run's default, not `latest`, its declarations not the project's and no `declare resource` row, its runs never the project's `gate_state`, `last_run`, receipt, fleet tiles or policy preview, though they take the project's one run slot and are listed with its runs, and it is never named to a deploy (`404`) — stored as `source.kind: "check"`, `source.ref` `""`; it moves no `main`; the base header and `force` are not read, so it is never `stale_base`; a label is `400 {error:"invalid_label"}`, since a check names no version; the `[resources]`, `description` and `[roles]` checks refuse it as they refuse any upload; the same tree as a package the project already holds answers that package and changes nothing of it; its audit row carries `detail.kind: "check"` and is never the `moved_by` of a head; a later upload or push of the same tree makes it current, by the same-tree rule above that a pull request's package follows: a package id is its tree, so there is no second row · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project · **a package id is unique within its project, not globally** (the same tree in two projects is two packages with one id), so every route and every client that names a package names its project too · two uploads racing the same base both succeed: the 409 is advice to pull, not a lock |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/packages/current` | — | tar stream + `Canyon-Package: pkg_x` header (this is `canyon pull`) — *current* is the project's newest package row that is not a pull request's or a check's · `404 {error:"not_found"}` when there is none |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/packages?cursor=&limit=` | — | `{packages[], next}` — **one page of the project's packages, newest first** by `created_at` then `id`, a pull request's or a check's never listed, so the first row of the first page is *current* (the row above): each is the upload's `package` (the `POST` above) plus when it landed — `{id, label?, tree_hash, commit, source{kind, ref, actor}, created_at}`, `source.actor` the uploader by display as the trail names them: the newest upload or push of the tree, else the stored source, so a row reads `CLI upload · Dana's agent` and never an identity provider's subject (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1523) · **a package row does not say where it runs**: which environment runs a package is `envs.preview.package` / `envs.prod.package` on the project (the project read) — one source, so a redeploy or a promotion changes the project and never rewrites a package · **every timestamp this seam carries, answered or accepted, is RFC 3339 at whole seconds in UTC with a `Z`** — `2026-09-02T09:31:12Z`: answers are truncated to the second (the deploy read's precedent), and an accepted one (`expires_at`, `since`) is stored truncated to the second in UTC, so what comes back is what went in · `cursor` is opaque and server-issued — a client passes back exactly what it was handed and parses nothing; inside, it is the last row's (`created_at`, `id`), and **a page is the next `limit` rows after that key in the sort order**, so across a project that does not change no row repeats and none vanishes; **a page is not a snapshot**: a project that gains a package between two pages may repeat or skip one row at the boundary, and one that uploads a tree again moves its row to the top, which a reader past that row does not see again until the first page; the reader tolerates both · `limit` defaults to 50; a non-integer or a value below 1 is `400 {error:"invalid_limit"}`, above 200 it is clamped to 200; a cursor the server did not issue is `400 {error:"invalid_cursor"}` · `next` is the cursor for the page after this one, `null` on the last page · **the same three rules page every list on the app page** — the gate runs, the audit trail — and the fleet read: one way to page, not four; the two timeline reads keep `after`, which is a poller keeping its place, not a page · a project with no package is an empty page, not a 404 · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/packages/:pkg` | — | `200 {package}` — **one package of the project by its id, whoever made it**: an upload, a push, a pull request's or a check's, so the two the list leaves out are answered too — as the list's row below, `{id, label?, tree_hash, commit, source{kind, ref, actor}, created_at}`, `source.kind` ∈ `upload \| repo \| repo-pr \| check` (`app-page.schema.json`'s `package_read`) · **guard (b)'s read** (design/OPERATIONS.md §2.3, cli.md §6.6): a package id is its tree, so a host this tree was never sent to has no such id, and **a package this project never had — another project's, a label, or none — is `404 {error:"unknown_package"}`**, its own answer and apart from the project's `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project, so a host older than this route, whose catch-all is `not_found`, never reads as a checkout sent to another Canyon · **a package's row outlives its tree**: retention (ARCHITECTURE.md) drops the tree and keeps the row, so a package this host made answers `200` however old, and only one it never made is `unknown_package` · `:pkg` is an id, never a label · at minimum `contributor`; below it `403 {error:"team_required"}` (*App teams*) · a read: no audit row |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/packages/:pkg/reports` | a SARIF 2.1.0 log, one run, at most 10 MiB (`:pkg` an id or a label, as on gate runs; any package of the project, a pull request's too) | `200 {tool, category, results, dropped, first_dropped?}` — **attaches one external tool's report to the package** (finding.md, *In: external SARIF*): `tool` the normalised `tool.driver.name`, `category` the normalised `automationDetails.id` (`""` when absent), `results` the count kept, `dropped` the count finding.md drops at attach, `first_dropped` naming the first of them as the `400`'s message does, `result <index>: <what is wrong>` (`result 3: kind is pass`), present only when `dropped` > 0 · one report per `(package, tool, category)`: a second PUT replaces the first; a run already started keeps the one it included · the raw body is never stored: the report's `sha256:<64 hex>` digest, the tool, its category, its `driver.version`, the attaching actor's display, `dropped`, `first_dropped` and the normalised results are · **every PUT writes one audit row**, `action` `attach report`, `detail{package, tool, category, digest, replaced}` — `replaced` the digest it replaced, `null` when none — in the transaction that stores it · the body is read to 10 MiB before decoding: over it **`413 {error:"too_large"}`** · **`400 {error:"invalid_report", message}`** for any refusal finding.md lists, `message` naming the first, `result <index>: <what is wrong>` when a result is · **`400 {error:"unknown_tool", message}`** when the snapshot carries no `sarif.<tool>`, `message` exactly `This report is from "<tool>", which isn't a tool your admin has added to Canyon. Ask an admin to add it in Guardrails.` · **`400 {error:"too_many_results", message}`** over 1,000 results, `message` exactly `<tool> reported <n> results; Canyon takes 1,000 per tool. Narrow the scan to this app's source.`, `<n>` with thousands commas · nothing is stored on any refusal · every PUT — refused or stored — counts toward the gate-run rate limit's per-actor count, checked before the body is read, and is refused past it as a run start is (counting attempts, not only stored reports, keeps the unredacted-literal check from being an unlimited oracle on a package's code) (`429 {error:"rate_limited", message, retry_after_s}`) · Canyon's own scans attach through the same core without this route, as actor Canyon, their stored row adding the image digest and `db_built_at`, count toward no rate limit and write no audit row (finding.md, *Canyon-run tools*) · `409 {error:"deleting"}` as on gate runs · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project and on a package that is not this project's |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/images/:digest/reports` | a SARIF 2.1.0 log, one run, at most 10 MiB (`:digest` an image's `sha256:` and 64 hex) | `200 {tool, category, results, dropped, first_dropped?}` — **attaches one imported tool's report of an image of the project** (design/PATCHING.md §3.4; finding.md, *An imported image result*; added 2026-11-03, #2077): the image one the project runs (a deploy row's), keeps (its history's) or Canyon scanned for it, else **`404 {error:"not_found", message:"This app doesn't run or keep that image."}`** · contributor and above, as the packages row: a read-only member and one with no access `403 team_required` (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Platform patches*) · its tool, category, limits, rate limit, `413`, `unknown_tool`, `too_many_results` and `409 deleting` are the packages row's · a result of kind `pass`, `informational` or `notApplicable` is dropped and counted; any other names its package under `properties.canyon.package`, else the report is refused whole, **`400 {error:"invalid_report", message}`**, `message` `result <index>: properties.canyon.package <what is wrong>` — a vulnerability Canyon cannot read is never left out · one report per `(project, image, tool, category)`: a second PUT replaces the first · the raw body is never stored: its digest, the tool, its category, its `driver.version`, who attached it and when, and each kept result's id, severity and package — names, versions and ids, never the report's prose · one audit row, `action` `attach image report`, `detail{image, tool, category, digest, replaced}` · `platform.known-vuln` reads it, beside Canyon's own matches and never in their place, only while its `settings.source` names the tool (the policies row; *The promote receipt*) |

### Naming a version

**A version is named by its `label` when it has one, else by its `id` without the `pkg_` prefix.**
One rule, every read: `latest.label` on the project row and the fleet row, `label?` on each
`envs.*` object, on every package row, and on every gate-run row of the runs list. The single
run read (`GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id`) names a package and no version: no screen names a version
from it. The label is optional because a
package may not have one; the id is always there, and `pkg_` is the store's prefix, not a name a
person says. So a read that names a package carries its label beside it, and a screen never has
to guess or go looking: it reads the two fields it was handed. A server sentence that names a
version — the receipt's `why`, below — writes that name and never a `pkg_` id.

### The package's stage

The lifecycle belongs to a **package**, not to the app. `latest.stage` is where the newest package
has got to; every stage is entered by an event, and nothing puts an app in one.

| stage | entered when |
|---|---|
| `created` | the project exists and has no package — nothing to review, nothing to run |
| `awaiting_review` | a package landed — an upload or a repo push — and no run has looked at it; `canyon deploy` uploads and runs in one command, so this is where imported code sits |
| `reviewing` | a gate run of that package is under way |
| `blocked` | the verdict stopped it, nothing was built, and some blocking finding carries no unexpired approval — with every one approved the run still ends `blocked` but the package is `approved`, and a deploy moves it on to `building` and `preview`, which outrank both |
| `approved` | the verdict stopped it and every blocking finding carries an unexpired approval: nothing of the gate is in the way of its deploy, and it reads approved, never blocked and never passed. An approval that expires puts it back in `blocked` at the next read: the stage is derived, never stored (amended 2026-10-01, `canyonv2-5r11.10`, decision D1) |
| `passed` | the gate passed on a check-only run; nothing was deployed |
| `building` | a deploy run cleared the gate — or had every blocking finding approved — and the build started |
| `preview` | the container runs in preview |
| `production` | the package was promoted |
| `failed` | the run ended `failed` with any `failure_reason` — `interrupted`, `error`, `model`, `build`, `detect`, `start`, `no_room`, `scan_no_room` or `image_gone`; the reason rides on `last_run`, never on the stage, and a new run retries it |

A new upload on any of them puts the **newer** package in `awaiting_review` while the environments
keep running what they run: the newer package moves through the stages beside production, not through it.

The stage is the **furthest point the newest package reached**, and an environment never walks back: re-running
the gate on a package already in preview leaves it in `preview`, and a `pending` run counts as `reviewing`. Among
verdicts the newest run of the newest package wins: a package that passed and then blocks on a re-run reads
`blocked` — a block is never hidden behind an older pass.

`since` is the instant the package entered the stage, and every stage reads it off the row that put it there:
`created` the project's `created_at` · `awaiting_review` the package's `created_at` · `reviewing` the run's
`created_at` · `blocked`, `passed` and `failed` the run's `ended_at` · `approved` the later of the run's
`ended_at` and the newest `approved_at` among the approvals covering its blocking findings — the instant the last
one was approved, or the run's end when every one was approved before it · `building` the run's first `build.log`
row's `ts` · `preview` and `production` that environment's `deployed_at` — so on those two the row's `since`
and its `envs.<env>.deployed_at` are the same instant, and a fleet row that disagrees with itself is a bug — but while preview runs a pull request's version (#1947), `latest` is unchanged, and that equality does not hold for `preview`.

## Gate

| Route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/gate-runs` | `{package?, rollback_to?, reason?}` (default: current; a package id `pkg_x` or a label — wherever a package is named, a label names it too, and an id wins over a label of the same text; any package of the project, a check's or a pull request's too: a review makes no package current) · **`rollback_to`** (**amended 2026-10-11**, #2055; design/OPERATIONS.md §1): a digest `sha256:…` production ran for that package — one of its rows in the status read's `earlier[]` — asks **a rollback review**: the package reviewed under today's policy, preview untouched · `reason`, beside `rollback_to` alone, an incident or change reference, plain text, 1 to 1024 characters with no control, format, line- or paragraph-separator character (Unicode Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp), which the `deploy prod` row records (the promotions row) | `202 {run: {id, state:"pending"}}` — a run of this project still `pending` is superseded by this one (it ends `failed`/`interrupted`; nothing queues behind a run, `queue_position` is unused in v1) — except a promote run, which is never superseded (an undeploy of the environment a run writes ends it as *The brake* says, a promote run too) · **Canyon's patch never holds this start back** (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-18, #2086): a patch run before its `/run` is ended `failed`/`interrupted` as a pending run is, and any run in its patch watch — a patch run, or a promote run shipping a patch — has its watch ended clean by a start that deploys (a deploy, a rollback's review), and this run starts; during the `/run` of either, and a check's start during the watch (*Patch runs*), the start is the `409 run_in_progress` below · **`409 {error:"run_in_progress", run, watch?}`** while a run of this project is `running`, or a promote run is `pending`; `run` is `{id, state}`, the 202's shape; `watch` is `{until}`, exactly while that run is a patch run whose watch runs — its `watch_until`, RFC 3339 UTC to the second — so a caller knows the run is Canyon's patch watch, when it ends, and that a deploy ends it now (amended 2026-10-29, #2568) · **`429 {error:"rate_limited", message, retry_after_s}`** past the per-project/actor rate limit (limit in the snapshot) — per project and per actor together, never a per-project half alone; the actor is the session's person, so the limit is live from M2b; every gate run started in the last hour counts, a superseded one too, and toward the actor's count every report the actor attached (the reports row), and a promotion is neither counted nor limited; `retry_after_s` is until the oldest counted run is an hour old · **`409 {error:"deleting"}`** while the project is being deleted (a confirmed `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id` marked it, under the lock this start takes) · **`409 {error:"undeploying"}`** while an undeploy of the project is in progress: nothing started, and the start never waits on it · **a run includes the reports attached to its package before it started**: their digests are journaled in the run's input, so a replayed step reads the same reports, and a report stays stored while any run names it · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project, on a package that is not this project's, and on a project with no package yet (as `packages/current`) · **a rollback review** (`rollback_to`): `409 {error:"not_earlier", message}` when the digest is not one of that package's rows in the status read's `earlier[]` — read under the start's lock, so it is the list the status read answers then — and nothing starts; `400 {error:"invalid_reason"}` for a `reason` outside its rule, or one without `rollback_to`; the deploys row takes neither field (`400 {error:"bad_request"}`) · **its limits are its own** (**amended 2026-10-11**): rollback reviews count toward the per-project and per-actor limits of rollback reviews alone — the same numbers, counted over rollback reviews — and never toward an ordinary review's, so a busy hour of reviews never holds back a rollback during an incident, and a loop of rollback reviews is `429` as any; the 202's audit row's `detail` is `{package, rollback_to, reason?}`; it records what production runs as it starts, the version the rollback would replace; in every other way it is a gate run: one at a time (`409 run_in_progress`), superseded and cancelled as any, the review cache (D9) as any · it ends as a review ends; ended `clean`, or `blocked` with every blocking finding covered by an unexpired approval, it records **`ships`**, the image production ran under that digest when Canyon kept it (`kept`, the status row; design/OPERATIONS.md §1.5) — the image a promotion of the run then ships (the promotions row) · **a rollback never builds**: when that image is not kept, the run records none and ends `failed`/`image_gone`, its last `build.log` line `<version>'s image is no longer on this Canyon, so it can't be put back as it ran. Deploy <version> to preview and promote it if you want it rebuilt.`, `<version>` named as *Naming a version* says · **`Canyon-Harness`** (**added 2026-11-15**, #2953): `claude_code` or `codex`, the coding agent the CLI runs under by its harness markers, kept on the run for the usage report's `agents`; any other value, or none, keeps nothing and refuses nothing |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/gate-runs?cursor=&limit=` | — | `{runs[], next}` — **one page of the project's runs, newest first** by `started_at` then `id`: each is `{id, package, label?, state, failure_reason?, failed_in?, blocking, warnings, started_by, started_at, ended_at, approved?, pull_request?}` — `pull_request` `{number, branch}` on a run of a pull request's package (#1947) · `kind` `patch` on a patch run (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-18, #2086), absent on every other, and `not_platform_only` beside `failure_reason` `not_platform_only` alone (events.md), as `not_same_inputs` beside `not_same_inputs` (amended 2026-11-02, #2117) · `approved` `true` on a `blocked` run whose every blocking finding an unexpired approval covers now, the rule a deploy goes on by, and absent on any other run (#1955): a screen offers what the run would let through, never inferring it from the counts — the single read below without its `findings[]`, `builds` and `errored` (a list carries the counts; the findings and what the host builds are one run's read), plus who and when · `label` is the label of the package the run reviewed, absent when it has none, so the runs list names a version the way the versions list does (*Naming a version*) · `started_by` is the actor triple of the request that started the run — the same actor a deploy row's `deployed_by` carries (the project read) — **recorded onto the run row when it is written** — a column on `runs` the gate-runs list PR adds; a run written before that column existed answers `null`, and there is no backfill · `started_at` is when the row was written; `ended_at` is `null` until the run reaches a terminal state · `blocking` and `warnings` are `0` before the verdict and **the verdict's counts after it, never net of approvals** — entries, not results (finding.md, *One problem, one entry*; amended 2026-10-11, #2013) — a run that ended with one blocking finding says so for good; only the receipt nets unexpired approvals, which is how a run row can read "1 blocking" beside a promote that goes ahead · `failure_reason` present only on `failed` · `check: true` when the run reviewed a check's package (`POST /api/v1/projects/:id/packages?check=true`), absent otherwise, read from the package's kind when the list is read: a run whose tree a later upload or push made current loses it, since that tree is then the app's code — so the app page of an app with no current package can show its newest check's review, read-only, as a check (amended 2026-10-04, #1251) · cursor and limit exactly as on the packages list: opaque cursor over (`started_at`, `id`), 50 by default, clamped at 200, `400 {error:"invalid_limit" \| "invalid_cursor"}` · a project with no run is an empty page · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project |
| `GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id` | — | `{run: {id, package, state, failure_reason?, failed_in?, not_platform_only?, not_same_inputs?, kind?, builds?, errored?, fix?, not_checked?, approvable?, link_github?, blocking, warnings, progress[], findings[]}}` — `kind`, `not_platform_only` and `not_same_inputs` as on the list (amended 2026-10-18, #2086; 2026-11-02, #2117) · `approvable` `true` when the caller may approve the run's blocking findings by who they are — an admin, or a responder, who is not the run's author (the approve rows; amended 2026-10-12, #2204) — and on a host with sign-in off, absent otherwise, so a screen offers *Approve* only where the route would take it, the session aside · `link_github` `true` in its place when the one thing missing is a linked GitHub account (`github_link_required`, the approve rows; #2342), absent otherwise, so the screen says so and links to linking · `progress` is the run's progress (events.md, `progress`; amended 2026-10-04, #1652): for each `(step, tool)` its newest row's `{step, tool?, status, position?, duration_ms?}`, in the order each first appeared, `[]` before any — so a screen draws where a run stands without replaying its timeline, and the timeline's `progress` rows move it on · `failed_in` present only on a run that ended `failed` with `error`: the step Canyon failed in (*A failure reason in words*; amended 2026-10-04, #1645) · `builds` present only on a run that ended `failed` with `detect`: the executor's `builds` for it (executor.md, `/build`), the buildpacks this host's builder builds, each by its builder's name for it, else its id (amended 2026-10-04, #1561), `[]` when it names none, so a screen says what the host builds without reading the detector's lines; `errored: true` beside it when the executor's answer carried it (a buildpack errored while detecting, so a malformed manifest is possible), absent otherwise · `fix` present only on a run that ended `failed` with `detect` or `start` when its facts name the one file the package is missing (*What a starter is missing*, below): `{file, why, examples[]}`, each example `{for, text}`, absent otherwise (amended 2026-10-04, #1654) · `not_checked: true` when the verdict row's log carries a `not checked` note (finding.md, *What nothing read is named*), absent otherwise, so a screen never reads a clean run with one as clean · `findings[]` is the verdict row's SARIF log flattened to its `runs[].results[]` in log order (a Canyon finding is a SARIF result, seam 1), each carrying `tool`, the `tool.driver.name` of the run it is in (`canyon-review`, `sarif/opengrep`, …), so a card names what found it (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1519); before the verdict `0, 0, []`; a finding an unexpired approval covers — the project's, on its `ruleId` and full `canyon/v1` fingerprint, the key the receipt nets on; every read that nets or shows an approval matches it this way — carries it as `approval: {reason, expires_at, approved_by}`, so a screen never shows an approved finding as open; any other finding carries nothing more, and an expired approval is not on the finding (it resurfaces, as in the receipt) · a finding that blocks before production — under a rule the app's effective snapshot places `blocking` there now, its `ruleId` the rule's id or under it, at a severity that rule blocks, the receipt's own per-finding answer (*The promote receipt*) — carries `blocks_production: true`, and any other carries nothing more, so a screen marks it and offers its approval without reading the rules itself (amended 2026-10-12, #2206) · every finding carries `entry`, the index in `findings[]` of the first finding of its entry (finding.md, *One problem, one entry*; amended 2026-10-11, #2013) — its own index when it is the first or alone — so a screen and the CLI join and count by it, an entry open while any of its findings carries no `approval` · a finding of one dependency carries `dependency: {name, version, vulnerability?, fixed?, link?, fixed_in?}`, its name and installed version as its tool wrote them, and the vulnerability, its fixed versions, its advisory link and the version that fixes every finding of its group, each when its tool names it (finding.md, *A dependency's name and version*; amended 2026-10-08, #1875), and any other finding carries none: a dependency is never read off a file, a rule id or a lockfile (amended 2026-10-08, #1848) · `blocking` and `warnings` here, alone among the reads, count `findings[]` — results, never net of approvals — so a client checks them against the list it renders, as a CLI released before entries does and every CLI since may: one read is never two counts that disagree with its own list, and the run's entries are its findings' `entry` (amended 2026-10-11, #2013); `failure_reason` present only on `failed` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` |
| `POST /api/v1/gate-runs/:id/cancel` | — | **cancel a run**: a `pending` run ends at once `failed`/`interrupted`, `200 {run: {id, state:"failed"}}` · a `running` run is asked to end itself `failed`/`interrupted` before its next step — never mid-step, so the project stays busy until the step in flight ends (a wait on the platform, within one ask) — `202 {run: {id, state:"running"}}`, and asking again answers the same and writes nothing · what earlier steps made (a database) stays, and the next deploy or promotion keeps it · **`409 {error:"too_late"}`** once the run's deploy step has begun — so the one build after a reused image `/run` answers missing (the deploys row) is never cancelled (for a promote run, production is switching) · `409 {error:"ended"}` on a run that has ended · an undeploy of the environment a run writes ends it or asks it to end the same way, and past its deploy step, where this route answers `too_late`, leaves a stop pending on it (*The brake*) · a promote run, while `prod.human-approval` is blocking, only a person — `403 {error:"person_required"}`, as undeploy · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown run · one audit row, `run` the run, `detail{package, kind, started_by}` (`kind` `gate`, `promote` or `patch`, `started_by` the run's actor or `null`); a refusal writes none |
| `GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id/sarif` | — | `200` `application/sarif+json` — **the run's verdict SARIF log as stored** (seam 1), for any CI to upload; a finding an unexpired approval covers at read time — the one the run read above attaches, found the same way — carries `suppressions: [{kind: "external", justification: <the approval's reason>}]`, **the reason only, never who approved**; the log still validates against SARIF 2.1.0 and the profile · a read: no audit row, the run read's session · **`409 {error:"no_verdict"}`** on a run with no verdict row (`pending`, `running`, or `failed` before the gate) · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown run |
| `GET /api/v1/gate-runs/:id/timeline?after=&limit=` | — | `{rows[], next}` — the run's timeline rows, polled (seam 2) · a deploy run's access-request rows (`build.log` under the dedupe `access:<email>`) are served only to a caller who owns the app or is an admin: for any other caller, or one whose level on the app cannot be read, they are left out, their ids never reissued (events.md, `build.log`; amended 2026-10-26, #2445) · `after` and `limit` are non-negative integers, else `400 {error:"invalid_after"}` / `{error:"invalid_limit"}`; `limit` defaults to 100, `limit=0` means the default, and it is clamped to 200 · an empty page answers `next` as the cursor sent, so a poller keeps its place · a run of a soft-deleted project stays readable (row 44) · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown run |
| `POST /api/v1/gate-runs/:run/findings/:fingerprint/approve` | `{reason, expires_at}` — both required: `reason` plain text, never markup, 1 to 1024 characters; `expires_at` RFC 3339, after now and **at most 90 days after now** | `200 {approval: {rule, fingerprint, reason, expires_at, approved_by}}` — keyed `(project, rule, fingerprint)` as before, an approvals table under that key the promote PR adds (nothing stores one today): the run names the project and its verdict names the rule the fingerprint belongs to; `fingerprint` is the finding's **full** `canyon/v1` (finding.md), never the 12-character prefix a screen shows, so the approval follows the finding across runs and uploads until `expires_at`, when it resurfaces in the receipt · `approved_by` is the request's actor, and **only a person approves through this route**: an `agent` or `canyon` actor is `403 {error:"person_required"}` · **a blocking finding** (amended 2026-10-12, #2204, the owner; design/PERMISSIONS.md) — `canyon.blocks` true in the verdict, or `blocks_production` on the run read — is approved only by an admin or a responder — on any app, a responder with no grant on it included (*App teams*) — never by the run's author, and only from a browser session, so neither the agent a gate stopped nor the person it works for approves their own way past it: the author is the run's `started_by` when it is a person, the person whose session started the run, an agent's run under it included, and for a run a GitHub push started every person who linked the account that pushed (`GET /api/v1/github/link`, #2228); a run no person's session started and no linked account pushed has none; judged once the finding is found (`404` first) and before anything is written, in this order, each refusal one `denied` audit row and nothing approved — `403 {error:"approver_required", message:"only an admin or a security responder can approve a blocking finding: ask <admin>"}` to anyone else, `<admin>` as `admin_required`'s; `403 {error:"author_cannot_approve", message:"you started this review, so you can't approve its blocking findings: ask another admin or a security responder"}` when the caller is an author, and `403 {error:"author_cannot_approve", message:"Canyon didn't record who started this review, so nobody approves its blocking findings: deploy it again"}` for a run that recorded no `started_by`, fail closed; `403 {error:"author_cannot_approve", message:"Canyon started this review of its fix and didn't record who wrote the code it fixes, so nobody approves its blocking findings: deploy it again"}` for a patch run whose author — the author of the run whose image it rebases (*Patch runs*) — was not recorded, that run having recorded none, fail closed; `403 {error:"author_cannot_approve", message:"you shipped the code Canyon's fix is for, so you can't approve its blocking findings: ask another admin or a security responder"}` when the caller is a patch run's author (amended 2026-10-19, #2098; reworded 2026-11-01, #2079: it said `platform patch rebases`); `403 {error:"github_link_required", message:"<org> asks whoever approves a push's blocking findings to link their GitHub account first: link it, then approve"}` on a run a GitHub push started while `push_approvers_linked` is on (the preferences row; #2342), to a caller who linked no GitHub account; `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"approve it in Canyon's dashboard"}` to a session that is not a browser's; with sign-in off none applies · an advisory finding is approved at contributor, as before · **an approval covers a blocking finding only when it was filed under this rule** — by an admin or a responder who did not start the run, from a browser session, or on a host with sign-in off — wherever an approval is read: the run read and its SARIF, the receipt before preview and before production, the runs list's and the fleet's `approved`, the next deploy and the GitHub check, one predicate for all of them; an approval filed before this rule (#2204), or filed by anyone else of a finding then advisory, covers that finding in none of them while it blocks, fail closed — the finding reads unapproved until an admin or a responder approves it — and covers it while it is advisory · nor does one ever cover a finding of a run whose author filed it, whichever run it was filed on — another run's with the same fingerprint included — nor any finding of a run that recorded no author, nor, while `push_approvers_linked` is on, a push run's finding by an approver who linked no GitHub account, wherever it was filed (fail closed: turning the setting on stops such an approval covering at once): one predicate, `runs.CoversBlocking`, read by every reader above against the gated run's authors · a finding whose verdict does not say whether it blocks reads as blocking · with sign-in on, an approval the anonymous person filed with it off covers nothing: the server voids them as it starts, and logs how many unexpired approvals cover no blocking finding · an approval filed outside the rule never replaces one filed under it: the route answers the approval that stands, writes nothing and no audit row · a responder with no grant on the app approves any finding — an advisory one's too — from a browser alone (`dashboard_required`) · a deploy or a promotion judges its review again in its deploy step, right before its container starts (`/run`) or each attempt hands its release to the org's platform — plain code inside that step, never a journaled answer, so every retry, replay or recovery that has not started it yet judges it again: a deploy run its own verdict, ended `clean` or `blocked` with every blocking finding covered; a promotion the review its receipt accepted it under, recorded on the run as it starts (`receipt_run`, in the transaction that evaluated the receipt), judged as the receipt judges it — `prod.clean-gate`, while it blocks, over every finding blocking before preview, and every rule placed blocking before production over its own, each covered now; at this judgement alone an approval counts only while it has 10 minutes yet to stand (`runs.ShipMargin`), so none expires between it and the container's start, while every read — the run read, the receipt, the fleet — shows it until it expires; fail closed — no review recorded, no verdict, a receipt that cannot be read or any error clears nothing — and one not cleared ends the run `failed`/`interrupted`, nothing started, production's line `deploying to production: failed: its review no longer clears it, an approval expired or withdrawn; the version running there is unchanged` (a lift's start again promotes no review and is not judged again, *The hold*) · **while the org's `prod_approval` is on** (the preferences row; amended 2026-10-10, #1824, the owner) a session that is not a browser sign-in's — the CLI's — is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message}` before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row and nothing approved: the finding is fixed and deployed again, or a person approves it in the dashboard; `message` is `While production is approved in Canyon's dashboard, a finding is approved there too. Fix the code and deploy again, or ask a person to approve it in the dashboard: <host>/#/apps/<app>?run=<run>`, `<host>` the host's public URL — an approved finding lets preview deploy as well, so the setting decides this too; off, or with sign-in off, nothing changes · the override is never silent (D6): one audit row, its actor the approver, `run` and `detail{rule, fingerprint, reason, expires_at, author}`, `author` the run's author — its `started_by`, or of a patch run the author of the run whose image it rebases (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-20, #2098) — `null` when it recorded none (#2204) · `400 {error:"invalid_reason" \| "invalid_expires_at" \| "invalid_body"}` — `invalid_expires_at` for a timestamp that is not RFC 3339, not after now, or past the 90 days · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown run, or a fingerprint that is not in its verdict |
| `POST /api/v1/gate-runs/:run/findings/approve` | `{fingerprints, reason, expires_at}` — `fingerprints` 1 to 200 distinct full `canyon/v1` fingerprints of the run's verdict, each naming one result of it (finding.md: the occurrence index runs across the whole log); `reason` and `expires_at` as on the single approve | **one dependency's findings approved together** (amended 2026-10-08, #1848: lodash 4.17.4's five CVEs took five reasons): `200 {approvals[]}` — each finding approved exactly as the single approve above approves it, keyed `(project, rule, fingerprint)`, one row per finding, all in one transaction, so a verdict, a fingerprint and an expiry read every one of them as they read a single approval; `approvals` is each one's `{rule, fingerprint, reason, expires_at, approved_by}` in the body's order · **the findings are one tool's and one dependency's**: each carries the run read's `dependency`, every one the same `{name, version}` and the same `tool`, else **`400 {error:"not_one_dependency"}`** and nothing is approved — the server reads both off the verdict, never off the request; the file is not part of the key, so one dependency in two lockfiles is one group · every fingerprint is found (else `404`) before the findings are compared (else `not_one_dependency`) · **a package only the built app holds** (amended 2026-11-02, #2610): a fingerprint the run's verdict does not hold is found among the `fingerprints` of `deps.known-vuln`'s `built_app` in the receipt read for the run (*The promote receipt*), approved or not — while the rule is placed before production, and only in a built app the row checked, else `404` — its rule `deps.known-vuln`, its tool `canyon/promote-gate`, its dependency the package's `{name, version}`, and it blocks while the rule is placed blocking before production, so its refusals are a blocking finding's · the single approve's rules: only a person, `403 {error:"person_required"}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor; `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message}` to the CLI while `prod_approval` is on, as there (#1824); contributor, or a responder with no grant, as the single approve (*App teams*); and when any of the findings blocks, its refusals for a blocking finding — `approver_required`, `author_cannot_approve`, `dashboard_required` — in its order, once `not_one_dependency` is judged (#2204) · the override is never silent (D6): **one** audit row, its actor the approver, `run` and `detail{tool, dependency: {name, version}, findings: [{rule, fingerprint}], reason, expires_at, author}`, the findings in the body's order, `author` as the single approve's · `400 {error:"invalid_reason" \| "invalid_expires_at" \| "invalid_body"}` as the single approve, `invalid_body` too for `fingerprints` that is not 1 to 200 distinct `f_` + 64-hex strings · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown run, or when any fingerprint is not in its verdict: nothing is approved |

### What a starter is missing

A stock starter can lack the one file that tells the buildpacks how to serve it. The run read
names that file when the run's own facts show it — the failure reason, the package's root file
names (its commit's top-level entries) and the buildpacks of the group that passed detection
(executor.md, *The buildpacks that ran*) — and never from the app's lines or the code's content.
Exactly three cases carry `fix`, each with these words; any other failed run carries none:

| Facts | `file` | `why` | `examples` (`for` → `text`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `detect`; the root holds `index.html` and no `project.toml` | `project.toml` | `No buildpack recognised the package: a static site needs a project.toml at its root that has nginx serve its files.` | `a static site served from its root` → the static block below with `BP_WEB_SERVER_ROOT` `.` |
| `start`; the buildpacks include `paketo-buildpacks/node-engine` and none of `paketo-buildpacks/npm-start`, `paketo-buildpacks/node-start`, `paketo-buildpacks/yarn-start` or `paketo-buildpacks/procfile` | `project.toml` | `The build gave the app no start command, so it could not start: a single-page app needs a project.toml at its root that has nginx serve what its build writes.` | `a single-page app whose build writes dist, as Vite's does` → the static block with `BP_WEB_SERVER_ROOT` `dist` and `BP_WEB_SERVER_ENABLE_PUSH_STATE` `true` |
| `start`; the buildpacks include `paketo-buildpacks/python-start` and not `paketo-buildpacks/procfile` | `Procfile` | `The build gave the app no start command, so Python started with nothing to run and exited: a Python web app needs a Procfile at its root naming the command that starts it on $PORT.` | `FastAPI, with uvicorn in requirements.txt (fastapi[standard] brings it)` → `web: uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port $PORT`; `Flask, with gunicorn in requirements.txt` → `web: gunicorn app:app --bind 0.0.0.0:$PORT` |

The static block is the agent skill's (`cli/skill/canyon.md`), each line ending in a newline:

```toml
[_]
schema-version = "0.2"

[[io.buildpacks.build.env]]
name = "BP_WEB_SERVER"
value = "nginx"

[[io.buildpacks.build.env]]
name = "BP_WEB_SERVER_ROOT"
value = "."
```

and for a single-page app the same with `value = "dist"`, then one more entry, `BP_WEB_SERVER_ENABLE_PUSH_STATE`
with value `"true"`. Each `text` is what the file holds, whole, so a screen shows it as it is and an
agent writes it as it is. Each case was reproduced on stock starters — a plain `index.html`, `npm
create vite` with React, the FastAPI docs' first app and the Flask quickstart — and each starter ran
with its file added (2026-10-04, #1654). FastAPI's line is uvicorn's, which runs both an app on
`fastapi[standard]` and one on plain `fastapi` with `uvicorn`, where `fastapi run` needs the first
(amended 2026-10-05, #1675).

## Deploy & promote

| Route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/deploys` | `{package?}` (default: current; a previous package here — by id or by label — redeploys it; a check's is `404`, as a package that is not this project's; **a pull request's goes to preview when a person asks** (#1947): from a browser session only — any other caller's is `404`, as before — and only while the newest run of that package ended `clean`, or `blocked` with every blocking finding covered by an unexpired approval — one still `pending` or `running` has not — else `409 {error:"review_not_passed", message:"Canyon's review of this pull request hasn't passed"}`; with sign-in off, from any caller, as `promote_anyway`; in this order: an unknown package `404`, a pull request's from any session but a browser's `404`, `review_not_passed`, then a gate run's 409s and 429; it replaces what preview runs; the deploy is a run of that package, so a pull request's run, never the project's `last_run` or `gate_state`, and `envs.preview.gate_run` names it; the fleet counts it as any deploy to preview) | `202 {run}` — a deploy IS a gate run that continues into build, then run, on clean — or on `blocked` when every blocking finding is covered by an unexpired approval: its verdict row lists those approvals and the run still ends `blocked` — the same run, the same row, the same 409/404/429 as a gate run, counted in the same limit · on `blocked` with any blocking finding unapproved the run ends blocked with no build and no deploy · **a package already built is not built again** (#1244): the run reuses an image, calling no `/build` and handing `/run` that exact image, when one of this project's candidates for the same package carries the build digest preview's build values give that candidate's own image — the candidates are the deploy rows Canyon runs (`run_by: canyon`, `preview` or `prod`) and every build a run of the project made, kept on the run whatever its start did after (an app that did not stay up still built its image), the newest first; its one `build.log` row says so (events.md) · the build digest is HMAC-SHA256, never a plain hash, under a key derived from the server's secret-encryption key's active version, over the project, preview's `where: build` names and values sorted by name, the image, and the builder's identity the executor answered before the build (executor.md, `GET /builder`), each length-prefixed; each candidate's is computed again with its own image and the builder's identity now, so a patched or replaced builder rebuilds, and a row whose image another writer changed never matches; the values never leave the server's memory, and only the digest is stored — on the run that built, and on every deploy row a Canyon run writes, a promotion's `prod` row copying it only from a `preview` row Canyon runs whose image is the one shipped, and a row the org's report writes (the report row) holding none · **beside it, the values-only HMAC** (design/PATCHING.md §5): HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from that same active version — a derivation of its own, never the digest's key — over the project and preview's `where: build` names and values sorted by name, each length-prefixed, with no image and no builder, stored with the version of the key it was made under (`values_key`) wherever the digest is stored, and copied with it: a promotion's `prod` row takes both from the same `preview` row, and a reused image's run makes it as a build does. So two builds can be told to have had the same values without either being read back, under one key version only. A run with no digest (an executor without `GET /builder`) still stores it, and a rebase patch's row keeps it and its version, since a rebase changes no build value, while its build digest becomes none (amended 2026-11-02, #2117) · **the builder that built it** (`builder`; amended 2026-11-02, #2117): the identity executor.md's `GET /builder` answered before the build, kept on the run that built and travelling with its image as its buildpacks do — a reused image's row, a promotion's `prod` row, a rebase patch's row — none when that build recorded none (an executor without `GET /builder`, or a build before it); the server's alone, answered by no read · **the buildpacks that ran** (executor.md, *The buildpacks that ran*) are kept on the run that built and travel with its image: every deploy row — a reused image's, a promotion's `prod` row, and a row the org's report writes, whose image is the one Canyon built — carries those recorded for the build of its image, none when that build recorded none, and `stack.blessed` reads preview's (amended 2026-10-04, #636) · anything else builds: a build value added, changed or removed, another package, a record written before the digest (it has none), a new key version, a new builder, an executor without `GET /builder` (it reuses nothing and records no digest), another project's record, and every run under `run_by: org`, which calls no `/run` · **a build waits its turn and never fails for waiting** (amended 2026-10-04, #1652): the host runs at most `CANYON_BUILD_CONCURRENCY` builds at once across every run (1 by default; `deploy/k8s` sets 4), first come first served, and one past that waits in the host's build queue — a busy answer asked again inside its slot after its `Retry-After` — its run's progress saying so (`progress`, `build`, `queued`), its 30 minutes counted from the call the executor accepts; a cancel takes a waiting build out of the queue at once and ends the run `interrupted` · a reused image `/run` answers missing (executor.md, `/run`'s `404`) is built as any deploy builds, once, and the run goes on to `/run` the new image · a build that fails ends the run `failed` with `failure_reason: build` (the package did not build — the user's fix; the executor's message is the last `build.log` line) · a package no buildpack on this host detects ends it `failed` with `detect`, never `build`: nothing was compiled (executor.md, `/build`); the detector's lines, then the executor's message, are the `build.log` rows, and the run read carries `builds` · a build whose pod the cluster had no room to schedule by the build's deadline ends it `failed` with `no_room` (its `PodScheduled` condition `False` with reason `Unschedulable`; the condition's message, as the scheduler wrote it, is the last `build.log` line; capacity, never the package's fault nor Canyon's; amended 2026-10-05, #1637) · a container that does not stay up, or answers nothing on port 3000, ends it `failed` with `start` (the app's fix, not Canyon's: the app's last lines, then the executor's reason, are the last `build.log` lines, events.md) · **under `run_by: org`** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3) the run builds, scans and gates as here, then sends a `deploy` release (`release.schema.json`) instead of calling `/run` and waits up to 30 minutes for the org's report, which writes its `deploy.completed` row; the `build.log` lines are events.md's, a `4xx` from the org ends the run `failed`/`error` at once, a `5xx` or no answer is tried again for an hour, and past the 30 minutes the run ends `failed`/`error` with the release still open · `409 {error:"deleting"}` as on gate-runs · the deploy is recorded in one transaction — the `deploy.completed` row, the environment, and its own audit row (`action: "deploy preview"`, `actor` the one who started the run, `run`, `detail{package, image_digest, url}`), so who deployed what is on the trail whoever triggered it · **`Canyon-Harness`** (**added 2026-11-15**, #2953): `claude_code` or `codex`, the coding agent the CLI runs under by its harness markers, kept on the run for the usage report's `agents`; any other value, or none, keeps nothing and refuses nothing |
| `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/promotions` | `{gate_run, promote_anyway?}` — **a run id, never "latest"**; `promote_anyway` a boolean, the person's override of preview's answer (below) | **a pull request's version is never promoted** (#1947): while the preview row the promotion would ship runs a pull request's package, whatever `gate_run` names — that pull request's own run among them — it is `409 {error:"pull_request", message:"Preview runs a pull request's version. Merge it, then deploy main."}`, before the run is looked for and before any approval is filed · **`409 held`** (*The hold*) while production is held — a promotion, a rollback's, and approving a production approval alike, an admin's too — read first under the lock the run starts in, before the approval, preview or the run named are read: no approval is filed and nothing starts · `202 {run: {id, state:"pending"}}` — the gate-runs row's shape: a **promote run**, one run of the project that never supersedes and is never superseded — an undeploy of `prod` ending it is no superseding (*The brake*): `409 run_in_progress` while any run of the project is `pending` or `running`, and every other run request answers the same while it is, read by its id and followed on its timeline; the receipt, the fleet and the gate-runs list read gate runs only, so it never stands in for the run a receipt names · the run makes every resource the promoted package declares real in `prod` — prod's own, never preview's — waiting on a driver that answers pending (30 minutes per resource), then starts preview's image in prod with prod's runtime values; its `build.log` lines say so (events.md) and the last is `live at <url>` · it ends `clean` when live, recorded in one transaction: the `deploy.completed` row (`env: "prod"`), the environment and the step's audit row (`action: "deploy prod"`); the environment is the project read's `envs.prod` object — `package, label, image_digest, gate_run, deployed_by` are the preview row's (a promotion ships the image preview runs, so it is the same version under the same name; both ends read the label from the packages table, nothing copies it), `url` the prod surface, `deployed_at` the promotion's instant, `promoted_by` the actor who clicked; the shape is `promotion` in `app-page.schema.json` · any failure ends it `failed` with `error` and its reason the last `build.log` line, before prod changes: the container prod runs keeps running and the environment is as it was · **under `run_by: org`** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3) the run makes prod's resources real as here, then sends a `deploy` release for `prod` in place of starting the image and waits for the org's report exactly as the deploys row says, the report writing the `deploy.completed` row; and *The promote receipt* reads what the org reported — until preview's release has reported `deployed`, nothing runs in preview and the receipt is `waiting` · the 202 writes the request's audit row, `run` the promote run, `detail{package, gate_run, answered?}`, `answered` there only when a person promoted despite it (below) · **the click is the decision**: nothing the receipt checked is checked again after the 202 — a policy edit, an approval expiring or a newer gate run does not stop a promotion already started · **`409 {error:"gate_not_clean" \| "approval_required", receipt}`** — `receipt` is *The promote receipt* below, evaluated for the run named and the actor asking; `approval_required` when `blocked_by` holds `prod.human-approval` and nothing else — the one refusal a person clears by clicking promote — and `gate_not_clean` for every other blocking set: any rule an admin set blocking can block a promotion (the policies row below: the four `prod.*` rules are editable, and so is `owner.required`), and `blocked_by` names the ones that did · **while the org's `prod_approval` is on** (Platform, the preferences row; amended 2026-10-07, #1820) a session that is not a browser sign-in's — the CLI's — never starts a promotion: where its receipt's `blocked_by` is `prod.human-approval` alone and the project is not marked for deletion (`409 deleting`, below), the route answers **`202 {approval: {code, url, expires_at}}`** in place of the run and of the 409 — a production approval (*Production approvals*, below) naming the run asked for and the package preview runs, `code` 8 Crockford base32 characters from `crypto/rand` shown `XXXX-XXXX`, `url` the host's public URL and `/#/approve?code=<code>`, `expires_at` 24 hours on, RFC 3339 at whole seconds UTC — and replaces the project's pending approval, whose row is deleted and whose code then reads `404`; no run starts, `409 run_in_progress` and `409 undeploying` are the approval's to meet when it is approved, never the request's, and one audit row is written, `request promotion`, `detail{package, gate_run, code, expires_at, replaced?}`, `replaced` the code it replaced · **`409 {error:"preview_answered", message, version, answered}`** (**amended 2026-10-08**, the owner; QA round 4, django-400: Canyon said preview answered `400 Bad Request` at `/`, an agent promoted it with `--yes`, and production served the broken page) when the preview row the promotion would ship carries an `answered` that is an answer to say beside readiness (the projects row, `envs.<env>.answered`) — that row is the version promoted, so the answer is that version's own; a preview row with no `answered` (the org runs it, or the deploy could not ask) is never refused for it · `version` is that row's version, named as *Naming a version* says, and `answered` that row's, `{status, said}` or `{error}`, both read under the lock with the image the promotion would ship · `message` is `Not promoted: <version> answers <status> <reason phrase> at / on preview. Fix the app and deploy again, or have a person promote it from the dashboard after checking it.`, or for nothing answered `Not promoted: <version> gives no answer at / on preview (<error>). Fix the app…` the same, `<version>` named as *Naming a version* says and the reason phrase the standard one, none for a code with none · **the order of the refusals**: `pull_request` (#1947), then `nothing_in_preview`, then the receipt's (`gate_not_clean`, `approval_required`), then `deleting`, then — the CLI's ask while `prod_approval` is on filing its production approval here, whose page shows the answer (*Production approvals*) — `preview_answered`, then `run_in_progress` and `undeploying` as the run starts · **a browser sign-in overrides it; a CLI session never does**: `promote_anyway: true` from a session whose `flow` is `browser` promotes as if nothing answered, when `gate_run` reviewed the package preview runs (an override names the version the person saw), and the 202's audit row carries `detail.answered`, preview's answer, so the trail says the person promoted despite it; from any other session — the CLI's, whatever its flags — `promote_anyway` changes nothing and the refusal stands; `promote_anyway` where nothing is refused changes nothing and records nothing · with `CANYON_AUTH` off no session tells the dashboard from the CLI, as with `prod_approval`, so `promote_anyway` counts from any caller there; the CLI never sends it · the 409 changes nothing: no promotion, no audit row · **`409 {error:"nothing_in_preview", message, receipt}`** when nothing runs in preview, or the preview Canyon runs reads `restarting`, `stopped` or `unknown` — the executor asked once, before the route reads under its lock — the receipt is `waiting` (*The promote receipt*) · the 409 changes nothing: no promotion, no audit row · **`409 {error:"deleting"}`** when the project is marked for deletion, read under the delete's lock before the run starts: nothing started, nothing recorded · **`409 {error:"undeploying"}`** while an undeploy of the project is in progress, as the gate-runs row · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project, or a run that is not this project's · **a rollback review's run** (the gate-runs row's `rollback_to`; **amended 2026-10-11**, #2055; design/OPERATIONS.md §1) ships the run's `ships` in place of preview's image: preview is not read, so neither `pull_request`, `nothing_in_preview` nor `preview_answered` is asked, and the receipt is the rollback review's (*The promote receipt*) · **`409 {error:"run_in_progress", message, run}`** while that review is still `pending` or `running` — it is the project's active run, `run` its `{id, state}` — nothing changes · **`409 {error:"nothing_to_ship", message}`** when the ended run recorded no `ships`, whatever the rules' states — before an approval is filed or a run starts; nothing changes · **`409 {error:"outdated", message:"production runs another version now: review it again"}`** when production no longer runs what it ran as the review started (nothing then: when it runs anything), read under the lock; nothing changes · the order: `run_in_progress`, `nothing_to_ship`, `outdated`, then the receipt's refusals; everything else is as above — the receipt's refusals, a production approval while `prod_approval` is on, `deleting`, `run_in_progress`, `undeploying` · the prod row's `package` is the run's, `gate_run` the run and `deployed_by` who started it · the promote run starts the image with the image it replaces kept (`keep`, executor.md), so the rollback can itself be undone; an image whose `/run` answers `404` fails the run (`error`) before prod changes, its last `build.log` line `<version>'s kept image is gone, so it can't be put back as it ran.`, and that image is no longer `kept` · **the `deploy prod` row's `detail`** is `{package, image_digest, url, replaces, rollback, reason?}`: `replaces` the package production ran until then, or `null` when nothing ran there; `rollback` true when the promotion shipped a rollback review's `ships`, else false; `reason` the review's, when it had one — under `run_by: org` the report's `platform reported deployed` row of a `prod` release carries the same three — the trail reads it `put <version> back in production, replacing <version>`, or `put <version> back in production` with nothing replaced |
| `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id/envs/:env` | — | `204` — **undeploy: stops the app and keeps its data.** The executor removes the env's container (`POST /stop`, contracts/executor.md), the env leaves `envs` on the project read and the fleet, and one audit row is written, `detail{env, package, run?}` — `package` the one the env ran, `null` when nothing was deployed there, `run` the run *The brake* ended or asked to end, absent when none; the project's secret rows — both envs — stay exactly as they are, so the next deploy starts the app with them. An environment the org runs (`run_by: org`): `202 {release}` — a `remove` release is sent, the environment stays on the project read with `pending` until the org reports `removed`, and leaves it then, with the same audit row, written at the `202` · **it is never refused for a run** (**amended 2026-10-11**, #2047): it conflicts only with the run writing the same environment, which it ends, asks to end, or leaves a stop pending on (*The brake*, below), and answers **`202 {stopping: {run}}`** when it left a stop pending — `run` the gate-runs row's shape — the run then stopping the environment itself · Deleting a project is the route that destroys data, never this one · `env` ∈ `preview, prod`, else `400 {error:"invalid_env"}` · who may is who may put something there: a contributor or above for `preview`, as a deploy, and below that `403 {error:"team_required"}` (*App teams*); for `prod`, while `prod.human-approval` is blocking, only a person — `403 {error:"person_required"}` otherwise, as promote's receipt refuses an agent; the brake changes neither · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project, and on an env with nothing deployed there, no open release there and no run writing it `pending` or `running` (as the logs row) · **the order of the refusals**: `404` the project, `403 team_required`, `400 invalid_env`, `403 person_required`, `409 held` (`prod` while production is held: nothing runs there, *The hold*), then `404` nothing there · `500 {error:"internal"}` when the executor fails: what *The brake* committed before the call stays — an ended run, a cancel asked — and the env stays recorded, so a retry resumes · every refusal reaches neither the executor nor the trail · a wait on the app's undeploy lock — behind an undeploy, a hold, a delete or a recreation of its network (Egress control) — is bounded at 20 s: past it `409 {error:"busy", message}` and nothing is done, so no waiter holds a database connection without end (**amended 2026-10-15**, #2254) |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/envs/:env/image` | — | **System libraries** (design/PATCHING.md §3, §8; the app page's *System libraries* card): what the image `env` runs holds of Canyon's, the system libraries and build tools it adds, as `platform.known-vuln` reads it (*The promote receipt*), whatever the rule's state. It is read from stored rows only, and the image is never read again. At minimum **contributor** (design/PERMISSIONS.md, technical detail); below it `403 {error:"team_required"}` naming the owners, as every team route · `env` other than `preview` or `prod` → `400 {error:"invalid_env"}` · nothing deployed there → `404 {error:"not_found"}` · `200 {env, image_digest, state, why, scanned_at, db_built_at, read_at, layers[], findings[], notes[], patched[]}`. `state` is `checked`, `could_not_check` or `not_scanned`: `not_scanned` when no image scan is stored, `checked` when the row would read a count, and `could_not_check` otherwise. `why` is the receipt row's own words. `scanned_at` is when the image scan was stored, null before one. `db_built_at` is the oldest database among the layers' newest matches, null before any. `read_at` is this read's time. `layers` are the image's, bottom first, each `{diff_id, owner}`: `owner` is `run-image`, `lifecycle`, `app`, the id of the buildpack whose layer it is, or null where the image's `io.buildpacks.lifecycle.metadata` names none. `findings` are the counted findings (finding.md, *System libraries and build tools*), most severe first, then by name, version and vulnerability. Each is a profile result with `tool: "canyon/promote-gate"`, `class`, and `dependency` with its `layer`. `notes` are `{name, version, layer, class, why}`, by layer, then name, version and why. Both are empty unless `state` is `checked`: a stale or unreadable match shows nothing as current · `patched` (**amended 2026-10-18**, #2086; design/PATCHING.md §8) is what Canyon's patches fixed in the image `env` runs, read from the patch runs that made it, newest first, each `{name, from, to, vulnerability, class, run, at}` — the package, its version before and after (its patch run's line *fixed*'s `<to>`, *Patch runs*), the vulnerability, its class as the patch run recorded it — `os` for a rebase's, which records none (amended 2026-11-03, #2631) — the patch run, and `at` when the image `env` runs last reached `env`, its row's `deployed_at`, the same on every entry — and `[]` on an image no patch made (patch.schema.json's `patched`) · **found by the image's digest** (amended 2026-10-29, #2105, the decision of #2556): the patch run whose `patch preview` row names that digest as its `image`, then, while that patch's `replaces` was itself a patch's `image`, the patch before it, each with its own fixes — so a rollback to an image a patch made names that patch, and `at` is when the rollback put it back |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/status` | — | `{project, envs, last_run, promote_run, receipt, earlier, needs, hold?}` — **to a responder with no grant on the app** (amended 2026-10-13, #2204), trimmed to what approving needs: `{project, envs{prod?}, last_run, promote_run, receipt, needs, hold?}`, `envs.prod` production as a viewer reads it, `{url, state?, seen_at?}`, and nothing of preview, of what either answered or said, of who deployed what, nor `earlier` (the schema's `status_responder`), and the receipt's `platform.known-vuln` packages without their `layers` and `go_stdlib` (amended 2026-10-30, #2579: the image read's technical detail, *The promote receipt*), and `deps.known-vuln`'s `built_app` whole, its fingerprints what the responder approves (amended 2026-11-02, #2610), and `prod.egress-allowed`'s `why` naming no destination (amended 2026-11-06, #2125; *The promote receipt*) · `hold` the project read's, while production is held (*The hold*), and the receipt then carries `held` · **`needs[]`** (**added 2026-11-08**, #2724) the app's needs (*What needs a person*), `blocked_prod` and `will_refuse` among them, read from this receipt, so the band and the go-live box never parse a row's `why`; trimmed for a responder, the `held` need alone · `receipt` is the receipt **at rest**, the same shape the 409 carries, evaluated for `promote_run` (the run the promote button would send) and the actor reading; `prod.clean-gate` answers for `promote_run` exactly as *The promote receipt* says — fail-closed, so with no run, or a run still under way, it has not passed, and a promote naming such a run is the same `409 {error:"gate_not_clean"}` · `project` is the project's id, `envs` the project read's `envs`, each with its `state`, and **`stopped: {exit_code, said}` on one whose `state` is `stopped` and that the executor said why of** (executor.md, `POST /running`, asked with `why: true` by this read alone — the fleet row, the viewer's reduced row and the promote read carry none) — `exit_code` the app's last exit code, `said` its last line masked by the server with that app and environment's runtime values by `/run`'s rule (`<NAME>`, the longest first, a URL's userinfo password as `<NAME password>`, never `PORT` or `CANYON_IDENTITY_*`), then each control, format, line- or paragraph-separator character (Unicode Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp: an escape, a bidi override) removed, as a description refuses them, then cut to its first 200 characters, `""` when it wrote none, and `""` too when it cannot be masked with the values the app was handed: a secret of that environment set (`set_at`) or removed (its audit row) at or after the start (`created_at`) of the run that started what runs there — the deploy run in preview, the promote run in prod, recorded on the deploy row when it is written, so a row written before has none and says no line — or the values not opening — the exit code alone then, never a line masked with other values (#1597); the screen draws it as *exited <exit_code> — <said>*, or *exited <exit_code>* when `said` is `""`, as text, never markup · **on one whose `state` is `restarting`, its `restarting` adds that same `exit_code` and `said`, of its last exit, when the executor said it** (executor.md, *Why it stopped*: on Kubernetes always, on Docker while it is down between two starts; amended 2026-10-10, #1597 — an app that keeps exiting never reads stopped on Kubernetes), masked and cut by the same rule, the screen drawing it as *last exited <exit_code> — <said>*; · **`envs.prod.missing`** (**added 2026-11-11**, #2786): on production while Canyon runs it, the names, sorted, of the runtime values a person set for preview (`where` `runtime`, `origin` `user`) that production has none of, `[]` when none is missing: the same rule as a promotion's `<NAME> is set for preview but not production`, said on the read beside what production answered, since an app without one may answer 500 there. A value a declared resource gives is the promotion's to make and a build value is in the image already, so neither is named. Names, never values. Absent on an environment the org runs, and on a responder's trimmed read; `last_run` the newest run's id or `null` — the run whose findings the app page shows · **`promote_run` is the newest run of the package preview runs** — a patch run only while preview runs the image it deployed (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-18, #2086) — or `last_run` when nothing runs in preview: a later review of another package — a `canyon check` of unshipped code, a deploy the gate stopped — never stands in for the run behind what preview runs, so it never blocks promoting a clean preview (amended 2026-10-01, `canyonv2-5r11.13`) · the passing count is the receipt's rules folded, so nothing else carries one · **`?run=<id>`** (**amended 2026-10-11**, #2055): the receipt is evaluated for that gate run of the project, as a promotion naming it would read it, and `promote_run` is that run; one that is not this project's gate run is `404 {error:"not_found"}`; a rollback review is read only when named — it never stands in for `last_run` or `promote_run`, nor is it a newer review of preview's package (*The promote receipt*) · **`earlier[]`**, on this read alone (**amended 2026-10-11**: on the read, not on `envs.prod`, so it is there after an undeploy too; design/OPERATIONS.md §1.4): production's earlier versions, each `{package, version, image_digest, deployed_at, promoted_by, patched?, kept}`, from production's history — a row for every promotion that went live there, written with the deploy row — taken per `(package, image)` at its newest row, newest first, the `(package, image)` production runs now left out, at most five · `version` is the package named as *Naming a version* says, `deployed_at` when it went live, `promoted_by` the actor who promoted it then · `patched: true` marks an image Canyon's patch produced (design/PATCHING.md), so one version twice reads as two images; absent otherwise · `kept` is true when the image was in the server's `keep` (executor.md) on every `/run` of the app since it stopped running, so it can be put back as it ran; false once one `/run` left it out, or a promotion's `/run` found it gone — under `run_by: org` no `/run` cleans the org's registry, so its images stay kept (design/OPERATIONS.md §1.6) · `[]` when production ran nothing before, and the gate-runs row's `not_earlier` reads the same list · a contributor or above reads it, as the rest of this read: below contributor the read is `403 team_required` |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/logs?env=&lines=&follow=&redaction=` | — | `200` `text/plain; charset=utf-8`, header `Canyon-Log-Redaction: none` — closed set in v1, `masked` under `redaction=masked` (below), a day-2 shipper answers `shipper` — chunked, raw passthrough: the container's stdout+stderr exactly as docker wrote them, no timestamp, no framing, no redaction — but for two lines: a read that fails after the executor's `200` ends on `canyon: this log could not be read: <reason>` (contracts/executor.md, `GET /logs`), passed through like any other; and **`canyon: keepalive`**, which the server writes after 30 s in which the stream carried nothing, and again after every 30 s more, only where the last line has ended (never inside an unfinished line): a proxy ends a response that is quiet too long (an AWS ALB at 60 s by default, nginx's `proxy_read_timeout` at 60 s), and a quiet app is not a dead stream (#1443). `canyon logs` drops the line; any other reader may too · `env` ∈ `preview, prod`, omitted = `preview` (the secrets rows' default, routes.md:175/177), else `400 {error:"invalid_env"}` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project, and on an env with nothing deployed there (as `packages/current` above) · `502 {error:"app_unreachable"}` when the deploy record names a container the executor no longer has · `500 {error:"internal"}` on any other executor failure, detail in the server log only, never the response · `lines` is the backlog, an integer `1..10000`, omitted = `200`, else `400 {error:"invalid_lines"}` · `follow` ∈ `true, false`, omitted = `true`, else `400 {error:"invalid_follow"}` · `redaction` ∈ `none, masked`, omitted = `none`, else `400 {error:"invalid_redaction"}`, never a raw read (**added 2026-11-09**, #2744): under `masked`, the dashboard's read, `follow` omitted = `false` and `true` is `400 {error:"invalid_follow"}`, and `lines` is 1–200, else `400 {error:"invalid_lines"}`; the last `lines` lines whole, masked with the environment's runtime values by the status read's rule (envs.<env>.stopped: the same function), header `Canyon-Log-Redaction: masked` with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and the same audit row; a log that rule can't mask — a secret of `env` set or removed at or after the start of what runs there, or a deploy row with no recorded start — is `409 {error:"not_maskable"}` with no line and no row; one over 1 MiB is `422 {error:"too_long_to_mask"}` with no line and no row, never cut to fit, since a cut can split a secret's value and leave its tail in the clear (**amended 2026-11-12**, #2744's review) · the raw read carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` too · the last `lines` lines, then under `follow=true` the live tail, ended by the client; under `follow=false` the body ends after them, so a reader that is not a person (an agent, a pipe) gets the log and its end (**amended 2026-10-07**, QA batch: `canyon logs` hung every agent that ran it) — the server sends `tail=<lines>&follow=<follow>` to the executor · a contributor or above may read, masked or not, and a caller below is `403 team_required` (*App teams*) · one audit row: `action` the route, `detail{env}` — who read which environment's logs, never a line of the log |

### The brake

**Amended 2026-10-11** (#2047; design/OPERATIONS.md §3.3): undeploy takes an environment offline at
once, whatever else the app's runs are doing. It conflicts only with the run writing the environment
it takes offline, and is never refused for a run — it answers no `409 run_in_progress`.

- **The run writing an environment.** A project has one active run at a time (the gate-runs row). It
  writes `prod` when it is a promote run, and `preview` when it is a deploy run — a gate run started to
  go on into build and run (the deploys row; a push's, and a pull request's a person deployed, among
  them), which its row records when it starts — or a patch run (*Patch runs*; amended 2026-10-18,
  #2086). A patch run and a promote run shipping a patch also write their environment while they
  watch it: an undeploy of it ends the watch clean, as *Patch runs* says, and goes on as below. A gate run that deploys nothing, a check, writes
  neither. A check and a run writing the other environment never conflict: they are neither waited on
  nor ended, so `canyon undeploy --env prod` goes ahead while a preview deploy runs. A preview undeploy
  during a promote run leaves the promotion alone: it ships the image it read when it started (`/stop`
  removes no image, executor.md), and production's row carries no build digest, preview's row being
  gone (the deploys row), so that package's next deploy builds.
- **Three steps, the undeploy lock held throughout**, so from the conflict check to the end a start of
  a run of the project answers `409 {error:"undeploying"}` and nothing else waits on it — **and the
  ledger lock never held across an executor call**:
  1. Under the ledger lock, taken before any runs row as every appender takes it, the conflicting run
