  - no image scan is stored for the digest under the project (finding.md, *System libraries and build tools*: a scan is the project's own): `no image scan recorded`;
  - the image scan answered `not_finished` (executor.md, target `image`):
    `could not check: <reason>`. The reason is:
    - `did not finish in time` for `timeout`;
    - `stopped before it finished` for `killed`;
    - `did not answer an SBOM` for `not_sarif`;
    - `the image is larger than this Canyon scans` for `over_cap` with `reason: "image"`;
    - `answered more than Canyon reads` for `over_cap` with `reason: "report"`;
    - `this Canyon host has no image for it` for `no_image`;
    - `the cluster had no room to run it` for `no_room`;
    - `its Java database could not be downloaded` for `db_missing`;
    - `its Java database is over 5 days old` for `db_stale`;
    - `its image scan did not finish` for any other answer.
  - **what is stored does not read whole** (finding.md, *System libraries and build tools*). It reads
    `could not check: a stored scan of the image does not read` in any of these cases:
    - a stored row of the image scan, or a match, does not decode;
    - a stored report is not trivy's (`SchemaVersion` 2, `Results` a list), or any element of a list
      stored does not read whole: a `null`; a component without its `bom-ref`, `type`, `name` or layer; a
      vulnerability without its id, `BOMRef` or layer; a diff ID not `sha256:` and 64 hex, or named twice;
    - a stored component names another layer than the one it is stored under;
    - the image has no lifecycle label, or one that does not parse;
    - its run image's top layer is not one of the image's layers, or no layer is the run image's;
    - a match's database was built after the read;
  - a layer of the image has no stored row of its packages (a layer holding none is stored with none):
    `could not check: Canyon kept only part of the image`;
  - no layer holds a package of class `os`, `runtime`, `lifecycle` or `unknown`, so no match can say the
    image is clean: `could not check: the image scan listed no package Canyon put there`;
  - a layer a re-match covers (finding.md, *System libraries and build tools*) has no match stored:
    `could not check: the image has not been matched yet`;
  - **the newest match of a layer is stale**: its database was built more than 5 days before the read.
    It reads `could not check: its vulnerability database is over 5 days old`, and it blocks
    wherever the rule blocks. A days-old scan never passes a version;
  - **the image's last rescan did not match every layer it covers** (design/PATCHING.md §3.2, *Stale or
    failed*), and none has since: `could not check: its last rescan did not finish`. What the
    layers matched before is no answer for now (amended 2026-10-15, #2075);
  - a match does not read whole (finding.md, *The join*): `could not check: a match names a
    package the image does not hold`.

  A failed read of the store answers no receipt at all, so it never passes.

  **While its `settings.source` names an imported tool** (design/PATCHING.md §3.4; added 2026-11-03, #2077) **the tool
  adds to Canyon's own scan and never replaces it.** The row reads the matches exactly as above, every check and
  could-not-check of theirs first, and then, beside them, the tool's newest report of the image in each of its
  categories (the image reports row), by when each was attached. Then, in this order: no report of the image,
  `could not check: no <tool> report is attached to this image yet`; a stored report that does not read, or attached
  after the read, `could not check: a stored report of the image does not read`; any category's newest report attached
  more than 5 days before the read, `could not check: its <tool> report for <category> is <n> day<s> old: attach a newer
  one`, the category the report's normalised `automationDetails.id` so a person knows which to attach again, or
  `could not check: its <tool> report is <n> day<s> old: attach a newer one` for a report of no category — a
  days-old export never passes a version, as a days-old database does not. Otherwise it counts the union of what the
  matches found and what the reports found, each result read by finding.md's *An imported image result*, a finding both
  name — the same package, version and vulnerability — once: the row passes only when neither counts anything, so a
  report finding nothing, or naming another image's packages, hides nothing the matches found. Its notes are both
  sources', each once, and with notes its `why` ends `; <m> note<s>: no fix published, or Canyon can't tell what put it
  there or which part of the image it's in`. Its `db_built_at` — the read's age — is the older of the matches' oldest
  database and the reports' oldest attach time, and `read_at`, `notes` and `packages` are as above. The patch plan never
  reads a report: patching plans from Canyon's own matches alone (amended 2026-11-03, #2630's review: the tool was the
  row's input in place of the matches, so an empty report hid what Canyon found).

  Otherwise the row reads **each layer's newest match**, by its database's `db_built_at`, so a rescan on a
  newer database moves the row and writes no verdict. It counts the findings at `high` or above, each
  package one entry (finding.md, *One problem, one entry*: one name and installed version). With any, it does
  not pass, `why` `<n> known-vulnerable package<s> with a published fix, in the system libraries and
  build tools Canyon adds`. With none, it passes, `why` `no known-vulnerable package with a published fix,
  in the system libraries and build tools Canyon adds`. Either way, with notes, `why` ends
  `; <m> note<s>: no fix published, or Canyon can't tell what put it there` (**amended 2026-10-30**,
  #2579: it said `Canyon can fix`, which named neither where the packages are nor read well beside a
  patch that had just fixed others; **amended 2026-11-01**, #2605: `the operating system` became `the system
  libraries`). No approval covers one of
  its findings yet: approving one lands with its dashboard card (design/PATCHING.md §13, PR 6). Once it
  has read the matches, its row carries three more fields, and a fourth when it counted any:
  - `db_built_at`: the oldest database of the newest matches it read;
  - `read_at`: when it read them;
  - `notes`: the number of notes;
  - `packages`: what it counted, so an admin or an agent reads what to update and what fixes it, never a
    count alone (**amended 2026-10-16**, #2300: the packages were in the image read alone, which no surface
    called). Each is `{name, version, advisories}`, a package by its name and installed version, in the
    findings' order (most severe first, then by name and version); `advisories` are the findings at `high`
    or above against it, each `{id, fixed}` once whatever layers hold it — the vulnerability's id and the
    versions that fix it as the database says them (the finding's `dependency.fixed`). Every reader of the
    receipt reads them: the dashboard's go-live checklist under the row, and `canyon status` and the
    promote refusal under its line (cli.md). **Amended 2026-10-30** (#2579: the row's old name said
    nowhere where these packages are, nor what fixes each): to a contributor and above each package also
    carries `layers`, each `{owner, class}` once, of the layers that hold its counted findings, bottom
    first — `owner` as the image read's `layers` name it (`run-image`, `lifecycle`, `app` or a
    buildpack's id; a counted finding's layer always has one), `class` its findings' there (`os`,
    `runtime` or `lifecycle`), so a reader says where it is and what fixes it: a newer run image (`os`)
    or a newer buildpack or lifecycle (`runtime`, `lifecycle`) — and `go_stdlib: true` when its purl is
    Go's standard library, `pkg:golang/stdlib`, absent otherwise. Both are the image read's technical
    detail (design/PERMISSIONS.md): a responder with no grant reads each package without them (the
    status row).

  A row whose `db_built_at` is more than 5 days before its `read_at` never passes. The fixture is
  `contracts/testdata/platform/claims-portal.json` (finding.md, *System libraries and build tools*).
- `blocked_by[]` is the ids of the rules with `blocks` and not `passed`, in `rules[]` order.
  Empty means the promotion goes ahead — unless `waiting` is present, when nothing can be
  promoted; non-empty is the 409.
- **What preview answered is not on the receipt** (amended 2026-10-08): a preview row whose `answered`
  is an answer to say beside readiness is refused outside it (`409 preview_answered`, the promotions
  row), so a receipt that passes can still meet that refusal; the status read's `envs.preview.answered`
  is what the page and the CLI read it from.

### Production approvals

**Amended 2026-10-07** (#1820, the owner; design/AUTH.md §4.5): while the org's `prod_approval` is on,
the CLI asks for a promotion and a person approves it in the dashboard. An approval names one
project, the run the promotion was asked for, and the package and image preview ran then. It is
`pending` until a person approves or denies it, `expired` 24 hours after it was asked for, and
`outdated` once preview runs another package or image than it names — an approval of a rollback
review's run (the gate-runs row's `rollback_to`) names its package and `ships`, and is `outdated` once
production no longer runs the image it ran as the review started, or runs anything when it ran nothing
(**amended 2026-10-11**, #2055), and once production is held, which stores it: the hold turns every
`pending` approval of the app `outdated` in its own transaction, recording on it who held production,
when and why, and a lift leaves it so (*The hold*; the record **amended 2026-10-15**, #2243) — read on every read and under
the lock on every decision: a person approves exactly the version the page showed, never what
preview runs by the time they click. At most one approval of a project is `pending`: the project's
next request deletes it (its code then reads `404`). Filing, approving and denying each read and
write the approval under the ledger lock a promotion takes, so of a request, an approve and a deny
exactly one wins. A code is drawn again when it collides with any approval's, and is looked up with
dashes and case ignored and the Crockford look-alikes I, L and O read as 1 and 0, as a device code
is. **Only a browser session decides**: approve and deny answer
`403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"approve it in Canyon's dashboard"}` to every caller
whose session's `flow` is not `browser` — before the code is read, so the approval is untouched;
the refusal is one `denied` audit row (the audit read) and one log line — and the agent that asked
can never answer its own request. The routes are served only while `CANYON_AUTH` is `oidc`, as the
device grants are; otherwise the `/api/v1` catch-all answers `404`. Every route here is at the
promotions route's level: `404 {error:"not_found", message}` for a code no approval has, then a
contributor or above on the approval's app, `403 team_required` below it (*App teams*). Approving
it yourself is allowed — the one who asked, in their own browser — as the Promote button is; the
setting decides where the act happens, not who. An approval filed while the setting was on can
still be decided after it is turned off. Approvals are deleted with their project. Cancelling the
promote run an approval started leaves it `approved`, naming that run.

**A patch approval** (**amended 2026-10-18**, #2086; design/PATCHING.md §4.5) is a production approval
of `kind: "patch"`: while the org's `patching` is `ask` (the preferences row), a patch whose watch
ran to its end in preview (*Patch runs*) is asked for production in one, filed by Canyon — `requested_by`
`{kind: "canyon", subject: "patch", display: "Canyon"}`, `requested_via` `canyon` — for the patch run
and the image it put in preview, whatever `prod_approval` says. Everything above holds of it: the code,
the three routes, a browser session deciding, contributor and above, 24 hours, `outdated`. And:
- **filed only onto what preview replaced**: when production runs the image preview ran before the
  patch, which the approval records as `replaces_image`. When preview is ahead, none is filed and
  production's `patch` reads `held`, `why` `preview_ahead`: a promotion of preview's version takes the
  fix there;
- **`outdated` once production moves**: once production runs another image than `replaces_image`, or
  nothing — a rollback, an undeploy — as well as once preview moves, read on every read and under the
  lock on every decision, so a patch never ships over a person's rollback; and once `patching` turns
  `off`, the app's own `platform.known-vuln` turns `off` (the preferences row), or the identity is paused, so
  an admin's stop stops what is asked too. Approving one is then
  `409 {error:"outdated", message:"production runs another version now: Canyon asks again if the patch still applies"}`,
  or, when preview moved, `message:"preview runs another version now: Canyon asks again if the patch still applies"`;
- **one per environment, and never over a person's**: a newer patch's approval supersedes an open one,
  as a project's next request does; none is filed while a person's approval is pending, and a person's
  request deletes a patch approval as it deletes any;
- **never onto a held production**: none is filed, and approving one is `409 held` (*The hold*);
- **filed again**: each rescan tick files one while the plan stands, production is not held, none is
  open and the app's team has denied none for that identity, so an expiry or an outdating is never the
  end. A denial is: nothing is filed again for that app and identity, and the fleet approval never
  decides for it. So is a patch that failed in production: once the promote run an approval of that
  identity started saw the patched app exit or restart in its watch — reverted, or its revert failed —
  the app's patch reads `reverted` in the identity's waves, as a revert in preview does, and counts
  toward its breaker (the Platform rows), and nothing is filed again for that app and identity
  (amended 2026-10-29, #2570); a newer identity is asked;
- **its image is kept** (executor.md, *What `keep` spares*) until it is decided, expires or turns
  `outdated`;
- **approving** starts the promote run for the patch run, which re-`/run`s the image that served
  preview (executor.md, invariant 4) with `keep` holding the image production ran, and watches
  production as *Patch runs* says; its revert puts that image back. Before its `/run` it reads
  production's row again: one that no longer runs the approval's `replaces_image`, Canyon's own, is never
  shipped over — the run ends `failed`/`interrupted`, nothing started, production's line `deploying to
  production: failed: production runs another version now: Canyon asks again if the patch still applies;
  the version running there is unchanged` (amended 2026-10-29, #2570). Only this promote run watches: a
  person's promotion of the patched preview is not watched;
- **its read** adds `kind`, `patch_run` (the patch run), `identity` (its platform identity, the Platform
  rows), `replaces_image`, `image` (**amended 2026-10-31**, #2593: the digest of the patched image it ships, the one
  the patch put in preview — so the page compares production's image with the image that goes live, never with a
  run image), `fixes[]` — each `{name, from, to, vulnerability, class}` — `proof`, exactly `The app's own files
  are byte for byte the ones in production.` (reworded 2026-11-02, #2614: it said `layers`) — a rebuild patch's (*Patch runs*, *A rebuild patch*; added 2026-11-02, #2117) `Rebuilt from the same code and the same dependencies with <newer>. A rebuild is a new program, not a copy of what production runs, so a person on your team decides.`, `<newer>` as its line *same* gave it, which the run records — `newer build tools` when it recorded none — and beside it `rebuild: true`, absent on a rebase's — and `watch: {until, result}`, `result` `passed` (patch.schema.json's
  `approval_read`); a person's approval carries none of them;
- **the fleet approval** (`POST /api/v1/patching/identities/:id/approve`, Platform) decides it as it
  comes due — never a rebuild patch's, which is always its team's (*A rebuild patch*) — as the approve route would for the admin who approved the identity: the same checks
  under the same lock, with no `promote_anyway` — no browser showed preview's answer — so `409
  preview_answered`, like every refusal, leaves it `pending` for the app's team. Approved, `decided_by`
  is that admin and its one audit row is `approve promotion`, actor the admin, `via` `canyon`, its
  detail the approve row's with `identity` added. It never decides the approval of an app with a
  scheduled job (design/JOBS.md; design/PATCHING.md §4.6): that app's team decides its own;
- filing writes one audit row, actor Canyon, `request patch approval` `detail{package, code, image}`.

| Route | Response |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/promotion-approvals/:code` | `200 {code, app, version, replaces, requested_by, requested_via, requested_at, expires_at, state, decided_by?, decided_at?, run?, receipt?, answered?, approver}` — `app` the project's id · `version` the approval's package, named as *Naming a version* says · `replaces` the version production runs, or `null` · `requested_by` the actor who asked, `{kind, subject, display}` · `requested_via` the asking session's channel, as the audit read's `via` (`cli`) · `state` `pending \| approved \| denied \| expired \| outdated` · `decided_by` (an actor) and `decided_at` once decided · `run` the promote run an approval started · `held` once a hold turned it `outdated` (**amended 2026-10-15**, #2243), the hold as it stood then, `{by, at, reason}` as the `409 held` body's `hold` — `by` the actor who held it, `at` RFC 3339 at whole seconds UTC — and still after a lift: the page says production is held, in the held sentence (design/EXPERIENCE.md D5), where an approval preview outdated says preview moved; absent on every other approval, an approval outdated before the record included · `receipt`, to a browser session only, *The promote receipt* for the approval's run and the actor reading — what a promotion they asked now would check; any other session's read (the CLI's poll) carries none and never asks the executor · `answered`, beside `receipt` and to the same reader, preview's `answered` (the projects row) while preview runs the approval's package and image and its row carries one (**amended 2026-10-08**): the page says it when it is an answer to say beside readiness · `approver` the reader, `{display, email}`, so the page says who is approving · a read: no audit row; `canyon deploy --env prod --wait` polls it |
| `POST /api/v1/promotion-approvals/:code/approve` | `202 {run}` — **the promotions route, asked by the approver for the approval's run**: the same checks under the same lock, the same refusals with the same bodies, each leaving the approval pending, and the same promote run · the body is empty or `{promote_anyway?}`, the promotions row's override: the page sends it when it showed preview's answer, so `409 preview_answered` is met by the approver's click, never by the request (**amended 2026-10-08**) · `409 {error:"outdated", message:"preview runs another version now: ask again from the CLI"}` when preview's package or image is no longer the approval's, read under that lock, and nothing starts — for a rollback review's, `message:"production runs another version now: ask again from the CLI"` when production no longer runs what it ran as the review started · the approval turns `approved`, with its run, in the transaction that starts the run — a conditional update of a `pending`, unexpired row, so a second click finds none — and that transaction's one audit row is `approve promotion`, `run` the promote run, `detail{package, gate_run, code, requested_by, answered?}`, `answered` as on the promotions route's row, in place of that row · `404` too for an approval no longer `pending` |
| `POST /api/v1/promotion-approvals/:code/deny` | `204` — the approval turns `denied` and nothing else changes; one audit row `deny promotion`, `detail{package, gate_run, code, requested_by}` · `404` too for an approval no longer `pending`; an `outdated` one can still be denied, but not one a hold outdated, which is no longer `pending` |

### What needs a person

**Added 2026-11-08** (#2724; design/EXPERIENCE.md, *What needs a person*): what waits on a person, as one
ordered list the server computes and the fleet read's rows and the status read carry as `needs[]`, so no
screen works out its own and two screens never disagree. Each need is `{kind, env, rank, since, you, ask[], …}`:
`kind` one of the eighteen below; `rank` its row's rank in the table, 1 to 11, so a screen orders needs across apps by the server's own order and holds no copy of it (**added 2026-11-09**); `env` `preview` or `prod` for a need of that environment, `null` for one of
the app; `since` when it began, RFC 3339 at whole seconds UTC, `null` where nothing records it; `you` whether
the reader can resolve it, by their level on the app and their role (never by their session: a route that
takes a browser's says so when it refuses another); `ask[]` who else acts on it, `{display, as}` with `as`
`admin` — on `held`, `blocked`, `unchecked`, `blocked_prod` and `unowned` the org's admins, `admin_emails` and
`bootstrap_admins` together, each by the display name of their newest session, else by the address
`admin_required` names (the preferences read answers every member those addresses), sorted; `[]` on every
other kind. The fields a kind adds:

| Rank | `kind` | `env` | Adds | `you` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `held` — production is held (*The hold*), `since` its `at` | `prod` | nothing: the row's and the read's `hold` say it | an admin, or a responder off the app's team (design/PERMISSIONS.md), exactly as the lift decides it: no owner or contributor grant now, directly or through a group their session carries, nor on the team the hold recorded, and groups read whole where a team group exists now or on that record; a responder holding a viewer grant lifts it (**amended 2026-11-09**) |
| 2 | `down` — Canyon runs production and the executor says it is `stopped` or `restarting` (`envs.<env>.state`), never while production is held, `since` its `deployed_at` | `prod` | `state` | the team |
| 3 | `refused` — an egress request production refuses now, by the `refused` tile's predicate (the fleet read), `since` its `first_refused_at` | `prod` | `destination`, `refusals`, `last_refused_at`, `other_calls` (the calls the other environment's records hold to `destination`, every port, in the records' 30 days), `held` (production is held: an Allow there answers `409 held`, a Dismiss does not) | the team |
| 3 | `refused_code` — a destination production's records hold refused that no allowlist can let through, a dotted IPv4 address or a port other than 80 and 443, seen since the deploy step started the running deployment's container (its deploy row's `container_started_at`, else `run_started_at`, else `deployed_at` on a row from before those columns) less the promote receipt's margin, 90 s, the deployment through Canyon, `since` its `first_seen_at` (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2738: anchored on the run's start, the outgoing version's calls during a preview's review and build counted as the new version's) | `prod` | `destination`, `port`, `why` ∈ `address \| port`, `calls`, `last_seen_at` | the team, in the code |
| 4 | `secret` — the latest review carries a blocking `secrets.*` finding with no approval: one no unexpired approval covers, as an approval covers a blocking finding (the approve rows), read whether or not the run is blocked. An approval with a reason is a person's decision, so an approved secret is no need; the review still shows it, marked approved, and the `secrets_exposed` tile counts it either way (**amended 2026-11-09**) | `null` | `version`, the newest package's | the team |
| 5 | `approval` — a production approval of the app `pending`, unexpired and current as its read reads it (*Production approvals*; a patch's is `patch`), `since` its `requested_at` | `prod` | `version`, `asked_by`, `code` (shown `XXXX-XXXX`), `expires_at` | the team |
| 6 | `blocked` — `latest.stage` is `blocked`, `since` `latest.since`; none while every one of those findings is a `secrets.*` one, which the `secret` need says with its fix, so a secret is one need, never two (**amended 2026-11-09**) | `null` | `version`, `count` (its run's blocking findings no unexpired approval covers) | the team |
| 6 | `unchecked` — as `blocked`, where every one of those findings is `gate.unreadable`; and **on the status read**, `prod` with one check, while `prod.egress-allowed` blocks and its row could not check: `{tool: "prod.egress-allowed", reason, fix}`, `reason` its `why` after `could not check: `, `fix` `host` where an admin's setting is the cause (Outbound is Off, an app on its old network) and `review` for any other, so a promotion it blocks is never without a need (**amended 2026-11-09**) | `null`, `prod` on the status read's | `version`, `count`, `checks[]`, `{tool, reason, fix}` from each one's `problem`, `could not check: <tool>: <reason>` (contracts/finding.md), `fix` `host` for a reason reviewing again fails on — `its vulnerability database is over 5 days old`, `this Canyon host has no image for it`, `could not load all of its rules`, `did not answer SARIF`, `answered more than Canyon reads` — and `review` for any other | the team |
| 6 | `blocked_prod` — **the status read alone**: its receipt's `prod.clean-gate`, blocking, refuses for findings that block before production alone (*The promote receipt*, the placed rules) | `prod` | `version`, `count`, `null` where a placed rule's row fails with no count to read | the team |
| 6, 7 | `will_refuse` — **the status read alone**: its receipt's `prod.egress-allowed` names what production doesn't allow; rank 6 while the rule blocks, 7 while it is advisory, none while it is off | `prod` | `version`, `blocks`, `denied[]`, `{destination, port, allowable}` in the row's order, `allowable` whether an Allow can let it through (a host name on 80 or 443), the rest a change to the code's | the team |
| 7 | `down`, `refused`, `refused_code` of preview, as of production, each read against preview's own deploy row and its container's start; `other_calls` production's | `preview` | as above | the team |
| 8 | `patch` — a patch approval of the app is open, as `needs_you` read it before (*Patch runs*), `since` its `requested_at` | `prod` | `version`, `code`, `expires_at` | the team |
| 9 | `access` — a request for access (*Access*), on a row its owners and admins read alone, `since` its `requested_at` | `null` | `asked_by`, `{kind: "person", subject, display}`, `display` the request's, else its address; `email`, the address it asked from, which Add and Dismiss name (the access routes), to whoever reads the request on the Access read (**added 2026-11-09**); `level`, the request's, as the Access read's `requests[]` says it (**added 2026-11-09**, #2774) | the reader |
| 10 | `unowned` — `owners[]` is empty | `null` | nothing | the row's `claimable` |
| 10 | `never_reviewed` — no run has ever looked at the app, `since` its creation | `null` | nothing | the team |
| 11 | `recreate` — to an admin alone, on Docker: the app's network is not what the host's `via` makes it (`networks_to_recreate`, the org's egress read) | `null` | `network` ∈ `internal \| open \| unknown` (a recreation under way or one that failed), `via` the host's | the admin |
| 11 | `notify_failing` — **the fleet read's `org_needs` alone**, to an admin: a chat channel whose webhook answered `404` or `410` (*Integrations*), until an admin saves it again, `since` it started failing (**added 2026-11-09**, #2725) | `null` | `channel`, its name; `channel_id`, its id | the admin |
| 11 | `licence` — **the fleet read's `org_needs` alone**, to an admin: the licence (*Licence*) is in `grace`, `lapsed` or `invalid`, `since` its `expires`, `null` for an invalid one (**added 2026-11-14**, #2879) | `null` | `licence`, the state; `grace_until`, in `grace` alone | the admin |
| 11 | `patching_unlicensed` — **the fleet read's `org_needs` alone**, to an admin: the server's `CANYON_PATCHING` is `on` but `patching` is `off` and no licence in force names `platform_patching`, so Canyon patches nothing (*Licence*, Patching at upgrade; **amended 2026-11-14**, #2881's review); on a server built without Canyon Enterprise, while its `CANYON_PATCHING` is `on`, whatever `patching` stores (*Licence*, A server built without Canyon Enterprise; **amended 2026-11-15**, #2923) | `null` | nothing | the admin |

`version` is named as *Naming a version* says; `the team` is a contributor or above on the app, or an admin.
The list holds them by rank, then `since`, the oldest first and `null` last, then by `kind` in the table's
order, then by `destination` or `asked_by`'s display. **Who reads it** (design/PERMISSIONS.md): what the reader
reads whole — a fleet row at `contributor` and above, every row for an admin, and the status read — carries
every need above it qualifies for; a responder's reduced row and the status read trimmed for one carry the
`held` need alone; no other reduced row carries `needs`. A reader below contributor learns nothing here they
could not read before: a read-only member's app page names who looks after it, from `owners[]`. **Fail
closed**: a client meeting a kind it does not know shows `Something here needs a person` with the app's page,
never nothing. **Bounded**: the fleet read computes the needs of every app the reader reads in a fixed number
of statements whatever the fleet's size, never one per app, and asks the executor once for every 400 environments, the most `POST /running` asks, the calls at once under one 2 s bound. **What it could not read is never all clear**: an environment whose running state the read could not tell is no need, which would flood the list while the executor is down, but the fleet read's `unchecked` counts them, and while it is above 0 no screen says nothing needs a person. The status read
evaluates its receipt for its two kinds, which no fleet read does.

## Egress control

What an app calls out to (design/EGRESS.md layer 1, *See*; **added 2026-10-11**, #2085): Envoy's access
logs reach the server's receiver, which keeps one row per destination an app env calls (`egress.schema.json`,
`destinations`; **amended 2026-10-14**, #2222: never a time series) and the gaps the rows miss calls in (`gaps`);
the reads below answer them. Who reads and changes what:
design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Outbound*, which wins on conflict. Layer 2, *Restrict* (**added 2026-10-18**, #2104;
design/EGRESS.md §3): each app env's mode and allowlist, the egress requests its refusals file, the policy
egress-control pulls and what Envoy answers a refused call (*The check*, below). Layer 3, *Inject* (**added
2026-11-07**, #2135; design/EGRESS.md §4): value routes, which add a stored credential to an app's calls so the
app never holds it, and what Envoy and egress-control say to each other to do it (*Interception*, below). The
server serves the rows that name it from #2137 on, and the leaf for an inject destination, an admin's sub-CA and a
rotation of the root from #2704 on.

| Route | Response |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/egress` | `200` `egress.schema.json`'s `project_read`: `{since, floor, envs: {preview, prod}, gaps[]}` — what the app called out to in the last 30 days, the rows' keep (design/EGRESS.md §2.4): `since` is 30 days ago · `floor` the app's network (design/EGRESS.md §2.2; **added 2026-10-15**, #2254), as its last start or recreation recorded it, no record reading open: `enforced` while internal, its calls out leaving through Envoy alone; `not_enforced` while open, the host's via `canyon` and an env of it Canyon runs — its calls reach past Envoy until an admin recreates the network; `null` otherwise — via `off` on an open network, or nothing Canyon runs · **on Kubernetes**, which has no app network, `floor` is read from the deploy rows, per env (**amended 2026-10-17**, #2286): `enforced` when every env Canyon runs ran under `canyon`; `not_enforced` when the host's via is `canyon` and an env Canyon runs ran `off`, or ran under no spec Canyon knows — its calls may reach past Envoy until that env's next `/run`; `null` otherwise · a retry on Kubernetes that finds its version already running starts nothing and records no spec on the env's row (**amended 2026-10-17**, #2286): the first attempt may have crashed before its `/run`, so the pod's policy is not known to be the floor, and the row reads not under `canyon` until that env's next `/run` (fail closed) · each env `{via, destinations[]}`: `via` the egress spec its running version was deployed under (`off` for a row from before one was recorded), `null` where nothing is deployed; each destination its row as stored, without the app and env, `{destination, port, first_seen_at, last_seen_at, calls, last_outcome, last_status_class, last_protocol, last_response_flags, last_credential_refusal}` — when it was first and last seen, the calls since first seen, and the last call's result; `last_credential_refusal` the last call of the row Canyon refused while adding a route's credential, `{reason, at}`, `null` for none since the row was first seen (*Interception*, A record; **added 2026-11-09**, #2692) — for every row last seen at or after `since`, the latest seen first, then by destination and port, `invalid` among them · `gaps[]` every gap ending in the window that names no app, **whosever the lost calls were**: a lost call names no app, so each may be this one's — and this app's own `over_cap` gaps, never another app's; the oldest first · never a path, query, header value, body or resolved address, which no record holds · **from layer 2** (**added 2026-10-18**, #2104; served from #2110 on — until then a reader finds them absent, and absent is not read, never allowed: a reader fails closed; the schema requires them from #2110) each env also carries `{mode, app_mode, allowed[], requests[]}`: `mode` the one that applies, `observe` or `allowlist` — `app_mode` when the env has one, else the org's (`GET /api/v1/egress`); `app_mode` the env's own, `null` while it follows the org's; `allowed[]` its allowlist, sorted, each entry once; `requests[]` its egress requests (*The check*), pending first, then dismissed, each the latest refused first, each with `refusing`, whether the env refuses it now as the fleet's `refused` tile counts it (the fleet read; **added 2026-11-08**, #2723: the app page's headline names only these), `false` on a dismissed one; **`allows[]`** (**added 2026-11-08**, #2722) who allowed each entry of `allowed[]` and when, one for each entry an Allow recorded, sorted by entry as `allowed[]` is: `{entry, by, at, last_refused_at}` — `by` the actor triple `{kind: "person", subject, display}` of the person whose `PUT` (below) put the entry there, `at` when, `last_refused_at` the latest `last_refused_at` of the requests that `PUT` closed, `null` when it closed none — so a reader can tell an app's failures from before an Allow from its calls since (design/specs/cli.md §6.1.5, §6.4.2); an entry allowed before #2722 has none, as nothing recorded who allowed it, and is still on `allowed[]`; `by` names a person to whoever reads this route, `contributor` and up, as the Outbound card's readers see who decides; and the read carries `platform[]`, the platform destinations (design/EGRESS.md §3.3), sorted — the same for every app, on no allowlist · **from layer 3** (**added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on, which makes it required: until then no route exists) each env also carries `routes[]`, `route_read`, sorted by destination: `{destination, header, scheme, placeholder_env, from_secret, waiting}` — `from_secret` true for one that came from a runtime secret, which only an owner or an admin replaces or deletes; `waiting` `null` while the policy names the route, else why not (*Interception*, Trust), the first that holds: `{reason: "host", secret: null}` while the host's via is `off`, its `credential_routes` is `false` (#2143), or its `run_by` is `org`; `{reason: "secret", secret}` while the env's runtime secrets name `secret`, one of the three trust variables; `{reason: "redeploy", secret: null}` while the env's trusted roots lack the signing root — **never a value**, to any level, an admin's included · minimum `contributor` (App teams): below it `403 team_required`, after an unknown or deleted app's `404` |
| `GET /api/v1/egress` · `PUT /api/v1/egress {via, mode, caps, credential_routes}` | **the host's egress setting** (design/EGRESS.md §2.5) · `GET` for any signed-in member → `200 {via, mode, credential_routes}` (`setting_member`), and to an admin `200 {via, mode, credential_routes, proxy, receiver, streams, last_entry_at, networks_to_recreate, canyon_refused, replicas, ca, caps}` (`setting_admin`): `mode` the org's default mode, `observe` or `allowlist`, `observe` until an admin sets it — every app env without its own follows it (**added 2026-10-18**, #2104; served from #2110 on — until then a reader finds it absent, and absent is not read, never allowed: a reader fails closed); `proxy` the server's `CANYON_EGRESS_PROXY`, `null` when unset; `receiver` whether it takes Envoy's records; `streams` how many of Envoy's streams are open now; `last_entry_at` when the last entry arrived, `null` before any; `networks_to_recreate` the apps, by id, sorted, Canyon runs an env of whose network is not what via makes it — open under `canyon`, internal under `off`, or unknown after a recreation that failed — which the next row's recreate moves; an app's network is what its last start or recreation recorded — and one with no record reads open, the truth for every app from before recreation, as via `canyon` was refused on every host (design/EGRESS.md §2.2; **amended 2026-10-15**, #2254) — and `null` on Kubernetes, which has no app network to recreate (the next row's `409`) · `networks` each app `networks_to_recreate` lists, by id, with its network now, `internal`, `open` or `unknown` (a recreation under way, or one that failed), read by the same rule, so Settings › Outbound reads them in this one call and never pages the fleet's needs; `null` on Kubernetes, as `networks_to_recreate` is (**added 2026-11-12**, #2767) · `canyon_refused` the `message` of the `409 egress_not_ready` a `PUT {via:"canyon"}` would answer now, `null` when it would be taken (**amended 2026-10-16**, #2300) · `replicas` each egress-control replica that asked for the policy in the last day (`GET /egress/policy`, below), by name, sorted — `{replica, have, seen_at, current_at}`: the version it last said it holds, `null` when none or out of shape, when it last asked, and the last time that version was the current one, `null` never — its policy's age now minus `current_at`, which Settings › Outbound shows (**amended 2026-10-18**, #2113; #2125) · `caps` the three egress caps, an admin's to set, each in its range and today's numbers until one is set (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2691) — `destinations` the rows an app env keeps (500), `new_names` the new names it may call a minute across every replica (30), `pending` the egress requests it may hold pending (50) (design/EGRESS.md §3.3; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Outbound*: host settings, an admin's; a member's read names none) · `ca` the interception roots' key dates, never a key (**added 2026-11-08**, #2704; design/EGRESS.md §4.5), `ca_read`: `signing` the root signing the current leaves (*Interception*, Trust), `null` while none is live; `roots` each root whose end has not passed, the newest first, `{fingerprint, byo, created_at, not_after, ends_at}` — `byo` an admin's sub-CA rather than Canyon's own root, `not_after` its certificate's end (a sub-CA's the earliest of the certificates it was given with), `ends_at` when Canyon stops using it: `not_after`, or 60 days after the rotation that replaced it began, the sooner; `rotates_at` when Canyon starts the next rotation itself, 60 days before the newest root's `ends_at` while that root is Canyon's own, else `null` — an admin's sub-CA never rotates by itself, so Settings › Outbound warns ahead of its `ends_at` · Settings › Outbound says `canyon_refused` above the choice and turns `Through Canyon` off while the host is not on it, so an admin never learns it on Save; after any refused save it shows the host's via again; it lists `networks_to_recreate`, each with its recreate (the next row), and shows no networks to recreate where it is `null`, and the app's Outbound card and `canyon status`'s `outbound` row say a `floor` that is not `enforced` — `not_enforced`, or a value they do not know (#2099) · `PUT` → `200` the admin's read · refusals in order, the first three before the body is read: `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · `403 {error:"person_required", message:"a person changes how apps call out"}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor · on a host with sign-in, `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's · `400 {error:"invalid_via"}` unless the body names `via`, `mode`, `caps`, `credential_routes` or any of them together and nothing else, a key left out keeping its value, `via` `off` or `canyon`, `credential_routes` `true` or `false` · **`credential_routes`** (**added 2026-11-09**, #2143, the owner's decision): whether Canyon adds credentials to apps' calls (Settings › Outbound, *Credentials*), `false` until an admin turns it on, an admin's alone as every key here is. While it is `true`, every `/run` that carries `egress` carries `egress.ca` (contracts/executor.md), so an app env trusts Canyon's interception root from its next deploy. While it is `false`, no `/run` carries `egress.ca`, the policy names no route at once, so nothing is intercepted, and a route's write is `409 credential_routes_off` (the route rows, below). Turning it on, `false` to `true`, is an Enterprise feature: `409 licence_required` (*Licence*), `feature` `outbound_keys`, while no valid or grace licence names it, before anything is written; turning it off, or a PUT leaving it as it is, is never refused for the licence (**added 2026-11-14**, #2879). Turning it off takes nothing an app already trusts away before that app's next deploy. It generates nothing: the roots already exist and rotate as they do · `400 {error:"invalid_caps"}` unless `caps` names one of `destinations`, `new_names` and `pending` at least and nothing else, each a whole number in its range — `destinations` 50 to 5000, `new_names` 1 to 600, `pending` 1 to 500 — nothing moved: never a value that means no cap (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2691) · `400 {error:"invalid_mode"}` for a `mode` other than `observe` or `allowlist` · a body naming `mode` is taken from #2110 on — until then `400 invalid_via`, the org's mode left as it is — and is refused, as an app env's mode is (the mode row, below), in this order: `409 {error:"floor_not_enforced", message:"apps' calls do not leave through Canyon's egress gateway while it is off"}` while the host's via — the body's when it names one — is `off`, when no app's floor holds a mode, then `409 {error:"egress_external", message:"the org runs its apps, so their calls do not leave through Canyon"}` while the host's `run_by` is `org` (design/HAND-OFF.md; design/EGRESS.md §5: under hand-off every layer is the org's) (**amended 2026-10-18**, #2104; the order design/PERMISSIONS.md's, *Outbound*, Order, #2110) · a body moving via to `off` while any app env holds a value route is `409 {error:"credentials_exist", apps, message:"<apps> have credentials that work only through Canyon. Their teams need to set those keys as secrets again and deploy, then remove the credentials."}` (`message:"<app> has credentials that work only through Canyon. Its team needs to set those keys as secrets again and deploy, then remove the credentials."` with one app, **amended 2026-11-15**), `apps` the apps that hold one, by name, before anything is written (**added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on): a route works only through Canyon · **`canyon` is `409 {error:"egress_not_ready", message:"Canyon can't send apps' calls out through its egress gateway: this server has no CANYON_EGRESS_PROXY. Leave this off until it does."}` while the server has no `CANYON_EGRESS_PROXY`**, on Docker and on Kubernetes alike — fail closed: a `/run` under `canyon` would name no Envoy · **on Kubernetes `canyon` is `409 {error:"egress_not_ready", message:"Apps routed through Canyon can't reach a sign-in served through this cluster's own gateway, so nobody could sign in to them. Set the front door to another sign-in provider, or leave this off."}` while the stored front door signs in at a sign-in served through the cluster's own gateway** — the host of the server's `CANYON_KUBE_GATEWAY_SIGNIN`, that provider's issuer URL (the Helm chart sets it on k3s to the Pocket ID it serves behind Caddy; an operator sets it to an issuer behind their own gateway), is the host, compared without case, of choice 2's `signin.issuer` or of choice 1 `oidc`'s `issuer` or `jwks_url`: that provider is reached only through the gateway's address, which kube-proxy rewrites to a pod address the floor refuses, so a floored app's sidecars could not sign anyone in. The door is read under the lock a front-door save takes, so a save made meanwhile is never missed (design/EGRESS.md §2.2; **amended 2026-10-17**, #2316, the owner's decision) · on Kubernetes `canyon` is taken otherwise, with no probe of its own: every `/run` there needs a front door, whose save passed the probe that refuses a cluster not enforcing NetworkPolicies (the front-door row; design/EGRESS.md §2.2; **amended 2026-10-17**, #2286) · on Docker `canyon` is taken with apps on open networks: the answer lists them in `networks_to_recreate`, and each is refused a deploy routed via `canyon` (`409`, executor.md, Egress) until an admin recreates its network (**amended 2026-10-15**, #2254, the owner's decision) · **`409 held`** (*The hold*), last, for a widening while a production it widens is held — the org's mode `allowlist` to `observe` while an app whose `prod` has no mode of its own is held, via `canyon` to `off` while any app's production is held — the first such app by id, its `message` the app's id and the hold's line (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Outbound*, A held production; **amended 2026-10-18**, #2110) · the actor's and the session's refusals write one `denied` row · one audit row `egress.set` `detail{via: {before, after}}` when via moves, `detail{mode: {before, after}}` when the org's mode does, `detail{caps: {before, after}}`, each the three caps, when a cap does (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2691), `detail{credential_routes: {before, after}}` when that does, each key that moved, none when nothing did; a mode reaches egress-control with the policy's next version (`GET /egress/policy`, below), a running app included · a change reaches no running app: **any deploy to preview — a redeploy of the same package included — reads it**, and a promotion, a rollback and a patch carry what the deploy that started their package in preview recorded — **except onto an internal network, where every `/run` carries `canyon`**, so neither the setting nor a recorded `off` moves an app off the floor: only the next row does (design/EGRESS.md §2.5) — **and on Kubernetes, which has no app network, every `/run` carries `canyon` while the host's via is `canyon`**, a recorded `off` included: the host's `canyon` wins, so nothing carried moves an app off the floor there, and an env leaves it at its first `/run` after via goes `off` (design/EGRESS.md §2.5; **amended 2026-10-17**, #2286) · **answers once every live egress-control replica holds it**, or names the one behind (*A change waits for the gateway*, below; **added 2026-11-12**, #2789) |
| `POST /api/v1/egress/networks/recreate {app}` | **an admin's Recreate networks, one app a call** (design/EGRESS.md §2.2; **added 2026-10-15**, #2254, the owner's decision): moves the app's network across the floor to what the host's via makes it — internal under `canyon`, open under `off` · minimum admin, a person, in a browser (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Host settings*): refusals in order, the first three before the body is read: `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · `403 {error:"person_required", message:"a person changes how apps call out"}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor · on a host with sign-in, `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's · `400 {error:"invalid_app"}` unless the body is exactly `{app}` · `409 {error:"egress_not_ready", message:"an app's NetworkPolicy is written at every deploy: there is no network to recreate"}` on Kubernetes (**amended 2026-10-17**, #2286), and under via `canyon` on a server with no `CANYON_EGRESS_PROXY`, PUT's own · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for an unknown or deleted app, and for one Canyon runs no env of · `409 {error:"busy", message}` while another recreation runs on the host — one at a time, a try, never a wait · then, under the app's undeploy lock, so no run of it starts meanwhile and a hold, an undeploy or a delete of it waits until the recreation ends, for 20 s at most (then `409 busy`) — the delete then stopping what it started: `409 {error:"deleting"}` for an app being deleted · `409 held` (*The hold*) while its production is held — nothing is stopped or started again · `409 run_in_progress {run}` while a run of the app is pending or running, but a patch run before its `/run`, which the recreation ends `failed`/`interrupted` first (*Patch runs*) · the host's via read again, under the lock · the app's network recorded unknown, in a transaction of its own — read as not internal, listed in `networks_to_recreate`, never `floor` `enforced` — then the executor's `POST /networks/recreate {app, internal}` (executor.md), `internal` the host's via is `canyon`, bounded at three minutes, which stops the app's containers and recreates the network; then, in a transaction of its own, the app's network recorded — internal or open, and when — which `floor` and `networks_to_recreate` read; the lock and the connection given back before the answer is written; then each env Canyon runs, prod first, its outage the shorter, unless the app was marked deleting meanwhile, started again with `/run` from its deploy row — its image, today's runtime values, `egress` as `internal` says — and the row's `egress` set to that spec · `200` the admin's read of the setting, the app gone from `networks_to_recreate` · **fail closed**: `502 {error:"recreate_failed", message}` when the executor's call or an env's `/run` fails — what it stopped stays stopped, never back on an open network, and the same call again repeats the whole of it; the message names the envs left stopped · one audit row `egress.networks.recreate`, project the app, `detail{internal, started[], stopped[], failed}` — the envs started again and those left stopped, and `failed` `null`, or `network` when the executor's call failed — `stopped` then empty, the failure saying nothing of what it stopped, the network recorded unknown — `start` when an env did not start again, `record` when the network or the rows could not be recorded (`500`) — once the executor's call was made, whatever it answered · the actor's and the session's refusals write one `denied` row |
| `POST /api/v1/egress/ca/rotate {}` · `{cert, key}` | **a rotation of the interception root** (design/EGRESS.md §4.5; **added 2026-11-08**, #2704): `{}` has Canyon mint a new root, five years, as at the first start that serves the policy; `{cert, key}` makes an admin's sub-CA the new root (BYO), so apps whose images trust the org's root trust what Canyon signs under it · minimum admin, a person, in a browser (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Host settings*): refusals in order, the first three before the body is read: `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · `403 {error:"person_required", message:"a person changes how apps call out"}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor · on a host with sign-in, `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's · `400 {error:"invalid_ca", reason}` for a body out of `egress.schema.json`'s `ca_rotate`, `reason` the first that holds: `body`, no such object or past 64 KiB; `cert`, not one to four certificates as Go's `encoding/pem` writes them and nothing else, each signed by the next, the last self-signed, each valid now; `ca`, the first certificate no CA that may sign a CA below it — basic constraints a CA, key usage certificate signing, a path length of none or at least 1; `key`, no PKCS #8 key, not the first certificate's, or neither ECDSA P-256 or P-384 nor RSA of 2,048 bits or more; `expiring`, a certificate of it ending within 60 days, inside the rotation it would start; `verify`, a leaf for a name it permits — the first permitted DNS subtree of its certificates, or a name below it for one with a leading dot — does not verify, by Go's `crypto/x509`, under the intermediate Canyon would mint beneath it against the certificates given: a key usage that excludes serving TLS, or a path length above it, refuses it, whether or not a route names a destination (**amended 2026-11-08**, #2717's review) · `409 {error:"ca_unconstrained", message:"Canyon takes only a sub-CA that is name-constrained to the destinations it adds credentials for, and that allows no IP addresses."}` unless one certificate of the sub-CA but the self-signed root carries critical name constraints that permit at least one DNS subtree, none of them empty, a leading dot aside, and constrain IP addresses — permitted IP ranges, none of them a `/0`, or excluded ranges covering all of IPv4 (`0.0.0.0/0`) and IPv6 (`::/0`): a CA key whose constraints permit an empty subtree, which matches every name, or leave addresses open, in Canyon's hands would let a compromised Canyon sign for every name or address the org's devices trust (**amended 2026-11-08**, #2717's review); Canyon's own intermediate excludes every address whatever the sub-CA allows · `409 {error:"rotating", ends_at}` while a rotation is under way — a root other than the newest has not reached its end, `ends_at` the latest such end: one rotation at a time · `409 {error:"ca_excludes", destinations}` for a sub-CA under which a leaf for a destination a route names would not verify — its own name constraints, or its key usages, refuse it: Canyon mints its intermediate under the sub-CA, constrained to the inject destinations as always (`GET /egress/leaf`, below), and a leaf for each, and verifies each with Go's `crypto/x509` against the certificates given; `destinations` those that fail, sorted · then, in one transaction under the roots' table lock: the new root, its fingerprint the SHA-256 of its certificate's DER — a sub-CA's own, the first — its key sealed under its own vault row as Canyon's own root's is, `("egress", "root", <fingerprint>)`; every other root's end brought to 60 days from now when later; one audit row `egress.ca.rotate`, `detail{root, byo, previous[]}` — the new root's fingerprint, whether it is an admin's sub-CA, and each root it replaces, `{root, ends_at}` — never a key · `200` the admin's read of the setting, its `ca` naming the new root · the key is never read back: no read, log line, error or audit row carries it · the actor's and the session's refusals write one `denied` row · the new root is among the roots every `/run` under via `canyon` carries from then (contracts/executor.md, `egress.ca`) — a sub-CA with the certificates above it — and signs once no env holding a route lacks it (*Interception*, Trust) · **on a schedule** Canyon starts the same rotation itself, as `{}`, the audit row's actor `canyon`, 60 days before the newest root's end while that root is Canyon's own — checked when the server starts serving the policy and every hour after — so a five-year root's 60 days run out as it ends; an admin's sub-CA never rotates by itself: at its end, with no other root live, no root signs, so egress-control serves no leaf and the policy names no route (*Interception*, Trust): fail closed |

**amended 2026-11-14**, #2881's review); a `DELETE` never is · `409 {error:"credential_routes_off", message:"Canyon adds no credentials to apps' calls until an admin turns them on in Settings › Outbound"}` while the host's `credential_routes` is `false` (the egress setting row, above; **added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on), a widening · minimum `owner`: `404` as `PUT`'s · `403 team_required` · `403 owner_required` · `403 {error:"person_required", message:"a person changes how apps call out"}` · `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` · `400 {error:"invalid_destination"}` · `400 {error:"invalid_route", field}`, the body `route_from_secret`, `header` and `placeholder_env` as `PUT`'s, `value` the `field` for a secret `PUT`'s `value` would refuse · `404 {error:"not_found"}` unless `:env` holds a runtime secret named `secret`, a `build` value being none · `409 {error:"provisioned"}` on a `provisioned` row, Canyon's and not the caller's to move · `409 egress_external`, `409 licence_required` (into a destination the env holds no route for, as `PUT`'s), `409 credential_routes_off`, `409 held`, `409 destination_not_allowed` and `409 trust_secret`, in that order, as `PUT`'s: under `run_by` `org` a move would take the secret out of the release's variables and nothing would add it · then, where a `PUT` may wait, `409 {error:"would_wait", reason, message}` unless the route would be live at once — the signing root among the env's trusted roots, via `canyon` and `run_by` `canyon` (*Interception*, Trust) — so a moved key is never stranded: `reason` `host` while via is `off`, `message` `ask an admin to turn on Through Canyon first: apps call out directly here, so Canyon can't add this credential yet`; else `redeploy`, `message` `the version running in <env> doesn't trust Canyon's certificate until its next deploy or promotion, so the secret stays where it is` · `204`, in one transaction: the route replaced whole, the value sealed under its own vault row, `from_secret` true, a new `rev`, and the secret's row gone from the env · `placeholder_env` omitted is the secret's own name, checked as a given one is, so an app that reads that variable still starts, and `null` is none · the app keeps the real key until its next deploy, whose `/run` carries the placeholder in its place · one audit row `egress.route.set` `detail{env, destination, secret}`, the secret's name and the destination, never the value · the actor's and the session's refusals write one `denied` row · **answers once every live egress-control replica holds it**, or names the one behind (*A change waits for the gateway*, below; | internal: Envoy's access-log receiver, gRPC `envoy.service.accesslog.v3.AccessLogService/StreamAccessLogs` | **not under `/api/v1`, no session, no response body**: the server serves it only when `CANYON_EGRESS_RECEIVER_ADDR` is set · **authenticated**: every stream carries the gRPC metadata `authorization: Bearer <CANYON_EGRESS_RECEIVER_TOKEN>` — at least 32 random characters the operator gives the server and Envoy's bootstrap (its ALS cluster's `initial_metadata`), the server refusing to start without one — compared in constant time **before the stream takes a slot**, else `UNAUTHENTICATED`; every other method `UNIMPLEMENTED` · **egress-control holds the token too** (**added 2026-11-08**, #2740): from its environment, on Kubernetes the Secret `canyon-egress`'s `token` as the server and Envoy read it, and serves it Envoy in the interception listener's access log, in the LDS answer over loopback alone, never in a log line, an error, a metric or an SDS answer; without a valid one it serves no interception listener, so a call to a destination with a route finds none and is `503` and no inner request goes unrecorded, and it checks every other call as ever · **bound**: on Docker the address is an IPv4 address and a port, the server's own address on `canyon-egress-ctl`, the one network Envoy shares with it, never `canyon-internal`; the server refuses to start on an unspecified address (`0.0.0.0`), loopback, a name, IPv6, an address of `CANYON_APPS_SUBNET`'s pool, or with `CANYON_APPS_SUBNET` unset; the listener closes at accept every address of that pool, each /24's gateway included, and every IPv6 peer but loopback · **on Kubernetes** (**amended 2026-10-17**, #2286) the address is `0.0.0.0` and a port — deploy/k8s sets `0.0.0.0:7781`, as no manifest knows the pod's address — and nothing else, a specific address, loopback, a name or IPv6 refused, the listener IPv4 alone, and `CANYON_APPS_SUBNET` is not needed: the server's NetworkPolicy admits that port from the pods labelled `app.kubernetes.io/name: canyon-egress` in `canyon` alone, and the token is the Secret `canyon-egress`'s key `token`, which the server and Envoy's pods both read — a static token and that policy, as on Docker a token and a dedicated network · **what it reads** of each HTTP entry: the direct peer's address (never `X-Forwarded-For`, a caller header or the remote address Envoy derives from them), the authority and scheme, whether the method is `CONNECT`, response code, response flags, stream id (at most 64 bytes, else none; a tunnel is its source's and stream's together) whether it is periodic or a tunnel's open entry, whether it carries the tag `canyon.tunnel_open_entry`, and the check's dynamic metadata in namespace `envoy.filters.http.ext_authz` — `app`, `env`, `outcome` and `reason`, which egress-control alone writes (*The check*; **amended 2026-10-19**, #2116); of each stream, the log name Envoy gives in its first message alone, `intercept` the interception listener's and any other `:3128`'s, and of an `intercept` entry alone the filter-state object `canyon.caller` (*Interception*; **added 2026-11-08**, #2740); every other field — path, query, headers, user agent, referer, the upstream's resolved address — is dropped on receipt, before any aggregation, log line or write, and no entry is ever logged · **the destination** (**amended 2026-10-19**, #2116) is the authority as *The check* canonicalises it, and its port whatever it is, so a call refused `port` is a row of the port it named; a dotted IPv4 address is kept as it is, and any other address, a host UTS 46 maps to another name, userinfo, a single label or an over-long name is `invalid`, on 443 · **the caller** (**amended 2026-10-19**, #2116) of an `intercept` entry is its `canyon.caller` alone, `<app>/<env>`, which ext_authz #1 named for the tunnel — never the check's `app` and `env`, nor an address, as its source is Envoy's internal address — and with none, or one that is no `<app>/<env>` string, it is no one's (**added 2026-11-08**, #2740); of any other entry, it is the one the check's metadata names, when it names one, and wins over the address's: the call was decided for that caller, and only egress-control writes it — one with an app id out of contract or a target other than `preview` or `prod` is no one's; a call the check named no caller for (`unknown_caller`, a check that did not answer, layer 1's Envoy, which asks none) is the peer's caller's, asked of the executor (`POST /callers`, executor.md) once per flush, every 10 s, each call bounded at 5 s; every call no app is named for — no one's address, ambiguous, no IPv4 address, unanswered, an executor without the call — is no row and is counted as `unattributed`, once its batch is written, and so is a caller the executor names with an app id out of contract or a target other than `preview` or `prod` · **one row per destination**: a flush's calls are summed in memory per `(app, env, destination, port)` and each key is one upsert in the flush's one transaction — `calls` added, every call whatever its outcome and reason, which an egress request's `refusals` is not (*Egress requests*; **amended 2026-11-08**, #2718), `first_seen_at` the earlier, `last_seen_at` the later, and the last result the later call's, its `last_outcome` the check's `outcome`, else `refused` for an entry Envoy flags `UAEX` (the check refused it, or did not answer) or `NR` (no route: Envoy routes the call nowhere, so the check never sees it and it never leaves, a port other than 80 and 443 among them, which every mode refuses, egress.schema.json's `mode`; **amended 2026-11-08**, #2723: it read `allowed`), else `allowed` (**amended 2026-10-19**, #2116), but an `intercept` entry's `allowed` only when ext_authz #2's `outcome` says so, else `refused`, so the interception listener's own `421`, which asks no check, never makes the name it asked for known, and its `last_protocol` `intercepted` (**added 2026-11-08**, #2740) — so no call is ever placed in a minute, an hour or a day · **a tunnel is one call of two entries** (**amended 2026-11-06**, #2684): Envoy logs it as it opens, once its `CONNECT` is answered `200` (`flush_log_on_tunnel_successfully_established`), so its row is there while it runs. The open entry counts the call and moves `last_seen_at`; it writes the last result only on a row it creates, never over one a row holds, so `last_outcome` and the rest stay the last ended call's. The last entry, a `CONNECT` answered `200` under the tag, counts nothing and writes the last result, as the later entry; with no row, its open entry lost, it writes one of 1 call, and on a row it adds none, so a call whose open entry was lost is one short there. A periodic entry between them, of such a `CONNECT`, moves `last_seen_at` alone, so a tunnel that runs on reads as seen now and not when it opened, for a reader of what preview connects to: it counts nothing, leaves the result and `admitted_at`, and with no row writes one of 1 call. Any other periodic entry writes nothing. Each of the bootstrap's entries carries the tag, so an entry without it is an Envoy from before this logged no open entry, and its tunnel is counted at its last entry as ever; a receiver from before this counts a tunnel twice while the new bootstrap runs, and the row is otherwise the same. A call refused, or with no upstream, is never a tunnel: it has one entry, which counts it. An upgrade that is no `CONNECT` (a `GET` with `Upgrade: CONNECT`, answered `101`) is logged as it opens too: that entry writes nothing and its last entry counts it, so it is one call, and no row while it runs. An open entry past the cap is counted on `over_cap`, an open entry no app is named for on `unattributed`, and the last entry adds nothing to either, though a tunnel's later entries past the cap move `over_cap`'s `to_at`, so it reads as a gap for as long as the tunnel runs (amended 2026-11-06, #2125); a tunnel's later entry past the cap with no `over_cap` row yet, its open entry lost, writes one of 1 call; a flush holds each of its app envs' advisory locks, taken in order, while it admits and writes their rows, so two writers never pass the cap · **bounds**, each turned-away call counted as a gap, never lost unseen: 16 streams at once (past them `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, which Envoy retries; Envoy runs one stream per worker, `--concurrency 8` on Docker, and on Kubernetes two replicas of four with a rollout's third, so reconnects fit — **amended 2026-10-15**, #2097), keepalive pings every minute and an idle connection closed after five, 4 MiB a message (a larger one `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` and a `dropped` gap no one counted), 16 KiB an entry (`dropped`), 65,536 calls waiting for a flush (`dropped`), 4,096 addresses asked per flush, the admin's `caps.destinations` rows (destination, port) per app env, 500 until one is set, read in each flush's transaction — a cap lowered below the rows an env holds deletes none, and every new key is over_cap until it is under it (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2691) — `invalid` not among them (a call to a new one past them is no row and is counted on that app env's `over_cap` gap; `invalid` is on 443, whatever port it named), a row unseen for 30 days deleted and a gap ended 30 days ago with it, a gap that names no app and continues the last one written of its reason and countedness extending that one row, and an app env's `over_cap` one row, extended by each burst past the cap, 16,384 tunnels whose caller is kept between their entries (past them a tunnel is attributed by its last), one unwritten gap per reason · **never silent**: on the way out it stops taking streams, waits for each stream's handler, then flushes what it holds, each `POST /callers` of that flush bounded at 1 s so the write keeps its time; a dropped gap no one counted is its own row beside a counted one; at boot the time since its last flush, past 15 s, is a `receiver_down` gap; while egress is in use — the host's `via` is `canyon`, an app env is in `egress_envs`, or either cannot be read — and no stream is open, it logs so once a minute (**amended 2026-11-08**, #2697: with `via` `off` and no app env under `canyon`, no call goes through Envoy and it logs nothing); `envoy_dropped` is what Envoy lost before it reached the receiver, which egress-control reads from Envoy's admin socket and reports (`POST /egress/dropped`, below; **amended 2026-11-06**, #2374): Envoy's own count of what it dropped, and a row of its own, `calls` `null`, for what no one counted |
| internal: `GET /egress/policy?have=<epoch>:<seq>[&caller=<address>]` | **what egress-control decides with** (design/EGRESS.md §3.2; **added 2026-10-18**, #2104): not under `/api/v1`, no session, on the server's internal listener, which refuses the app pool · **the listener** is `CANYON_EGRESS_POLICY_ADDR`, never the API's (**amended 2026-10-18**, #2113): on Docker the server's IPv4 address on `canyon-egress-ctl` and a port, `7782`, under the receiver's rules (above); on Kubernetes `0.0.0.0:7782`, which the server's NetworkPolicy admits from the gateway's pods alone; unset, none is served — **but on Docker with the receiver on**, where it is the receiver's address and port `7782`, under the same rules (**amended 2026-10-19**, #2119: the bundled compose file, append-only, names it nowhere, and the holder's floor admits that one port by the same default); on Kubernetes it stays explicit, unset none · **authenticated**: on Kubernetes a projected token of egress-control's, audience `canyon-egress-policy`, verified offline against the cluster's service-account key set, over TLS pinned to a certificate the server mints and an install step publishes; on Docker the dedicated `canyon-egress-ctl` network and the listener closing every address of the app pool at accept (`refuseAppNet`); else `401`, no body · **on Kubernetes** (#2113) the token is `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, signed by the cluster's key set, its issuer the cluster's, its audience `canyon-egress-policy`, unexpired, its subject exactly `system:serviceaccount:canyon:canyon-egress` and bound to a pod; the server reads the issuer and key set from the API server with a projected token of its own and trusts a read 10 minutes at most: it reads them again once the last read is that old, or on a key id it does not hold, at most once a minute, and a read that fails keeps the last one until it is 10 minutes old, from then every request `401` until a read answers; the certificate is self-signed P-256 for `canyon-server.canyon.svc`, ten years, its key sealed in the vault, minted at the first start that serves the listener: `canyon-server egress-policy-cert` prints it and the install step publishes it as the ConfigMap `canyon-egress-policy-cert`, key `tls.crt` · **the replica** is who asks: on Kubernetes the pod its token names, on Docker its address on `canyon-egress-ctl`; it asks every 5 s with the version it holds, and each ask is recorded, `setting_admin`'s `replicas` · `200` `egress.schema.json`'s `policy`: the callers, each app env Canyon runs with its mode and allowlist, the platform destinations and the host's public URL — **policy, never secrets**: no credential and no route value, to anyone · **from #2116** (**amended 2026-10-19**) the policy carries `format`, `1`, which a server raises only for a change an older egress-control must not read as it reads `1` — a field added is no such change, as egress-control ignores the fields it does not know (*The check*) — and each env its `known[]`: the names a call of it went through to (`egress_destinations`, the receiver row, whose `admitted_at` only a call whose `outcome` is `allowed` sets — never one refused, `rate_limited`, `not_allowed`, `port` or any, so no app makes a name known by being refused) in the last 30 days, `invalid` and IPv4 addresses left out, the latest admitted first up to the smaller of the admin's `caps.destinations` and 500, the most `known` an older egress-control takes (**amended 2026-11-09**, #2691), then sorted, which the rate limit counts as no new name (*The check*); `known[]` is read at each `200` and moves no version, unlike every other field: it only ever makes a name not new, so one a replica holds that is older is stricter, never looser, and a replica's first ask, `have` empty, always reads it · `304`, no body, **only** when `have` is exactly the current `<epoch>:<seq>`: any other `have` — empty, malformed, an older `seq` or another `epoch` — is `200` · `seq` grows with every change to what the policy says — every row written to a table it is read from, and a `TRUNCATE` of one, in the writing transaction: the org's via and mode, an app env's mode and allowlist, the front door, the envs and the callers (#2113); requests, which it does not carry, move nothing — and the policy is read with its version in one snapshot; `epoch` is 128 random bits the server draws at each start, and again before it answers when its read finds the database's server started again (`pg_postmaster_start_time()`, in the policy's snapshot: a restore or a failover always restarts it) or a `seq` lower than one it answered under that epoch, so a restored database can never bring an old version back under its old epoch: egress-control refuses, whole, a policy that fails the schema's fields it knows or whose `epoch` is the one it holds and whose `seq` is lower, and serves the one it holds (*The check*, What it takes) · **`caller`** — one IPv4 address, else `400 {error:"invalid_caller"}` — is egress-control's single-flight refetch for a source address the policy does not name: the server asks the executor's `POST /callers` for it (executor.md) and answers `200`, the address among the callers, under a new version, when one app env Canyon runs holds it, and the policy it has when none does — on Docker and Kubernetes alike, every caller enters the policy so (#2113); `503 {error:"caller_unresolved"}`, and no policy, when the executor could not be asked — never the policy as if no one held the address: egress-control asks again, and remembers no answer it did not get (**amended 2026-10-19**, #2119) · **a caller's lifetime**: every 15 s the server asks `POST /callers` again for every caller; one the executor names otherwise or not at all leaves at once, under a new version; one unconfirmed for 30 s, at the answer's own snapshot, is in no answer — an answer that finds one drops it, under a new version, first — and an env's callers go with the env at once; egress-control forgets every caller once its policy is more than 30 s old (*The check*): 30 + 30 s, no caller keeps another app's allowlist more than a minute past the executor last confirming it · **the envs** are the app envs Canyon runs under via `canyon`: each named before its `/run` and by its deploy row under via `canyon`, dropped, its callers with it, when that row goes, is handed to the org or runs under via `off`, and when the first `/run` that named it fails · **convergence** (#2113): a `/run` under via `canyon` names its env, then waits, at most 30 s, until every live replica — one that asked within 30 s, as long as one cut off from the server still serves the policy it holds (**amended 2026-11-12**, #2789) — reports a `have` of the current epoch and a `seq` at least the current one once the env is named; past it the run fails closed, nothing started: *the egress gateway has not taken this version (<replica>)*; with no live replica — read so, never on an error — there is nothing to wait for; a replica holding another epoch's version, or none, is behind; an error reading the version or the replicas, or the run's context ending, fails the run before `/run` · **from layer 3** (**added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on, which makes it required: until then no route exists) each env carries `routes[]`, `policy_route`, sorted by destination: `{destination, header, scheme, rev}`, no value — `rev` drawn for each write of a route, a `PUT` or a from-secret, from one sequence over every route write of the host, so it never repeats for an app env and destination, a route deleted and set again included — and a route written moves `seq`, as a row of any table the policy is read from does · an env's `routes[]` names its routes only while the signing root is among the env's trusted roots, else `[]` (*Interception*, Trust) · **the signing root** (**added 2026-11-08**, #2704): the policy carries `signing_root`, the fingerprint of the root signing the current leaves (*Interception*, Trust), `null` while none is live — every leaf egress-control serves must chain to it, and it fetches again a leaf under another (`GET /egress/leaf`, below) · the signing root can change with no row written that moves `seq` — a root passing its end, its certificate's or 60 days after the rotation that replaced it began, or a deploy row with no roots written for an env holding a route — so `egress_policy` keeps the signing root the last answer named, and an answer whose read finds another moves `seq` first, records it and reads again: no replica is told `304` for a version under another signing root · **on Kubernetes, from layer 3** (**added 2026-11-09**, #2142; *The check*, a pod's token) the policy carries `kubernetes {issuer, keys}`, the cluster's service-account issuer and key set as the server last read them for egress-control's own token (above, the same read, trusted 10 minutes), else `issuer` `null` and `keys` `[]`, so no pod's token verifies; a key set changed or forgotten moves `seq`, as a signing root does: `egress_policy` keeps the fingerprint of the key set the last answer named · each env carries `account`, `canyon-app-<app>-<env>`, the executor's name for its ServiceAccount, from before its first `/run`, so the check knows it before the env's new pod first calls out · and `token_only` once the server marks it: set once a `/run` of it with `egress` succeeded and its answer said `token_callers` and `previous_gone` both `true` (contracts/executor.md, The pod's token), never by a pod, a `POST /callers` answer, an older executor's answer or a failed `/run`; it moves `seq`, and goes with the env · on Docker the policy carries none of the three · **its `format`** (#2142's review) is `2` whenever it carries `kubernetes`, else `1`: an egress-control from before #2142 reads `1` alone, ignoring `token_only`, so it refuses a Kubernetes policy whole and fails closed rather than name a token_only env by its pods' address and intercept its call; one from #2142 on refuses a Kubernetes policy of `format` `1` (*The check*) |
