a group at owner is not a person), `production` and `preview` those with something deployed there (`running` reads them so: an environment with a `url`), `passed` those whose `gate_state` is `clean`, `deploys_7d` their deploys in the seven days before the read — the `deploy preview` and `deploy prod` audit rows, and on an environment the org runs the `platform reported deployed` ones — and `production_7d` those to `prod` among them · `apps[]` is **one page**, each row the project of row 47 plus `last_run` (the newest gate run's id, `null` when the project has none), `warnings` (that run's verdict's advisory count, `0` before its verdict and with no run, so a passed app with advisories never reads as a clean one, **amended 2026-10-04**, #1522) and its environments' `state` (row 47), and **`needs[]`** (*What needs a person*; **added 2026-11-08**, #2724), a responder's reduced row its `held` need alone — `latest` is row 47's, on every project read; below `contributor` the reduced row, with no `last_run` or `warnings` — sorted by `sort` ∈ `risk \| changed \| name`, `risk` when absent (else `400 {error:"invalid_sort"}`): `risk` the rows read whole that carry needs first, by their first need's rank (**amended 2026-11-08**, #2724: a refused call, a production down or an approval asked read as a passed app), then by `latest.stage` in the risk order `blocked`, `failed`, `approved`, `awaiting_review`/`created`, `reviewing`/`building`, `passed`, `preview`, `production`, then by staleness (`latest.since` ascending, the oldest change first inside a bucket), then by `id`; `changed` by `latest.since` descending, the newest change first, then `id`; `name` by `name`, compared case-insensitively, then `id`, every row among the others since a reduced row carries its name; under `risk` and `changed` the reduced rows come after every other row, by `id` alone, so a row's place says nothing its fields do not (**amended 2026-10-05**, `changed` and `name`) — **the server sorts**: a client that re-sorts sorts one page · `total` is how many rows the filters and `scope` select, across every page, the same on each page of one walk — what "1–50 of 213" reads · `cursor` is opaque and server-issued — a client passes back exactly what it was handed and parses nothing; inside, it is the sort it was issued for and the last row's key under it — (the first need's rank, stage rank, `since`, `id`) for `risk`, (`since`, `id`) for `changed`, (`name`, `id`) for `name`, a reduced row keyed by `id` alone after the rest under the first two — and a cursor issued for another sort is `400 {error:"invalid_cursor"}`, so **a page is not a snapshot**: across a fleet that does not change between pages no row repeats and none vanishes, and a fleet that changes underneath may repeat or skip one row at a boundary — the reader tolerates both · `limit` defaults to 50; a non-integer or a value below 1 is `400 {error:"invalid_limit"}`, above 200 it is clamped to 200 · `next` is the cursor for the page after this one, `null` on the last page · **`page` is a page by its number** (**amended 2026-10-05**, the fleet's pager: "Page 4 of 26" and a jump to any page need a page a number names, which a cursor cannot): 1-based, the rows at offset (`page` − 1) × `limit` under the same sort and filters, so `total` and `limit` say how many pages there are — ⌈`total` ÷ `limit`⌉, and 1 when `total` is 0; a page past the last is the last page, never an error, and the response's `page` is the one served, so a page that emptied under a poll reads as the last one; `page` is `1` when neither `page` nor `cursor` is given and `null` on a read by `cursor`; a non-integer or a value below 1 is `400 {error:"invalid_page"}`, and so is `page` with `cursor` — a read pages one way; like a cursor's, a numbered page is not a snapshot · the filters narrow `apps[]` only and combine; `tile`, `stage`, `running`, `stack` and `access` match only rows the caller is `contributor` or above on, `needs_you` the rows carrying needs, `q` and `owner` every row: `tile` ∈ `secrets_exposed \| blocked \| approved \| never_reviewed \| unowned \| failed \| refused` — exactly the apps that tile counts, so the screen never filters a page client-side; `scope=team` — "Your apps" — the apps the caller is `contributor` or above on by their own grants, an admin's grants too, where an admin otherwise reads every row (else `400 {error:"invalid_scope"}`) · `owner` an **owner's subject or address matched exactly, never a display name** (two people may share a name; the subject is the identity) · `stage` one of the ten keys of *The package's stage* · `running` ∈ `production \| preview \| nothing` — what the app runs, not where its latest package got to: `production` every app with a deployed `envs.prod` (one with a `url`), `preview` likewise with `envs.preview` (one running both matches either), `nothing` neither — an environment waiting on its first release runs nothing · `running` reads what is deployed, never `state`: what the executor answers changes between two pages, and a filter pages the same rows on every read · `stack` and `q` are **literal case-insensitive substrings** — `stack` of `stack`, `q` of `id` **or** `name` (the row carries both and a reader types whichever they know) — never a SQL pattern and never a regex, so `%`, `_` and `.*` match themselves; each is at most 64 characters · `needs_you=true` is the server-computed queue: the rows carrying at least one need (*What needs a person*; **amended 2026-11-08**, #2724: it was three tiles' facts, an open patch approval and a refused egress request, so an approval asked, an environment down or a request for access needed nobody), a responder's held reduced row among them, whose hold they read; only the exact `true` turns it on · `access` ∈ `private \| shared \| everyone` — `everyone` an app shared with everyone, `shared` one shared with a person or a group and not with everyone, `private` neither (the row's `access`) · `banner` ∈ `none \| loopback \| exposed` — `none` on an authenticated host, the other two **derived from the listener and the peer address, never asserted**; the two strings they select are D5's and stay in the client · behind a port mapping the container's own listener and peer say nothing about who reaches the host's port, so under `demo` with `CANYON_PUBLISHED_ADDR` set — the address the host publishes the listener on, `host:port` as `CANYON_ADDR` is, a value that does not parse failing the boot; the compose file sets it beside the port it publishes, and `make demo-check` proves the two equal against `docker port` — the banner is `loopback`, whatever the peer: a `CANYON_PUBLISHED_ADDR` that is not a loopback IP fails the boot under `demo` (**amended 2026-10-09**, #1892: the demo published on `127.0.0.1` read `exposed`; **amended 2026-10-26**, #2449: published beyond loopback, Docker hands a remote client the gateway's address, which the listener serves) · `400 {error:"invalid_tile" \| "invalid_stage" \| "invalid_running" \| "invalid_access" \| "invalid_scope" \| "invalid_sort" \| "invalid_limit" \| "invalid_page" \| "invalid_cursor" \| "invalid_q" \| "invalid_stack"}` — the last two only for over-length; an `owner`, `stack` or `q` that matches nothing is not an error, it is a page with no rows |
| `GET /api/v1/timeline?after=&limit=` | `{rows[], next}` — the org timeline rows, polled (seam 2) · `after` and `limit` as on the run timeline · a member is answered the rows naming a project (`app.updated`, `run.transition`) only for the apps they are `contributor` or above on, and `tiles.updated` carries the counts the caller's fleet read answers, never the whole fleet's |
| `GET /api/v1/policies` · `PUT /api/v1/policies/:rule {state?, production?, grace_days?, run?, settings?}` | `GET` → `200 {policies: [{id, name, state, production?, grace_days?, scheduled?: {state, effective_at}, scheduled_production?: {state, effective_at}, set_by?, set_at?, additions?}]}` — every rule the snapshot carries, in the snapshot's order; `state` is the rule's effective state now (`blocking` \| `advisory` \| `off`) before preview — a go-live rule's before production, its only gate — and `production` its state before production, present only when the rule is placed there (snapshot.md, *Two gates*); `scheduled` is the newest edit naming the gate before preview — a go-live rule's only gate — while its grace has not yet elapsed, and `scheduled_production` the newest naming the gate before production while its grace has not, each present only when that gate has one, so neither gate's pending change hides the other's, `set_by`/`set_at` the actor and instant of the newest edit, absent for a rule never edited · `additions` the apps' additions to the rule (the next row), `[{project, state?, production?, set_by, set_at}]` by project, those of the apps the caller is `contributor` or above on (an admin's: every app's; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *The gate pipeline*), absent when there are none: the one read the Pipeline tab and the app page draw them from · `PUT` without `?apply=true` → `200 {would_block: [{project, findings}]}`, the projects whose latest verdict holds findings under this rule that would block at the target state — on `platform.known-vuln`, which no verdict holds, the projects whose image in preview its receipt row would hold at `blocking`, `findings` the packages the row counts, read as *The promote receipt* reads it (amended 2026-10-17, #2264); on `prod.egress-allowed`, which no verdict holds either, the live projects whose preview its receipt row would hold, read as *The promote receipt* reads it: at `blocking` each of them, below it those holding a `blocking` addition, which the org's state can't clear, `findings` the destinations the row names, `0` when it could not check; never one whose row is neutral, the org running both, nor one with nothing in preview, whose receipt reads no row, whose rollback the row never holds, and which a version in production or its history changes nothing of (amended 2026-11-06, #2125); on `deps.known-vuln` placed `blocking` before production, beside its verdicts' count, each project whose built app in preview its row would hold (*The promote receipt*, `deps.known-vuln`'s row reads the built app too), `findings` the more of that count and the packages the row counts, `0` when it could not be checked (#2355) — and nothing is written · `PUT ?apply=true` → `200 {policy, would_block}` with the same shape: one edit row, effective now or after `grace_days` (an integer 0–365; absent is 0), and one audit row `policy.set` with `detail {rule, name, before, after, grace_days?}`, `name` the rule's name as the snapshot carries it at the edit, so the trail reads the rule as the Guardrails page does (a row written before this carries none and is read by its `rule`, amended 2026-10-04, #1545), `before` and `after` the rule's `state`, and `production: {before, after}` beside them when its state before production changes, `off` where it is not placed · **`state` sets before preview — on a go-live rule before production, its only gate — and `production` sets before production, `off` taking the rule out of that gate; an edit names the gates it sets** · rule states come from the org's policy edits, per gate: a rule's effective state in a gate is its newest edit naming that gate — newest by when it was set, never by when it would start, so an edit scheduled with a grace and then superseded by a later decision never wakes — whose start has passed, and a change bites the next run — a stored verdict keeps the `blocks` it was derived with (finding.md) · the four `prod.*` rules are editable like any other (the owner, 2026-09-22) · `400 {error:"invalid_state"}` for a state outside the three, `400 {error:"invalid_grace_days"}`, `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a rule the snapshot does not carry — except `sarif.<tool>` with `<tool>` 1 to 40 characters already in normalised form (finding.md: `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, so no leading, trailing or doubled `-`; anything else is the `404`), which **adds that tool** (`sarif.gitleaks`, `sarif.trivy`, `sarif.opengrep`, `sarif.eslint` and `sarif.checkov` need no adding: every snapshot carries them, snapshot.md, and a PUT edits them as any rule): the apply registers the rule (snapshot.md: producer `sarif`, installs `off`) and writes the edit to the state given, in one transaction, one `policy.set` row with `before` `off`; without `?apply=true` it answers `would_block: []` and registers nothing; turning a tool `off` is how it is removed, and its reports stay · PUT is `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, and `403 {error:"person_required"}` for an `agent` or `canyon` actor (design/PERMISSIONS.md: no CLI verb changes the pipeline), before anything is written · **while the org's `prod_approval` is on** (the preferences row; amended 2026-10-10, #1966, the owner) a session that is not a browser sign-in's — the CLI's — is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message}` on every PUT, preview or apply, before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row and nothing written: otherwise an admin's agent could turn a blocking rule off and promote past it; `message` is `While production is approved in Canyon's dashboard, policies are changed there too: <host>/#/guardrails/policies`, `<host>` the host's public URL; off, or with sign-in off, nothing changes · with `production`, `would_block` is the projects whose `promote_run` (the status row) holds findings under this rule that would block before production at the target, and at `blocking` every project whose `promote_run` did not have the rule on, `findings` `0`: its receipt has nothing of the rule to read and holds the promotion (*The promote receipt*, `check it again`) · `production` on a go-live rule (snapshot.md: never placed before preview, its `state` is before production) is `409 {error:"production_not_offered", message:"The go-live rules are checked before production only: set their state"}` · an apply that leaves both gates as they are — each target already the rule's effective state there, in force now, with no edit scheduled ahead of it to withdraw — writes no edit and no audit row · a change to the effective states moves the snapshot's `version` and `hash` (snapshot.md), exactly once per change, which is what the suppressed no-op keeps true · a body with none of `state`, `production`, `run` and `settings` is `400 {error:"invalid_state"}`, as is a `production` outside the three, and `grace_days` beside `run` or `settings` without a `state` or `production` is `400 {error:"invalid_grace_days"}` · **a custom rule** (`custom.<slug>`, snapshot.md) takes `state` and `grace_days` like any rule; setting one `advisory` or `blocking` on a host with no model configured — the preview and the apply alike, a scheduled edit too — is `409 {error:"no_model", message:"No model is configured on this Canyon host, so a custom rule can't run. Ask whoever runs it to set one up."}` before anything is written · `production` `advisory` or `blocking`, or `state` `blocking` (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1555: no review would check it), on a rule an agent reviews (snapshot.md: producer `llm-review` — the AI review's rules, and the custom rules, which keep their own message) on such a host is `409 {error:"no_model", message:"No model is configured on this Canyon host, so the AI review can't run. Ask whoever runs it to set one up."}`, the preview and the apply alike, before anything is written · **refusal order**, after the member's `403`, then `person_required`, then `dashboard_required`: a rule the snapshot does not carry (other than `sarif.<tool>`) is `404` before any `409` (`run_not_offered`, `production_not_offered`) · **Canyon-run tools** (finding.md, *Canyon-run tools*): on `sarif.trivy`, `sarif.opengrep`, `sarif.eslint`, `sarif.grype` and `sarif.checkov` the PUT also takes `run` (a boolean: Canyon runs this tool on this host) and `settings`, the tool's allow-list, replaced whole (`{}` clears it) — trivy `{scanners?, skip_paths?}`, `scanners` a subset of `vuln \| misconfig \| secret`; opengrep `{rules?: {repo, commit, paths?}, skip_paths?}`, `repo` an `https://` URL, `commit` a full 40-hex sha and `paths` at most 64 distinct directories of the repo in `skip_paths`' form, whose rules files alone are read (finding.md, *The org's rules repo*), which the server fetches (finding.md, *Canyon-run tools*: public addresses only, no redirects, 60 s and 50 MiB) — the vendored sast-rules run whether or not it is set; eslint and grype `{skip_paths?}`; checkov `{frameworks?, skip_checks?, skip_paths?}`; `skip_paths` paths of the package, never absolute, never through `..` and never starting with `-` · both take effect at once, and a PUT carrying no `state` answers `would_block: []` · `GET`'s row, the apply's `policy` and the snapshot's rule carry `run: true` and non-empty `settings` only when set, so each has one spelling · the apply's `policy.set` detail carries `run: {before, after}` and `settings: {before, after}` when each changes, a change moves the snapshot's hash, and setting what is already set writes no row · on a tool not yet added, `run` and `settings` ride with the `state` that adds it; without one it is `400 {error:"invalid_state"}` · **`platform.known-vuln` takes `settings` and no `run`** (design/PATCHING.md §3.4; added 2026-11-03, #2077): `{source}`, the imported tool whose image reports the rule adds to Canyon's own matches, never replacing them (finding.md, *An imported image result*) — `trivy`, which is stored as no settings at all so it has one spelling, or the name of a tool added to Canyon that Canyon does not run on trees, else **`400 {error:"invalid_source", message:"Canyon doesn't know <tool> yet: add it under Guardrails first, then pick it here."}`**; a source that is no tool's name is `400 invalid_settings` · `run` or `settings` on any other rule — `sarif.gitleaks`, which runs in process while it is on, among them — and `run` on `platform.known-vuln`, is `409 {error:"run_not_offered", message:"Run and settings are offered on trivy, opengrep, eslint, grype and checkov only, and settings on platform.known-vuln"}`; a key outside the tool's allow-list, or a value its shape refuses, is `400 {error:"invalid_settings", message}` naming the first · a tool with `run` on that this host's executor has no image for blocks the run it is asked in, as a scan that did not finish (finding.md) · the samples are `contracts/testdata/policies/meridian.json`, `scanner_apply`, `production_apply`, `production_scheduled` and `production_no_model` among them |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/policies/:rule {state?, production?}` · `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id/policies/:rule` | **an app's additions** (design/EXPERIENCE.md D14; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *The gate pipeline*): the app's owners' and admins' · PUT replaces the app's addition to the rule whole — `state` the app's state before preview, `production` before production (on a go-live rule `state` is before production, as in the snapshot) — and each field present must be stricter than the org's effective state in that gate now; the project's runs use its effective snapshot (snapshot.md) · without `?apply=true` → `200 {would_block}`, the policies row's shape: this project when its `promote_run` holds findings under the rule that the addition would block in a gate it names (on `platform.known-vuln`, when its image in preview would be held, and on `prod.egress-allowed`, when the addition is `blocking` and the row would hold its preview, each as the policies row reads it), else `[]`, an addition replacing the one the app holds, and nothing is written · `?apply=true` → `200 {policy, would_block}`, `policy` the rule's `GET /policies` row as the caller reads it, the addition on its `additions`; one audit row `app_policy.set`, `project` the app, `detail {rule, state?: {before, after}, production?: {before, after}}`, each the app's state in force in that gate, present for each gate the addition changes · an addition sets states only: whether Canyon runs a tool (`run`, `settings`) stays the org's, so an addition to a tool the org does not run produces nothing · DELETE → `204`, the whole addition removed, one audit row `app_policy.remove` `detail {rule}`; `404 {error:"not_found"}` when the app has no addition to the rule · **refusal order**, nothing written by any: `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted project · `403 {error:"owner_required"}` (*App teams*) · `403 {error:"person_required"}` for an `agent` or `canyon` actor · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a rule the snapshot does not carry · `400 {error:"invalid_state"}` for a body with neither field, a state outside the three, or a key other than these and `grace_days` (`run`, `settings`) · `400 {error:"invalid_grace_days"}` for any `grace_days` — an addition takes effect at once · `409 {error:"production_not_offered", message}` as on the policies row · **`400 {error:"not_stricter", message:"An app's checks can only add to the org's, never weaken them."}`** when a field is not stricter than the org's state in its gate · `409 {error:"no_model", message}` as on the policies row, on a host with no model, for `state` `blocking` or a `production` other than `off` on a rule an agent reviews (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1555) · the samples are `app_policy_put` and `not_stricter` in `contracts/testdata/policies/meridian.json` |
| `GET /api/v1/custom-rules` · `PUT /api/v1/custom-rules/:slug {name, instructions, check?, playbook?}` | **custom rules** (snapshot.md): `GET` → `200 {custom_rules: [{rule, name, instructions, check, playbook, version, set_by, set_at}]}`, every custom rule the host has, by `rule`, readable by every member; `check` is the version's, `{by, expr?, says?, facts_version}` as snapshot.md, *Custom rules*, defines it (amended 2026-11-04, #2639), and the dashboard reads `by` and `says` as `Checked by code: <says>` or `Checked by AI` · `PUT` creates `custom.<slug>` at `version` 1 — installed `off` (its state is the policies row's) — or saves its next version, and answers `200 {custom_rule}` in the same shape; one audit row `custom_rule.set` with `detail {rule, version, name: {before?, after}, instructions: {before?, after}, check: {by, says?}, playbook?: {before?, after}}`, `before` absent at version 1, each text whole, `playbook` there only when it changed · **how the version is checked is decided in the request, once**: `check` is `"code"` (the default) or `"ai"`. With `"ai"` the version is checked by AI, `{by: "ai", facts_version}`, and no model is called. With `"code"` the host's model is asked whether the facts decide the rule (gate/custom's translation, the rule's text sent as data) and Canyon takes its answer as code only when the expression compiles over the facts, returns a bool, fits their bounds and, evaluated once on facts at their bounds, finishes within 1 second and its estimate and allocation budget; anything else — the model's `ai`, an answer outside those, no model on the host, or the model failing — saves `{by: "ai"}`, never refuses the save, and the answer then carries `why`, beside `custom_rule`: why the AI review checks the rule though code was asked for, in plain words, at most 280 code points, no control character and no secret gitleaks finds — the model's own reason when it answered that the AI should check the rule, else Canyon's (no model on the host, the model not answering, or a check Canyon could not use). It is said whether or not a version was written, and never stored. The model call and that evaluation hold the request: a save can take seconds, so the dashboard shows it saving. The evaluation's allocation is the process's, so a busy host can turn a rule to the AI that a quiet one would check by code: that fails toward the AI review, never toward a pass · **`playbook`** (**added 2026-11-20**, design/specs/mcp.md §3.4): the slug of the org's playbook the rule points at (the playbooks row, below), or `null` for none, kept per version: absent or `null` in a PUT is none, and a slug no playbook that is not removed has is `400 invalid_custom_rule`, `message` `the playbook is one of the org's playbooks, by its slug`; a playbook removed later leaves each version's `playbook` as it was, and the snapshot carries it only while the playbook stands (snapshot.md, *Custom rules*) · a PUT that changes nothing writes no version and no row: the same name, instructions and playbook, and `check` absent or naming the current version's `by` — so `"code"` on a rule the model put to the AI asks it again, and writes a version only if the answer differs · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, before anything is written · `403 {error:"person_required"}` for an `agent` or `canyon` actor, before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row and nothing written, as the policies row (amended 2026-10-10, #1972: a custom rule's instructions change the pipeline as a policy does, and no CLI verb changes the pipeline) · **while the org's `prod_approval` is on** a session that is not a browser sign-in's is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message}` before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row and nothing written, as the policies row (amended 2026-10-10, #1966): a custom rule's instructions are what a blocking rule checks · `400 {error:"invalid_custom_rule", message}` naming the first problem: a slug outside snapshot.md's, a name outside 1–80 code points or with a control character, instructions outside 1–2,000 code points or with a control character other than a newline, or a name or instructions carrying a secret gitleaks finds (a snapshot is journaled and sent to a model, and a name reaches builders' agents through the skill manifest) · `409 {error:"too_many_rules", message:"A Canyon host takes 20 custom rules."}` for a 21st · there is no delete: Off retires a rule |
| `GET /api/v1/playbooks` · `GET /api/v1/playbooks/:slug` · `PUT /api/v1/playbooks/:slug {name, kind, when, body}` · `DELETE /api/v1/playbooks/:slug` · `GET /api/v1/playbooks/:slug/versions` · `POST /api/v1/playbooks/:slug/restore {version}` | **the org's playbooks** (design/specs/mcp.md §3; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Playbooks*; **added 2026-11-20**): how the org builds, its skills and architecture notes, which agents read and Canyon never checks. Free, not Canyon Enterprise. **No review reads one**: the AI review is never sent a playbook, and no playbook's text reaches a rule's evaluation; a custom rule only points at one (the custom-rules row, above) · **a playbook** is `{slug, name, kind, when, body, version, updated_by, updated_at}`: `slug` 1 to 40 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, beginning with a letter; `name` 1 to 80 code points, no control character; `kind` `skill` or `note`; `when`, one line on when to read it, 1 to 200 code points, no control character; `body` Markdown, 1 to 65,536 bytes of UTF-8, no control character but a newline and a tab; `version` from 1, one more at each save; `updated_by` and `updated_at` the newest version's actor and instant · at most **50** that are not removed · `GET /api/v1/playbooks` → `200 {playbooks: [{slug, name, kind, when, version, updated_by, updated_at}]}`, every playbook not removed, by `slug` bytewise, never a body · `GET …/:slug` → `200 {playbook}`, its newest version, body and all; `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a slug that names none, or one removed · both reads are **every signed-in member's**, as the rules are · `PUT …/:slug` saves the next version whole, the first at `version` 1, and answers `200 {playbook}`; one audit row `set playbook`, `detail{slug, version, diff}`, `diff` each field that changed: `name`, `kind` and `when` as `{before?, after}`, `before` absent at version 1, and `body` as a unified diff of the body, before to after (headers `--- version <n>` and `+++ version <n+1>`, the first version's from an empty body, `version 0`); a PUT that changes nothing writes no version and no row, and answers `200 {playbook}` as it is · `400 {error:"invalid_playbook", message}` naming the first field out of shape, in D5's words; and when Canyon's secret scanner (gitleaks, finding.md's live key) flags anything in the name, `when` or body, `message` D5's `A playbook may carry no secret: agents read it, and a model reads what they read.`, nothing saved: a playbook is org-written text handed to a model, held as a custom rule's instructions are · `409 {error:"too_many_playbooks", message:"Canyon keeps 50 playbooks. Remove one to add another."}` for a 51st · `DELETE …/:slug` → `204`, one audit row `remove playbook`, `detail{slug, version}`, `version` the newest; its versions are kept, readable to admins, and the slug may be saved again, from the next version; `404 {error:"not_found"}` for one absent or removed · `GET …/:slug/versions` → `200 {slug, removed, versions: [{version, name, kind, when, body, set_by, set_at}]}`, every version kept, newest first, `removed` whether the playbook is removed now; `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a slug with no version; **admin** is its minimum: `403 admin_required` for anyone else · `POST …/:slug/restore {version}` saves that version's name, kind, `when` and body as the next version, a removed playbook's too, which then stands again: `200 {playbook}`, one audit row `restore playbook`, `detail{slug, version, from}`, `from` the version restored; `400 {error:"bad_request"}` unless the body is `{version}` with an integer from 1; `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a version the slug never had; restoring a playbook that stands to the text its newest version holds writes nothing and answers it; the same `invalid_playbook` and `too_many_playbooks` refusals as a PUT · **PUT, DELETE and restore are an admin's, a person's, from a browser**: `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member; `403 {error:"person_required", message:"only a person can change a playbook"}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor; on a host with sign-in `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's; each before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row and nothing written · shapes: `playbooks`, `playbook`, `playbook_put`, `playbook_restore` and `playbook_versions` in guardrails.schema.json; the samples are in `contracts/testdata/guardrails/preferences.json` |
| `POST /api/v1/custom-rules/:slug/dry-run` | **a dry run of a custom rule checked by code** on the org's apps (added 2026-11-04, #2640; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *The gate pipeline*): the rule's current version evaluated on each app as a review would evaluate it, nothing written and no model called → `200 {rule, version, apps: [{project, result, why?}]}`, every app of the host by `project`, deleting ones aside, `result` `pass`, `fail` or `not_checked`, and `why` beside `not_checked` alone, in plain words. Each app is read at its latest reviewed package — the package of its newest verdict that is no pull request's, check's, rollback review's or patch run's, as `would_block` reads it — with the facts its review reads (finding.md, *A custom rule checked by code*: the tree, the app's secrets by name as they stand now, and trivy's packages, asked of the executor only while the rule reads them and `sarif.trivy` is on with `run`), and judged as a review judges: could not be checked is `not_checked` with the review's own reason. **Bounded, never a pass past a bound**: at most 50 apps are evaluated, the 50 reviewed most recently, at most 4 at once, and the run ends 50 seconds after it starts, so its answer comes within a front door's common 60-second read timeout (amended 2026-11-04, #2657); an app past the 50 is `not_checked` `A dry run checks at most 50 apps.`, one not finished by then `not_checked` `The dry run ran out of time.`, one with no reviewed package `not_checked` `It has no review yet.`, and one whose review Canyon could not read again (its repository, its policy) `not_checked` `Canyon could not read the app's latest review.` · **one at a time on the host**: a dry run holds a lock while it runs, whichever server answers — one per Canyon, named for its schema, so two sharing a Postgres never hold each other's, and kept against a managed Postgres's idle-in-transaction timeout — and a second asked meanwhile is `409 {error:"dry_run_running", message:"Another dry run is running on this Canyon. Try again when it ends."}` at once, never queued: each fans out over every app · the dashboard asks for it after a save that leaves the rule checked by code, and shows `Would pass`, `Would fail` or `Not checked: <why>` beside each app · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for a slug the host has no rule under · `409 {error:"checked_by_ai", message:"This rule is checked by AI. A dry run checks a rule checked by code."}` for a version checked by AI · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, before anything is read: a dry run reads every app, and only an admin sees every app (an agent under an admin's session acts as that admin, design/AUTH.md §4.4) |
| `GET /api/v1/scanners/data` | — | **how old the host's vulnerability databases are** (added 2026-11-09, #2743; design/ARCHITECTURE.md, *Scanner data*): `200 {databases: [{tool, built_at?, stale_at?}]}`, the executor's `GET /scandb` as it answered (executor.md), trivy's, grype's and trivy's Java database (`trivy-java`) in tool order, `built_at` and `stale_at` together or neither, `stale_at` when a scan on it stops running and the review reads `could not check` (finding.md); `[]` from an executor before that call or a host with none. It reads only: it refreshes nothing. **Admin** is the minimum level (design/PERMISSIONS.md, Settings): anyone below it is answered `403 {error:"admin_required", message}`, as every admin-only route is. `502 {error:"scanner_data_unavailable", message}` when the executor's answer can't be read. `canyon status` (design/specs/cli.md §6.1) and Settings › App hosting read it for an admin's warning a day before a database turns stale (D5) |
| `GET /api/v1/scanners/opengrep/credential` · `PUT` · `DELETE` | PUT `{repo, username, token}` — `repo` as the policies row's `rules.repo` shape, `username` and `token` each 1–256 printable ASCII characters | **the credential the server reads a private Opengrep rules repo with** (finding.md, *Canyon-run tools*): sealed in the vault, never answered, logged or shown, and sent as HTTP Basic only when fetching the repo it was set for, as finding.md compares them · `GET` → `200 {credential: {repo, set_at, set_by} \| null}`, never the username or token, so the Scanners screen can say one is set · `PUT` → `204`: one credential, replaced whole · `DELETE` → `204` whether or not one was set · a policies apply that changes or clears `sarif.opengrep`'s `rules.repo` removes the credential in its transaction, so **the order is the repo first, then its credential**: a credential set before the repo is pointed at it goes with that change, and the next fetch says the repo asks for a credential and none is set for it (finding.md; **amended 2026-10-04**, #1121) · one audit row per change, `action` `scanner.credential.set` or `scanner.credential.remove`, `detail {tool: "opengrep", repo, username}` on set and `{tool: "opengrep", repo}` on remove, never the token · `GET` for any signed-in member, as `GET /api/v1/policies`; `PUT` and `DELETE` admin only: `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, before the body is read · `400 {error:"invalid_credential"}` for a body without all three or any outside its shape, nothing written |
| `GET /api/v1/front-door` · `PUT /api/v1/front-door` | **how people reach apps on a Kubernetes host** (contracts/executor.md, *On Kubernetes*): one front door per host, sent on every `/run` there · `GET` for any signed-in member, as `GET /api/v1/policies`; `PUT` admin only, `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, before the body is read · `GET` → `200 {runtime, default_domain, front_door, warning, grants_note, egress_note, discovery_note, hand_off_needs, probe_note}` — `runtime` ∈ `docker \| kubernetes`, the server's `CANYON_RUNTIME` (unset is `docker`): on `docker` people reach apps through Canyon's own sign-in (*App surfaces*) and nothing here applies · `default_domain` the configured public URL's host, without its port · `front_door` `null` until one is saved, then `{choice, gateway?, issuer?, audience?, jwks_url?, cookie?, alb_arn?, signin?, domain, form, set_by, set_at, probed, enforcing}` — `/run`'s `front_door` field for field, `signin` as `{issuer, client_id}` and **never its secret**, `set_by` the actor triple, `set_at` when, `probed` whether the save that stored it passed the probe (below; a door stored before the field is `true` on a host that never saved a platform webhook, else `false` — a door saved under `org` was never probed, and Canyon cannot tell which it is, so an admin saves it once more; **amended 2026-10-23**, #2438), `enforcing` whether the save that stored it found NetworkPolicy enforced, whatever the certificate — nothing builds behind a door that is not, under either `run_by` (below; a door stored before the field is `false`: nothing records which executor probed it, so whether its probe caught a new pod let out before its policy is unknown, and an admin saves it once more; **amended 2026-10-26**, #2507) · `warning` the probe's `warning` (contracts/executor.md, `/probe`) from the save that stored `front_door`, `""` when it had none or nothing is stored: the save went through, and the dashboard's **Settings › App hosting** and `canyon status` show it as an amber line beside the front door, pointing at deploy/k8s/README.md's *Host firewall* — `canyon status` to an admin alone, or to anyone where sign-in is off, as an admin is the one who acts on it with the cluster's operator (amended 2026-10-07, #1803) · `grants_note` is whether a grant to a person applies through the stored front door (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Grants*; amended 2026-10-17, #2299): D5's other-issuer line when its sign-in's issuer — choice 1 `oidc`'s `issuer`, choice 2's `signin.issuer` — is not `CANYON_OIDC_ISSUER`, compared exactly, as the sidecar compares a token's; D5's `alb` line for every `alb` front door, whose issuer Canyon learns only from a request the load balancer signs; `""` otherwise, with none stored, and on a host with sign-in off, where every grant's entry matches by address; Settings › App hosting shows it beside the front door as an amber line, and it never refuses the save · `egress_note` is D5's gateway-sign-in line while the host's via is `canyon` and the stored front door signs in at a sign-in served through the cluster's own gateway (the egress row's `CANYON_KUBE_GATEWAY_SIGNIN`), the via read under the save's lock, `""` otherwise and on a `docker` host: Settings › App hosting shows it beside the front door as an amber line, once the save answers and on every read after, and it never refuses the save, which would lock admins out of fixing their sign-in (**amended 2026-10-17**, #2316, the owner's decision) · `discovery_note` is D5's discovery line while the stored front door is a `choice` `2` whose issuer's host the canonicalisation keeps and has no hosts recorded (*the discovery*, below), `""` otherwise and on a `docker` host: Settings › App hosting shows it beside the front door as an amber line, and `canyon status` as it shows `warning`, once the save answers and on every read after until the hosts are recorded; it never refuses the save (**amended 2026-10-20**, #2344); and D5's daily-check line while its hosts are recorded but the server's daily read of its discovery (*the discovery*, below) has failed since one last succeeded, on every read until one does (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2389) · `hand_off_needs` is what this server's own settings lack to serve `run_by: org` — `[{error, message}]`, the preferences PUT's `identity_image_required` and `https_required` refusals in that order, `[]` when it lacks neither and on a `docker` host — and Settings › App hosting shows each `message` to an admin under *To hand apps off to your platform, Canyon's server still needs* (amended 2026-10-11, #2010) · `probe_note` is D5's not-enforcing line while the stored front door has `enforcing` `false` — no deploy builds behind it (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2507) — else D5's not-checked line while it has `probed` `false`, either followed by one space and why — the probe's `fix` verbatim (for a door a refusal cleared, below, that refusal's), or D5's `probe_unavailable` sentence when the probe could not finish under `org` — or by nothing for a door stored before the field, and `""` otherwise and on a `docker` host: Settings › App hosting shows it beside the front door as an amber line, to every member (**amended 2026-10-23**, #2438) · `PUT {choice, gateway?, issuer?, audience?, jwks_url?, cookie?, alb_arn?, signin?: {issuer, client_id, client_secret?}, domain?, form}` → `200` the GET shape: the front door replaced whole — `choice` `1` with `gateway` `oidc` and `issuer`, `audience`, `jwks_url` and `cookie` (the gateway's own session cookie, which the sidecar keeps from the app), or `gateway` `alb`, `alb_arn` and `cookie` if the load balancer's is not `AWSELBAuthSessionCookie`; `choice` `2` with `signin`, whose `client_secret` is sealed in the vault and never answered, logged or shown, and omitted keeps the stored one while `issuer` and `client_id` are unchanged; a save without `signin` removes the sealed secret; `domain` omitted is `default_domain` · refusals, after the member's `403` and before anything is written (but for the one `probe_failed` below), in this order: `409 {error:"not_kubernetes", message}` on a `docker` host, `message` D5's Docker sentence · `400 {error:"invalid_form"}` for a `form` other than `app.env.domain` and `app-env.domain` · `400 {error:"invalid_domain"}` for a `domain` that is not a lower-case DNS name of two labels or more, is over 189 characters (the longest address, a 55-character app's under `preview`, is then 253) or has `preview` or `prod` as its first label · `400 {error:"invalid_front_door", message}` for a body that is not an object, or any other key or value `/run`'s `front_door` refuses — an `http` URL, a `gateway` with another's fields, a `client_secret` omitted with none stored for that `issuer` and `client_id` — `message` naming the first key and never a value · **the discovery** (design/EGRESS.md §3.3; **amended 2026-10-20**, #2344): a `choice` `2` save whose `signin.issuer` host the canonicalisation keeps (*The check*) has the server fetch its discovery document (`<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, over the server's own egress, ten seconds, no redirect followed, at most 1 MiB), after `signin_unsupported` (below) and before the probe; it reads one that names that issuer, an `https` `authorization_endpoint`, `token_endpoint` and `jwks_uri`, and a `userinfo_endpoint` that is `https` or absent, and records, by issuer, the hosts of its `token_endpoint`, `userinfo_endpoint` and `jwks_uri` as platform destinations, each as the canonicalisation keeps it — one it refuses, an IP address or a single label, is none — adding to the issuer's hosts recorded before, never dropping one: a sidecar started earlier keeps calling the host the document named then (**amended 2026-10-20**, #2344, round 2); every such save fetches again, one that changes nothing included; a fetch that fails, or a document it cannot read, never refuses the save: the reason is a log line, and from its start, for its whole life, the server tries every minute each `choice` `2` issuer in use (below) with none recorded, logging an issuer's first failure and then at most one an hour; once a day it reads again the discovery of each such issuer with hosts recorded, adding the hosts it names as a save does and logging each new one, which moves the policy's version — one finding nothing new moves nothing; one that fails keeps every host recorded, is tried every minute until one succeeds, and is answered as `discovery_note` meanwhile, its row written once a failure, not at every try (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2389); an issuer whose host the canonicalisation refuses is never fetched — its provider goes direct (`CANYON_KUBE_SIGNIN_DIRECT`), and none of its hosts is a platform destination · a door is **in use** while it is the stored front door, one it replaced in the last 30 minutes — a `/run` that read it before the save may still be starting its sidecars; the server's minute loop forgets it after, which moves the policy's version — or the one a running deploy under via canyon was given (its `run_config`, *Patch runs*), a deploy's first write of it as much as a later one moving the policy's version: the platform destinations are every door in use's — its sign-in hosts, an `alb`'s key endpoint, a `choice` `2`'s recorded hosts — so a door replaced keeps its hosts until the last app behind it is deployed again, and a deploy recorded without its run config (written before #2373, or by the org's report) adds none (fail closed); none is ever answered: `front_door` stays `/run`'s · then the server asks the executor's `POST /probe {domain, form}` (contracts/executor.md) and writes the door `probed` on `enforced` and `ok`, and **enforcing** on `network_policy` `enforced` whatever its `certificate` (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2507: what the build below needs; any other answer, or none, is not enforcing); on any other `200` a `choice` `1` door while a platform webhook or a deploy repo is saved (#2470) is written with `probed` `false` — the org's platform can run apps behind it, and Canyon runs none (below) — and any other is refused; a probe that could not finish refuses the save under `run_by: canyon` whatever is saved, changing nothing, so a passing failure never demotes a probed door (**amended 2026-10-23**, #2438): `409 {error:"probe_failed", message, network_policy, certificate}`, `message` the probe's `fix` verbatim — the switch to turn on, or the names the certificate must cover; a `probe_failed` whose `network_policy` is not `enforced` is the one refusal that writes, first setting the stored door, whatever door the PUT names, `probed` `false` and `enforcing` `false`, its `probe_note`'s why this refusal's `fix`, with a line in the server's log and no audit row — the cluster was just seen not to enforce NetworkPolicy, so nothing runs or builds behind any door until a save passes (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2507) — and any answer but a `200`, or none, `502 {error:"probe_unavailable", message}`, `message` D5's and the reason in the server's log · one audit row, `set front door`, `detail{before, after, secret_replaced}` — the GET's `front_door` on each side, `before` `null` for the first, `secret_replaced` whether the body carried a `client_secret`, which is always sealed anew; a PUT carrying none whose front door equals the stored one still runs the probe, records what it found — `probed`, `enforcing`, `warning`, `probe_note`'s why — and writes no row · **a running app keeps the front door it was deployed with**: each `/run` carries the one set at its call, so a change reaches an app at its next deploy or promotion · **under `run_by: org`** (design/HAND-OFF.md) the same door goes in each release's sidecar spec instead, the probe still runs and never refuses a `choice` `1` — a platform webhook or a deploy repo is saved whenever `run_by` is `org`, and one that could not finish stores the door `probed` `false` too — so a door that passes it is `probed`, the way back to `canyon` (the preferences row), and `domain` and `form` are not read by a release, the address being what the org reports (**amended 2026-10-23**, #2438); a `choice` `2` is then `409 {error:"signin_unsupported", message:"Your platform signs people in: set the front door to choice 1 first."}`, before the probe · on a `kubernetes` host a deploy or promotion while none is set fails before the executor is called, `failed` with `error`, its last `build.log` line D5's `No front door is set…` · **Canyon never runs an app behind a front door that has not passed the probe** (**amended 2026-10-23**, #2438): under `run_by: canyon` a deploy or promotion while the stored door has `probed` `false` fails the same way, its last `build.log` line D5's `The front door hasn't passed…`, and no `/run` is sent behind such a door, a patch run's included · **Canyon never builds on a cluster it has not seen enforce NetworkPolicy** (**amended 2026-10-26**, #2507, the owner's decision): under either `run_by`, a deploy while the stored door is not enforcing — the probe answered `not_enforced`, a new pod let out before its policy is in force included, or could not finish, or the door was stored before the record — fails the same way, before the executor is called, its last `build.log` line D5's `Canyon hasn't seen this cluster enforce NetworkPolicy…`, and no `/build` is sent: a build's NetworkPolicy holds only where the cluster enforces it (contracts/executor.md, *A build reaches no private or link-local address*); an admin turns enforcement on and saves the door again · **app addresses** on a `kubernetes` host — an environment's `url` (the project read), the `live at <url>` line and oauth2-proxy's redirect — are `https://<app>.<env>.<domain>` for `app.env.domain` and `https://<app>-<env>.<domain>` for `app-env.domain`, recorded when the deploy is; on `docker` they stay the public URL's scheme, host and port under `<app>.<env>.` · the samples are `contracts/testdata/front-door/meridian.json` |
| `GET /api/v1/platform-webhook` · `PUT /api/v1/platform-webhook` · `POST /api/v1/platform-webhook/token` · `POST /api/v1/platform-webhook/secret` · `POST /api/v1/platform-webhook/ping` | **where releases go under `run_by: org`** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3; the shapes are `release.schema.json`'s `webhook_*`) · `GET` for any signed-in member → `200 {url, set_by, set_at, last_delivery}` — `last_delivery` `{delivery, release, sent_at, status}`, the newest attempt to deliver a release since the webhook was last saved (`set_at`) — `sent_at` that attempt's, `status` the HTTP status it got, `0` for no connection or no answer in 10 seconds — `null` before any; a test delivery (`…/ping`) is never one (**amended 2026-10-23**, #2439) — and `200 null` before any webhook is saved, a defined answer that is no error status (**amended 2026-10-29**, #2560: a browser logs every 4xx it is answered); `POST …/token`, `…/secret` and `…/ping` answer `404 {error:"not_found"}` then · `PUT {url, secret?}` admin only, `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member → `200 {url, secret?, token?, set_by, set_at}`: `secret` is what Canyon signs every delivery with — the body's, or one Canyon makes on the first save when the body carries none — sealed in the vault as the front door's `client_secret` is; it is answered **once**, on that first save, and never again: a PUT carrying a `secret` uses it and answers none, a PUT without one keeps the stored secret and answers none · `token` is the host token the org reports with, stored as its SHA-256, answered **once**, on the first save, and never again: a later PUT changes it not — `POST …/token` is how it is replaced · `url` must be `https://`, else `400 {error:"invalid_url"}`; a `secret` outside 1–1024 printable ASCII characters without spaces (`0x21`–`0x7e`) is `400 {error:"invalid_secret"}` (amended 2026-10-27, #2425) · **deliveries** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3; `release.schema.json`): `POST <url>` with `Canyon-Event`, `Canyon-Delivery` and `Canyon-Signature-256: sha256=<hex>`, the HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body under the secret, GitHub's scheme; a `deploy`'s `variables` are the environment's runtime secrets' names less every name a resource `gives`, and its `resources` what the package declares — the platform provisions those and Canyon creates none (#2432); `delivery` and `sent_at` are in the signed body, `sent_at` the time of each attempt — every retry is the same delivery re-stamped and re-signed — and **a receiver must refuse a delivery whose `sent_at` is more than 30 minutes old, and must treat a `delivery` it has seen in the last 30 minutes as done**: freshness bounds the dedupe state it keeps, and Canyon's hour of retries stays valid because each attempt is fresh; **a receiver dispatches on the signed body's `event`, never the header**, which no signature covers, answers a bad signature `401`, and answers `2xx` to an event it does not act on, a `ping` among them, once its signature verifies, and ignores an event it does not know (#2439) · `POST …/token` admin only → `200 {token}`, a new host token, the old one dead at that instant · `POST …/secret` admin only → `200 {secret}`, a new webhook secret Canyon makes, sealed in place of the stored one and answered **once**: every delivery after that instant is signed with it, a release being retried then included; `url` stays, and `set_by` and `set_at` move to this replace, as for any save, so `last_delivery` never shows an answer to a release signed with the old secret (**added 2026-10-23**, #2439) · `POST …/ping` admin only, a **test delivery** (#2439): one `POST` of `release.schema.json`'s `ping` — `{event:"ping", delivery, sent_at}`, a fresh `delivery` — to the **saved** url, never one the request names, with `Canyon-Event: ping` and a release's signature, 10-second timeout and transport, no redirect followed, made outside any transaction; never retried, never a release, no audit row and never `last_delivery`, one server log line with the status alone → `200 {delivery, sent_at, status}`, `status` the HTTP status the url answered, `0` for no connection or no answer in 10 seconds, never why; one at a time per server: a second while one is in flight is `429 {error:"ping_in_flight", message}` · every answer carrying a secret or a token — the first PUT's, `…/token`'s and `…/secret`'s — is `Cache-Control: no-store` · one audit row per write, `set platform webhook` `detail{url, secret_replaced}`, `rotate platform token` or `rotate platform secret`, never a value |
| `GET /api/v1/platform-repo` · `PUT /api/v1/platform-repo` · `POST /api/v1/platform-repo/check` · `POST /api/v1/platform-repo/token` · `POST /api/v1/platform-repo/fetch-token` · `POST /api/v1/platform-repo/host-token` | **where releases go under `delivery: repo`** (design/HAND-OFF.md §3b, #2470; the shapes are `release.schema.json`'s `repo_*`; *The deploy repo*, below) · `GET` for any signed-in member → `200 {url, username, preview, prod, root, api, api_url, store, ca, set_by, set_at, checked_at, last_commit}` — never the token — `preview` `{branch}`, `prod` `{branch, mode}` with `mode` ∈ `pull_request \| push`, `ca` the PEM or `null`, `checked_at` the last passing check since the last save or `null`, `last_commit` the newest repo release's `{release, env, state, at, commit?, pull_request?, problem?}` (`state` the release's, so a screen says *committed, waiting for the first check-in* while it is `accepted`; `problem` the code its last attempt ended on) or `null` — and `200 null` before any is saved, as the platform-webhook row's read (**amended 2026-10-29**, #2560); `…/check`, `…/token`, `…/fetch-token` and `…/host-token` answer `404 {error:"not_found"}` then · `PUT {url, username?, token?, preview, prod, root?, api?, api_url?, store?, ca?}` admin only, `403 {error:"admin_required"}` before the body is read → `200`, the read's shape plus, **once**, `fetch_token` and `token`, the host token (the report row), on the first save — both kept on this row, never a platform-webhook row, so a server from before the deploy repo, which reads only that row, still boots on a host that saved only a deploy repo (before rolling back to one, leave repo mode: save a platform webhook and `PUT {delivery: "webhook"}`, which is `409 webhook_required` under `run_by: org` until one is saved, or `PUT {run_by: "canyon"}`) — every answer `Cache-Control: no-store`: `url` `https://`, a DNS name of lower-case letters, digits, `.` and `-` alone, with an optional `:<port>` — never an IPv6 literal or a name with `_` (amended 2026-10-27, #2473: nothing a shell reads, as the card hands it to `kubectl`; a row saved before keeps delivering, and its next save names the host by DNS), a path of one or more segments, no userinfo, query, fragment or percent-escape, at most 2048 bytes, and with `api: github` exactly `/<owner>/<repo>`, with `api: gitlab` two segments or more below GitLab's own root, `/<group>[/<subgroup>…]/<project>`, each with `.git` or not, else `400 {error:"invalid_repo_url"}` · `username` `x-access-token` when absent, 1–128 of `[A-Za-z0-9._@+-]`, else `400 {error:"invalid_username"}` · `token` 1–1024 printable ASCII characters without spaces (`0x21`–`0x7e`; amended 2026-10-27, #2425), required on the first save and on a save that names another host (with its port) for `url` or `api_url`, changes `ca` or changes `api` — the stored token never follows an address to a host, a trust or an API it was not given for — and kept otherwise when absent, sealed in the vault, else `400 {error:"invalid_token"}` · each branch 1–200 of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`, no `..`, `//`, `@{`, leading `-` or `/`, trailing `/` or `.lock`, never under `canyon/`, else `400 {error:"invalid_branch"}` · `prod.mode` `pull_request` when absent, `push` the only one under `api: none`, else `400 {error:"invalid_mode"}` · `root` `apps` when absent, a clean relative path of lower-case segments `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*`, at most 200 bytes, else `400 {error:"invalid_root"}` · `api` ∈ `github \| gitlab \| none`, `github` when absent, and `api_url` an `https://` URL as `url` is — when absent `https://api.github.com`, under `gitlab` `https://<url's host, with its port>/api/v4` — and under `gitlab` on `url`'s host and port, so the token goes to no other host, its path ending `/api/v4` and the rest of it, GitLab's own root (`/gitlab` on a GitLab served under one, else empty), the start of `url`'s path, else `400 {error:"invalid_api"}` · `store` `canyon` when absent, a DNS-1123 label, else `400 {error:"invalid_store"}` · `ca` `null` or 1 to 64 KiB of PEM holding one or more certificates and nothing else, else `400 {error:"invalid_ca"}` · a save clears `checked_at` · `POST …/check` admin only → `200 {passed, problems, templates}`, outside any transaction: it reads both branches, reads `<root>/.canyon` from each, renders a sample release of every resource type a template is found for, pushes a throwaway branch `canyon/check-<n>` and deletes it, and lists open pull requests under `api: github`, and open merge requests under `api: gitlab`, when `prod.mode` is `pull_request`; `problems` `[{code, message}]`, each code once, in that order, the codes and sentences D5's, never the remote's words; `templates` the files found under `<root>/.canyon`, sorted; `passed` exactly when `problems` is `[]`, which writes `checked_at`; one at a time per server, a second while one runs `429 {error:"check_in_flight", message}`; no audit row · `POST …/token` `{token}` admin only → `200 {set_by, set_at}`, the repo token replaced, `400 invalid_token` as the PUT's · `POST …/fetch-token` admin only → `200 {fetch_token}`, a new fetch-only token, the old one dead at that instant, `Cache-Control: no-store` · `POST …/host-token` admin only → `200 {token}` (`release.schema.json`'s `repo_host_token`), a new host token for this row, its old one dead at that instant, `Cache-Control: no-store` · one audit row per write, `set platform repo` `detail{url, preview, prod, root, api, api_url, store, token_replaced}` — never the token, the user name or the CA's bytes — `replace platform repo token`, `rotate fetch token` and `replace platform repo host token`, never a value |
| `GET /api/v1/integrations/channels` · `PUT /api/v1/integrations/channels/:id` · `DELETE /api/v1/integrations/channels/:id` · `POST /api/v1/integrations/channels/:id/test` | **the org's chat channels** (design/INTEGRATIONS.md; added 2026-11-09, #2725): where Canyon posts when production needs a person · **a PUT is a widening**, as it widens who reads every app's name, so a person's from the dashboard: `403 {error:"person_required", message}` to an `agent` or `canyon` actor, and on a host with sign-in `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's, each one `denied` row, before the body is read; a DELETE and a test work from any session (**amended 2026-11-12**, #2725) · PUT `{name, kind, url?}`: `:id` 1 to 40 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, beginning with a letter; `name` 1 to 80 printable characters; `kind` `slack` \| `teams`; `url` required on a new channel and kept when left out of a change, at most 2048 characters, `https`, no userinfo, its host `hooks.slack.com` for `slack` and for `teams` one ending `.logic.azure.com`, `.webhook.office.com` or `.powerplatform.com`; anything else is `400 {error:"invalid_channel", message}`, D5's words · a PUT posts *The summary* (*Integrations*, below) to the URL before it saves: answered 2xx it seals the URL in the vault, as a secret is, and saves, `200 {channel}`, audited `integration.channel.set` with the channel's id, name, kind and host; answered anything else, or nothing in 10 s, it saves nothing, `422 {error:"channel_unreachable", message, status}`, `status` the answer's code, `0` for none, the message D5's · a sixth channel is `409 {error:"too_many_channels", message}` · GET → `200 {channels: [{id, name, kind, host, tail, set_by, set_at}]}` (`integrations.schema.json`), by id, `host` the URL's host and `tail` its last four characters, `failing` `null` or `{since, status}` while the channel is failing (*Integrations*, the outbox): **no read answers the URL**, which is a bearer credential, nor does a log, an audit row or a timeline row hold it · DELETE → `204`, audited `integration.channel.removed`; an unknown id is `404 {error:"not_found"}` · POST …/test posts *The summary* again: `204`, or `422 {error:"channel_unreachable", message, status}`, audited `integration.channel.tested` with the answer's status · **admin** is the minimum level for every one of them, the reads included (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Integrations*): anyone below it is answered `403 {error:"admin_required", message}` |
| `GET /api/v1/patching/identities` | **the platform identities** (design/PATCHING.md §4.2; added 2026-10-18, #2086): each target run image Canyon patches onto, as Settings › App hosting draws it · an admin's: a member is `403 {error:"admin_required", message}` · `200 {identities[], run_image, plan}` (patch.schema.json's `identities_read`), newest `planned_at` first, each `{id, kind, run_image, builder?, planned_at, wave, apps, paused?, stalled?, approved?}` — `kind` `rebase`, or `rebuild` for a rebuild patch's (*Patch runs*, *A rebuild patch*; added 2026-11-02, #2117), whose `builder` is exactly the `GET /builder` answer's `builder` the plan named and `id` its `sha256:` digest, and which is never `approved` — `run_image` exactly the `GET /builder` answer's `run_image` the plan named (executor.md), and a rebase's `id` its `sha256:` digest, the one a patch's proof checks the rebased image against (*Patch runs*), `planned_at` when a plan first named it, `wave` the wave whose watches run now — `0` the canary, one app's preview, then `1` five apps, `2` twenty-five and `3` the rest, apps ordered by a hash of their id, a wave starting once every watch of the last ran to its end with no revert — a watch a request ended early proves nothing: its app files no patch approval for that patch and is left out of the wave's count, and when it was the canary's, the next tick names a replacement canary, the next app in canary order with a patch planned, whose watch the wave then waits on (amended 2026-10-18, the review of #2340) — `apps` the identity's apps counted by the `patch.state` of production where production has one, else of preview (`planned`, `patched_preview`, `asked`, `patched`, `reverted`, `held`, `denied` — added 2026-10-31, #2601), an app deleted counted in none (amended 2026-10-31, #2596; `paused.projects` keeps the names it paused), `paused` `{at, projects}` while two reverts or failed starts tripped its breaker — `projects` theirs — and no admin has resumed it, `stalled` `{at, projects}` while one revert in the last wave, short of the breaker, holds the next wave back — never beside `paused`: the breaker's pause clears the stall (amended 2026-10-31, #2595) — `approved` `{by, at, reason}` once an admin approved it for the fleet · **`run_image`** (amended 2026-10-29, #2105; design/PATCHING.md §0.4, §8) the run image Canyon names now and whether apps wait on a newer one, `{image, behind, projects}`: `image` the run image the newest rescan tick read from executor.md's `GET /builder`, as an identity's `run_image` is written, `behind` how many apps have an environment that reads `wait` on the status read (*Patch runs*, `envs.<env>.patch`), and `projects` those apps' ids, sorted, so the page names them (amended 2026-10-30, #2582; the read is an admin's, and an admin sees every app) — `null` when no rescan tick has read a run image or its match does not read, while the operator's or the org's patching is off (Canyon looks at nothing then), and while any app has an environment that reads `could_not_rescan`, so the page says Canyon can't tell and never that nothing is behind · **`plan`** (added 2026-11-01, #2588; design/PATCHING.md §4.2, *Each tick says what it planned*) what the newest rescan tick's patch plan said, the causes of its server log line, so Settings › Patching says beside the identity it planned onto why each app got no patch: `null` until a tick has planned, else `{at, identity, why?, apps}` — `at` when that tick planned; `identity` the `id` of the identity it planned onto, `null` when it named none; `why`, only when it read no app, one of `server_off` (this server does not patch: `CANYON_PATCHING` is not `on`), `org_off` (the org's `patching` is `off`), `not_wired` (this server has no executor, image read or policy to plan with), `no_preview` (no app has a preview), `rule_off` (no app has `platform.known-vuln` on, so the tick read and planned no app; added 2026-11-01, the review of #2607) and `failed` (the tick failed in its plan or before it, in its sweep or its plan of what to rescan, its own server line saying why: no app's cause is kept from it, nor from a tick before it); `apps` each app the plan read, sorted by `project`, each `{project, why}` with the one cause the line gives it — `started` (its patch run started), `refused` (a rebase of its image onto this run image was refused for good), `no_digest` (its preview's image is not named by digest), `policy` (its policy could not be composed), `rule_off` (`platform.known-vuln` is off), `unread` (its image's read could not check), `no_finding` (no counted finding at `high` or above), `no_fix` (the run image fixes none of its counted findings), `runtime` (its counted findings are in runtimes or build tools, which a rebase does not fix), `run_image_unread` (the run image's own scan could not be read), `stopped` (its preview is not running), `under_way` (a patch run of it is under way), `passed` (its patch onto this run image passed), `ended_early` (its watch ended early: planned again once its preview runs another image), `reverted` (its patch onto this run image reverted, and is not retried), `failed_start` (its patch failed to start, and is not retried), `failed` (its patch run failed: planned again once its preview runs another image), `paused` (patching onto this run image is paused until an admin resumes it), `wave` (it waits for its wave), `stalled` (it waits for its wave, which a stall holds until an admin resumes it), `goes_first` (another run of it goes first), `offline` (it is being taken offline), `moved` (it changed since the plan) and, failing closed, `unknown` for a cause the plan does not name, never read as nothing to do — an app deleted since left out; each tick's plan replaces the last, so an app it no longer read has no cause; names and causes alone, never an image's contents · a read: no audit row |
| `POST /api/v1/patching/identities/:id/approve` | `{reason}` — 1 to 1024 characters of plain text | **the fleet approval** (design/PATCHING.md §4.5; added 2026-10-18, #2086; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Platform patches*): an admin approves one identity for production across the fleet · `200 {identity}`, the identity as the read answers it, `approved` set · from then on each app of the identity whose preview watch ran to its end — now, and in each later wave — has its patch approval decided as it comes due, as *Production approvals* says: the approve route's checks under its lock, a refusal leaving it `pending`, `decided_by` the admin, and the promote run that starts reads that admin as its actor, so `prod.human-approval` reads that person (*The promote receipt*); each decision only while that admin is still one — a member of the org whose address is on the admin list, or a live sign-in of theirs under the current issuer that claimed admin, as a session is read (#2306) — else the approval is left `pending` for the app's team and the server logs it once per tick (a fail-closed reading of *never expires*: amended 2026-10-29, #2571), a held production is skipped and filed nothing, as ever, an app whose team decided its own approval first keeps that decision, an app whose team denied one for this identity is never decided for, and an app with a scheduled job is always its team's to decide (design/PATCHING.md §4.6) · it never expires, and v1 withdraws none: `patching: off` stops it · refusals in order, each before anything is written: `403 {error:"admin_required", message}` · `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"approve it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from a session whose `flow` is not `browser`, with one `denied` audit row, before the body is read · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for an id no identity has · `409 {error:"always_asked", message:"Canyon asks each app's team before a rebuild goes to production"}` for an identity of `kind` `rebuild` (*A rebuild patch*; added 2026-11-02, #2117) · `400 {error:"invalid_reason"}` · approving an approved identity answers it and writes nothing · served only while `CANYON_AUTH` is `oidc`, as *Production approvals* are · on a server built without Canyon Enterprise `409 licence_required`, `feature` `platform_patching`, after the refusals above, nothing written (*Licence*; added 2026-11-15, #2923) · one audit row `approve patch identity` `detail{identity, run_image, reason}` |
| `POST /api/v1/patching/identities/:id/resume` | — | **resume a paused or stalled identity** (design/PATCHING.md §4.2; added 2026-10-18, #2086) · `200 {identity}`, `paused` and `stalled` gone: the apps that reverted are left out of the identity, its waves go on from the one it stopped in, and its breaker counts reverts afresh · refusals in order, each before anything is written: `403 {error:"admin_required", message}` · `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` from a session whose `flow` is not `browser`, with one `denied` audit row · `404 {error:"not_found"}` for an id no identity has · `409 {error:"not_paused"}` on one neither paused nor stalled · on a server built without Canyon Enterprise `409 licence_required`, `feature` `platform_patching`, after the refusals above, nothing written (*Licence*; added 2026-11-15, #2923) · served only while `CANYON_AUTH` is `oidc`, as the fleet approval is · one audit row `resume patching` `detail{identity, run_image}` · the breaker's own pause is one row, actor Canyon, `pause patching` `detail{identity, run_image, projects}` |
| `GET /api/v1/component-types` | `200 {component_types: [{type, class, gives, status, notes}]}` — one row per `component_menu` entry (snapshot.md), **in the snapshot's order** · `type` is the Score resource type name (`postgres`, `s3`, …; score.dev) · `class` is Score's resource class, `default` for both — a Score key, never a Canyon category · `gives[]` the env var names the type provisions when declared (`DATABASE_URL`, `STORAGE_URL`), the type's promised outputs (contracts/provisioner.md) · `status` ∈ `available \| planned` — whether this host can provision the type today (it has a driver for it in preview; s3 only through an adapter), not whether an app may declare it; **under `run_by: org` every type is `available`**: the org's platform provides it (#2432), and a package declaring one uploads — a deploy after switching back to `canyon` then fails at its resources step on a type no driver serves · `notes` is agent-read product copy describing what the type does and does not do · **this is the prose shape**: `GET /api/v1/skill/manifest`'s `component_types[]` (row 180) is the same menu as the bare Score names alone, for a workload file's direct translation — the two shapes differ on purpose |
| `GET /api/v1/audit?actor=&subject=&via=&since=&until=&project=&rule=&category=&action=&page=&cursor=&limit=` | `{events[], total, page, next}` — event = `{id, ts, actor{kind, subject, display}, action, category, via?, role?, session?, source?, project?, run?, detail}` · **how and from where each action was done** (**amended 2026-10-05**, audit provenance: a CLI agent read as a person in a browser, and no row said which session or address did it): `via` ∈ `dashboard \| cli \| github \| platform \| canyon` — `dashboard` a request a browser session made, `cli` one a CLI (device) session made, `github` a row the GitHub App's work wrote, `platform` a release report the org's platform made, `canyon` a row Canyon wrote itself, a workflow's step among them (a deploy, a verdict) — and absent on a row a request with no session wrote (a demo host's sessionless caller, who may be either); `role` ∈ `admin \| responder \| member` the role of the person a request acted as; `session` names the session that acted, `ses_` and the first 16 hex of the sha256 of its secret, never the secret, and `source` the client address the server saw (the address the rate limit counts), on a row a request wrote; `session` and `source` are answered to admins alone; each is recorded from this amendment on where it applies, and a row written before it carries none · `via` narrows to the rows of one of the five (else `400 {error:"invalid_via"}`), and a row written before carries none, so it matches no `via` · `category` is the action's, from *Audit categories* below, read at the read (**amended 2026-10-05**, the audit filters: a screen filters on the server, never one page in the browser) · **a member is answered the rows of the apps they are `contributor` or above on, and of the rows naming no app, their own `sign in`, `sign out`, `denied` and `sign in refused` rows — `actor` a person whose `subject` is theirs — and, on any app, the `stop app` and `lift hold` rows they are the actor of (a responder's, *The hold*), and no other** (**amended 2026-10-07**, a member's audit is their own work: every other row naming no app — a policy changed, an admin's or a setting's change, another person's sign-in, and a change the member made while they were an admin — is an admin's to read), an admin every row; **an app's `request access` and `dismiss access request` rows — who asked for access, and when — are answered to its owners and admins alone**, as the project read's `requests` are, and left out of a contributor's read, from `total` too (amended 2026-10-26, #2500: design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Access requests*, shows a request to owners alone, and the trail answered it to every contributor; when the apps a member owns cannot be read, the read fails `500 {error:"internal"}` and answers no row); **so are an app's `share app` and `unshare app` rows — who holds what on it, which `GET …/access` answers its owners alone — and the `denied` rows naming it, who was turned away from it** (amended 2026-10-26, #2505: design/PERMISSIONS.md, *The org's own records*; a member's own `denied` rows naming no app stay theirs, as above); `total` counts only the rows the caller is answered; `project=` naming an app the member is below `contributor` on is `403 team_required`, before any row is read; an id no project, live or deleted, has carried is an empty page, as before · **one page, newest first** by `ts` then `id` (the screens read the trail from the latest change backwards; a page walked from the oldest end never reaches the recent one) · `rule` narrows to the rows that changed one rule — `policy.set`, `custom_rule.set`, `app_policy.set` and `app_policy.remove` rows whose `detail.rule` is that id, exactly; the first two are the org's rows, so an admin reads them, and an app's are read as its rows are, and an id no row names is an empty page, never a 404 (a custom rule's card links its History here) · `project` narrows to one project's rows by exact id — a deleted project's rows stay on the trail and stay reachable here, so an id that matches nothing is an empty page, never a 404 · cursor and limit exactly as on the packages list: opaque cursor over (`ts`, `id`), 50 by default, clamped at 200, `400 {error:"invalid_limit" \| "invalid_cursor"}`; the filters combine with the cursor and narrow the pages, not the trail · `total` and `page` as on the fleet read (**amended 2026-10-05**, the audit list's pager): `total` how many rows the filters select, across every page, the same on each page of one walk; `page` a page by its number, clamped to the last, `1` with neither `page` nor `cursor` and `null` by `cursor`, `400 {error:"invalid_page"}` for a non-integer, a value below 1 or `page` with `cursor` · `detail` values are plain text, never markup; a row whose `detail` names a `package` with a `label` carries that `label` too, read at the read from the package, so a deploy's, a promotion's or a review's row names the version as its upload's does (**amended 2026-10-04**, #967); **an event is a state change that took place**: the row commits in the same transaction as the change (the two land together or neither does) and names what the action produced (`project` on a create, `run` on a gate-run start, `detail.package` on an upload); a refused or failed request leaves a log line, never an audit row — but for the two refusals the trail keeps (**amended 2026-10-05**, denied and refused attempts: a reviewer of privileged access read pod logs for them): **`denied`**, a request refused `403` `admin_required`, `owner_required`, `team_required`, `person_required`, `responder_required` or `dashboard_required`, its `actor` the one refused, `project` the app the refusal is about when it knows it (a run's route names its run's app once it has read the run), `detail{action, error, target?}` — `action` the route pattern as the router names it (`GET` among them: a refused read is an attempt too), always, never the key the route would have written, which may hang on a body never read and `error` the refusal's code; `target` `{kind, id}` names what the refusal is about when that is no app, `{kind: "channel", id}` for a chat channel's PUT (the channels row), and `project` is then absent, so a channel whose id is an app's never reads as that app's denial (**amended 2026-11-14**, #2821) — and **`sign in refused`**, an identity provider vouching for a person Canyon then turned away at the browser sign-in, its `actor` `{kind: "person", subject, display}` from the provider's claims — `subject` the provider's `sub`, the email in `detail` — `detail{flow: "browser", email, reason}` — `reason` `not_a_member` (on neither membership list) or `setup` (not an admin while Canyon is being set up); each in a transaction of its own once the refused request is answered, never inside it (it changed nothing, and its transaction may hold the ledger lock the row's append takes), and a row that cannot be written is a log line that leaves the answer as it was;
