a `sign in refused` row, and a `denied` row naming no app, are answered to admins and to the person refused alone (the four actions above) — the people a provider vouched for and Canyon refused are not every member's to read — a `denied` row naming an app is read as that app's grants are: its owners' and admins'; `sign in` (a session minted by a flow in the Open group), `sign out` (`DELETE /api/v1/sessions` without `subject`), `revoke sessions` (with `subject`), the Access group's `share app`, `unshare app`, `request access` and `dismiss access request`, and `complete setup` (the `PUT /api/v1/preferences` that sets `onboarded_at`, the write that ends the setup state; `detail` is that PUT's, the keys that changed with their before and after) are actions on the trail, and a sign-in that fails for any other reason (the provider's error, a state or nonce that does not match) writes no row; a step a workflow completes outside any request (a deploy) writes its row too, in the transaction that records the step, `action` naming the step (`deploy preview`) and `actor` the one who triggered the run; the gate's verdict is Canyon's own act, so its row is Canyon's, never the triggering actor's — one per run, in the verdict's transaction: `action: "verdict"`, `actor{kind: "canyon", subject: "gate", display: "Canyon"}`, `run`, `detail{package, state: clean \| blocked, blocking, warnings}`, the counts the run row carries; a run that ends `failed` because the package did not build, no buildpack on the host recognised it, the app did not stay up, or the cluster had no room to build it, after its review let it through writes Canyon's row too, in the transaction that ends it, so the trail says what broke and not only that the review passed (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1518; `detect`, #1242): `action: "build failed"`, `"detect failed"`, `"start failed"` or `"build had no room"` (#1637), the same `actor` and `run`, `detail{package}`; `actor` filter ∈ `person, agent, canyon` (else `400 {error:"invalid_actor"}`); `since` is RFC 3339 and inclusive, `ts >= since` (else `400 {error:"invalid_since"}`); `until` is RFC 3339 and exclusive, `ts < until` (else `400 {error:"invalid_until"}`), an `until` at or before `since` an empty page; `subject` narrows to one actor's rows by exact `actor.subject`, at most 320 characters of valid UTF-8 with no NUL (else `400 {error:"invalid_subject"}`); `category` is a comma-separated list of at most three of `governance`, `access` and `outcome` (else `400 {error:"invalid_category"}`) and narrows to the rows of any of them; `action` is a comma-separated list of 1–64 stored action keys, each 1–200 characters of valid UTF-8 with no NUL (else `400 {error:"invalid_action"}`), and narrows to the rows of any of them, exactly, nothing trimmed — a key no row carries matches nothing, never an error; an empty `subject`, `category` or `action` is no filter, as an empty `project` is; the filters combine with each other as they do with the cursor |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/secrets/:name` | `{value, env?, where?}` → `204` — **no route reads a vault value back, ever** — an app that writes its own secret to its stdout discloses it through `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/logs` (row 119), which is why that row's `Canyon-Log-Redaction` header says so; the vault is never the source · `env` ∈ `preview, prod`, omitted = `preview` (else `400 {error:"invalid_env"}`) · `where` ∈ `runtime, build`, omitted = `runtime` (else `400 {error:"invalid_where"}`): the build receives exactly the `build` values and the container exactly the `runtime` values — the user declares the split, no name prefix decides it · a build runs once, in preview, and a promotion ships the image it made, so `env` `prod` with `where` `build` is `400 {error:"invalid_where", message:"Production runs the image preview built: set build values on preview"}`, before anything is written (**amended 2026-10-04**, #765) · `name` matches `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`, ≤ 128 chars, never `PORT` and never starting `CANYON_IDENTITY_` — the executor sets both — and never one the runtime owns: `PATH`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, or one starting `CNB_`, the buildpack launcher's, each of which would replace the image's own and stop the app finding or loading its runtime (**amended 2026-10-04**, #1256) (else `400 {error:"invalid_name"}`) · **on a Docker host** (`CANYON_RUNTIME` unset or `docker`), never a name the Docker executor refuses in a call's env (contracts/executor.md), since the app's next deploy, promotion, rollback or platform patch would fail naming it (**amended 2026-10-27**, #2490): any name starting `DOCKER_`, in any case, which docker's client reads; `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `PACK_HOME`, `PACK_VOLUME_KEY`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, which docker and pack take from the executor's own environment; and with `where` `build`, besides, `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `GODEBUG`, `REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE` and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, each in any case, and any name starting `PACK_`, in any case, which pack reads during the build — `400 {error:"invalid_name", message:"<name> is reserved here: this Canyon runs apps with Docker, and docker or pack reads it, so the app's next <deploy|build> would fail. Choose another name"}`, `build` for a `where` `build` value; on Kubernetes none of these is refused · `value` is bytes without NUL, ≤ 65,536 bytes, multi-line allowed (else `400 {error:"invalid_value"}`); all values of one `(project, env)` ≤ 1 MiB together (else `413 {error:"too_large"}`) · a PUT on an existing `(env, name)` overwrites and bumps `version` — no history, no read by version · **`409 {error:"provisioned"}`** when the row under that `(env, name)` is `provisioned`: Canyon's, not the caller's to overwrite · **`409 {error:"route_trust", message:"remove the app's credentials in <env> first: Canyon needs to set <name> itself for them"}`** (**added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on) when `where` is `runtime`, `name` is `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` or `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE` and the env holds a value route: the app would stop trusting the certificate Canyon adds its credentials with (*Egress control*, *Interception*, Trust), checked and written under the app env's advisory lock, as a route's write is · one audit row: `action` the route, `detail{name, env, where}` — who set what, never the value |
| `GET /api/v1/projects/:id/secrets` | `{secrets: [{name, env, version, set_by, set_at, origin, where}]}` — names only, never values; `where` as stored; `origin` as stored: `user` for a row the PUT wrote, `provisioned` for one a deploy sealed from a declared resource's outputs (set_by `{kind: "canyon", subject: "provisioner", display: "Canyon"}`; written when the resource is made ready — and again, overwriting it and bumping `version`, only when its record is not `ready` or the row is missing: a redeploy keeps the credential the running app holds — each write with one audit row `action: "ensure resource"`, `detail{name, type, env, outputs[]}`, outputs by name only — and when an operator re-issues the built-in driver's passwords after a restore, `action: "reissue resource password"`, contracts/provisioner.md, *After a restore*; amended 2026-10-11, #2014) · a deploy whose resource would give a name a `user` row already holds fails, naming it, and leaves the row as it was |
| `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id/secrets/:name?env=` | `204` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` · `409 {error:"provisioned"}` on a `provisioned` row · `env` as on PUT, omitted = `preview` · one audit row, `detail{name, env}` |
| `POST /api/v1/projects/:id/secrets/:name/adopt` | `{gate_run?}` | `204` · `409 {error:"publication_prefix"}` — the laundering refusal · `404 {error:"no_candidate", available[]}` — not routed in v1: no producer writes candidates yet, so the `/api/v1/` catch-all answers `404 {error:"not_found"}` |
| `GET /api/v1/design-systems` · `POST /api/v1/design-systems` | the registry rows (M9) · GET → `{design_systems: [{id, name, namespace, source_type, registry_url, tokens_url?, created_at, created_by}]}`, `id` is `ds_` + at least four lowercase letters or digits, `source_type` ∈ `shipped \| registry` — shadcn and Canyon are seeded `shipped` and no route edits or deletes one · POST `{name, namespace, registry_url, tokens_url?}` → `201 {design_system}` as `registry`: `name` 1–64 chars; `namespace` is the package name an app declares (`@meridian/ui`, 1–128), matched literally against a package's UI dependency by `design.org-system`; both URLs absolute `https` (`http` only on a loopback host) · `400 {error:"invalid_name" \| "invalid_namespace" \| "invalid_registry_url" \| "invalid_tokens_url"}` · `409 {error:"exists"}` when any row already carries that namespace, shipped or not — two rows under one namespace make the resolved system ambiguous · POST is `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · **Canyon never stores a registry credential** · one audit row · `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/design-system` (M9 9.1) is not routed in v1, so the `/api/v1/` catch-all answers `404 {error:"not_found"}` |
| `GET /api/v1/skill/manifest?app=` | `{host, policies[], component_types[], stacks[], design_system, prod_approval, app_additions?, playbooks[]}` — typed data, never the model's prose: the two texts are Canyon's own `how` and a custom rule's `instructions`, below, each handed only where its rule blocks · `host` is **the host's configured public URL, scheme and all, never the request's `Host` header**: this string is written into a builder's agent config, so a header must not be able to point their agent at another server; scheme and host only (`http` or `https`, any host), no path and no trailing slash, since a skill file joins routes onto it · `policies[]` are `{id, name, state, production?, how?, instructions?, says?}`, every rule the snapshot carries that is on (snapshot.md, *Two gates*), in the snapshot's order — the snapshot's `name`, `state` and `production`, never the internal `producer`. Where a rule is `blocking` in either gate, and only there, it carries how to meet it, so an agent reads that before it writes code rather than in a blocked deploy's next steps; a rule that only warns is handed by name alone (amended 2026-11-03, #2622). **`how`** is a Canyon rule's one line on how to pass it, Canyon's own words, shipped with the server beside the rule (`gate/policy`, `howToPass`, each taken from the rule's "How to fix it" in `docs/src/content/docs/rules/<id>.md`), at most 120 characters, never on a `custom.*` rule; the dashboard's *What agents are told* and `canyon manifest` both print it, so the admin reads what an agent reads. **`instructions`** is a `custom.*` rule's text as its admin wrote it (snapshot.md, *Custom rules*), the one org-written text an agent is handed: only admins write it, every version is audited with its diff, and it never holds a secret. **`says`** rides with it on a custom rule checked by code: its check's `says`, the condition Canyon checks in plain words, so an agent builds to what is checked (amended 2026-11-04, #2639); a rule checked by AI carries none. **A rule Off in both gates is never handed out:** it gives an agent nothing to do, and a file committed to a repository must not list which guards are down · `component_types[]` are the snapshot's `component_menu` strings — **Score resource type names** (`postgres`, `s3`, …; score.dev), so a Score workload file is a direct translation and no mapping table exists — not the rows `GET /api/v1/component-types` answers — that shape's `notes` is prose · `stacks` is the org's stacks as GET /preferences answers it — what agents are told to build with · `design_system` is `{name, namespace, registry_url, tokens_url?}` or `null` — no `id`: an agent names a registry, not a row · `prod_approval` is the preferences' (amended 2026-10-07, #1820), so an agent is told whether a promotion it asks for waits for a person · **`app_additions`** — with `app`, an app's id, the additions that app holds (the `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/policies/:rule` row) as `[{id, name, state?, production?, set_by, how?, instructions?, says?}]`, in the snapshot's order: the rule's id and the snapshot's `name`, the addition's own state in each gate where it is stricter than the org's state there now, `how` or a custom rule's `instructions` where the addition it hands is `blocking` in either gate, as `policies[]` carries them — an addition the org has since caught up with in both gates is left out — and who set it; present only when `app` names a live app that holds additions and the caller is `contributor` or above on it (an admin: any app), as `GET /policies` shows additions — otherwise absent, never a refusal, because a session hook reads this route and must not fail a session. An agent is then told which of the app's rules are stricter than the org's and who made them so (amended 2026-10-07, QA batch 2: agents read the org's states alone and told the user "this rule only warns" or "one of the two is wrong") · **`playbooks[]`** (**added 2026-11-20**, design/specs/mcp.md §3.3) is every playbook not removed as `{slug, name, kind, when, version}`, by `slug` bytewise, never a body, so the session hook's context stays small: an agent reads a playbook whose `when` fits its task by its slug (the playbooks row), `[]` for none · the samples are `manifest` and `manifest_app` in `contracts/testdata/guardrails/preferences.json` |
| `GET /api/v1/preferences` · `PUT /api/v1/preferences` | one org per host, one row · GET → `{stacks, approved_buildpacks, buildpacks_seen, design_system, setup_available, ai_review, viewer, org_name, allowed_domains, allowed_emails, admin_emails, bootstrap_admins, admin_claim?, admin_value?, responder_emails?, responder_claim?, responder_value?, groups_claim, onboarded_at, run_by, access_grace, delivery, prod_approval, patching, rebuilds, push_approvers_linked, db_prod, db_sessions, overridden}` · **`responder_emails`, and `responder_claim` with `responder_value`** (design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Security responders*): the security responders by address, and the one claim and value that mean responder, mirroring the admin pair — the pair both set or neither, matched at sign-in and kept on the session; **all three answered to an admin only**, `responder_emails` `[]` until set, the pair absent while unset; PUT names them as the admin keys — `responder_emails` a list as `admin_emails` is, the pair together, both strings or both `null`; each change audited before and after with the rest · `stacks` is `[{language, frameworks[]}]`, one row per language with ≥1 framework, ≤16 stacks and ≤16 frameworks each, every name plain text 1–64 chars and no control characters — what agents are told to build with; it checks nothing · `approved_buildpacks` is the buildpacks `stack.blessed` approves (*The promote receipt*), by id — at most 64, each once, each `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9./-]{0,127}$` (a CNB buildpack id), stored and answered sorted by id so the same set is the same snapshot, `[]` until an admin sets it · while `prod_approval` is on, a PUT naming it from a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` with one `denied` audit row, as a policy change is (amended 2026-10-10, #1966): emptying it leaves `stack.blessed` unset, so it blocks nothing · **so is a PUT naming `design_system_id`** (amended 2026-10-12): clearing it leaves `design.org-system` with nothing to check, so a Blocking one passes every app · `buildpacks_seen` is every buildpack a deploy on this host recorded running (the deploys row), `{id, name?}` once per id, sorted by id, the first 256 — what the screen offers to approve, `name` the newest recorded one — `[]` before any deploy recorded one (amended 2026-10-04, #636) · `design_system` is the org default resolved → `{id, name, namespace, registry_url, tokens_url?}` or `null` · `setup_available` is whether this host serves the setup chat (`POST /api/v1/guardrails/setup`), so the screen knows whether to offer the setup panel — a model speaking the Claude API; an `openai` host has none · `ai_review` is whether this host's AI review runs — any configured model, `openai` included — what Settings › AI review reports (amended 2026-10-11, #2003) · `viewer` is `{email, role}` **here — the address the session stores and `member \| responder \| admin`, and no display name** — the signed-in person, so the settings page can find the reader in the lists it is drawing: which of these addresses is mine, and may I edit any of this. `GET /api/v1/fleet` (row 208) answers a `viewer` too and answers it as `{display, role}`; the name is shared because the concept is, the projection differs because a header and a membership list need different facts, and no consumer receives both · **the organisation's settings** (design/ONBOARDING.md §5; each was an environment variable, design/AUTH.md §3, and the variable is now the bootstrap and the override): `org_name` plain text 1–128 chars · `allowed_domains` an array of bare lower-case domains, each with a dot, and `allowed_emails` an array of lower-case addresses, each with an `@` — membership is their union, **not both empty** · `admin_emails` an array of addresses · `bootstrap_admins` an array of addresses too: **the admins `CANYON_ADMINS` grants, answered separately from `admin_emails` and never merged into it.** Admin is the union of the environment's list, the row's list and a matched claim (canyonv2-cr23), and `admin_emails` is deliberately not among the keys `overridden` marks, so nothing else in this read tells a screen that some of the admins it is showing are not the row's. Without this key a screen draws one list, somebody removes a bootstrap address, the PUT succeeds — the key was never operator-set, so there is nothing to refuse — and that person is still an admin. A control that reports success and changes nothing is exactly the failure `set_by_operator` exists to prevent on every other key; this key is how the screen prevents it here, by drawing the two groups apart and saying which one it can act on · `admin_claim` and `admin_value`, the one claim and value that mean admin, **both set or neither, and answered to an admin only** (canyonv2-7mtz) — present together or absent together on an admin's read, **both absent on a member's**, whatever the row holds. `overridden` still names the pair to a member when the environment sets it: a variable's name is not its value, and a member who cannot tell a redacted field from an unset one reads an operator-pinned claim as no claim at all · every domain 1–253 and every address 1–254 characters, every string plain text with no control characters · `groups_claim` is the sign-in claim that carries a person's groups, `""` while unset — and while it is unset no `group` grant is written (`409 groups_unsupported`, the Access group); a PUT setting it to a claim other than the one stored is `409 licence_required` (*Licence*), `feature` `group_sync`, while no valid or grace licence names it, before anything is written, and clearing it never is (**added 2026-11-14**, #2879) · **hand-off** (design/HAND-OFF.md §1): `run_by` ∈ `canyon \| org`, where apps run, `canyon` by default, and `access_grace`, how long a handed-off app keeps its last access list when Canyon is unreachable — a duration as Go spells it, `0` to `24h`, `1h` by default — both on every read; a PUT of `run_by: org` is refused, before anything is written, `409 {error:"not_kubernetes", message}` on a `docker` host (D5's Docker sentence), `409 {error:"identity_image_required", message}` while the server's `CANYON_IMAGE_IDENTITY` is unset — every release names it as the sidecar's image — and `409 {error:"https_required", message}` while `CANYON_PUBLIC_URL` is not `https` — the sidecar refuses any other access URL — each `message` naming the setting and that the server must restart with it (amended 2026-10-11, #2010; the server also refuses to start while `run_by` is `org` and either holds, naming the setting), `409 {error:"webhook_required"}` with no platform webhook saved under `delivery: webhook`, `409 {error:"repo_required", message:"Save your deploy repo first."}` with no deploy repo saved under `delivery: repo` (#2470), `409 {error:"front_door_required"}` with no front door, `409 {error:"signin_unsupported", message}` with a front door at choice 2, and `409 {error:"credentials_exist", apps, message:"<apps> have credentials that work only through Canyon. Their teams need to set those keys as secrets again and deploy, then remove the credentials."}` (`message:"<app> has credentials that work only through Canyon. Its team needs to set those keys as secrets again and deploy, then remove the credentials."` with one app, **amended 2026-11-15**) while any app env holds a value route, `apps` the apps that hold one, by name (**added 2026-11-07**, #2135; served from #2137 on); a PUT moving `run_by` from `org` to `canyon` while the stored front door has `probed` `false` is `409 {error:"front_door_unchecked", message}`, D5's, before anything is written — Canyon never runs an app behind a door that has not passed the probe, and saving the door again runs it (the front-door row; **amended 2026-10-23**, #2438); `access_grace` outside its shape or range is `400 {error:"invalid_access_grace"}`; each takes effect on the next release sent, and neither is ever `overridden`· **`delivery`** ∈ `webhook | repo` (design/HAND-OFF.md §1, §3b; #2470), how releases reach the org's platform — `webhook` by default, on every read, admin-only to change and audited before and after with the rest — a value outside the two is `400 {error:"invalid_body"}`, and a PUT of `delivery: repo` while `run_by` is `org` with no deploy repo saved, or of `delivery: webhook` while `run_by` is `org` with no webhook saved, is `409 repo_required` or `409 webhook_required` before anything is written; it takes effect on the next release, which records it as its `via` (*The deploy repo*), and is never `overridden` · **`push_approvers_linked`** (#2342, the owner 2026-10-18) is whether a blocking finding of a run a GitHub push started is approved only by someone who linked a GitHub account (`GET /api/v1/github/link`; the approve rows) — `false` by default, an admin's to set; a PUT of `true` with sign-in off is refused `409 {error:"signin_required", message}` before anything is written, as `prod_approval`'s is, and a PUT naming it from a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` with one `denied` audit row; read as stored, so one turned on before sign-in was turned off still shows, and still holds, until an admin turns it off; never `overridden`; one audit row names it as every preference change does · **`prod_approval`** (design/AUTH.md §4.5; amended 2026-10-07, #1820) is whether a promotion the CLI asks for waits for a person to approve it in the dashboard (*Production approvals*, Deploy & promote) — `false` by default, and `false` on every read while sign-in is off (`CANYON_AUTH` other than `oidc`), when nobody can be told from the dashboard: a PUT of `true` there is refused `409 {error:"signin_required", message}` before anything is written; a PUT naming it from a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}`, before anything is written, with one `denied` audit row — otherwise an admin's agent could turn it off from the CLI and promote; never `overridden`; it covers promotion, approving a blocking finding (the approve rows; amended 2026-10-10, #1824) and changing a policy, a custom rule, the approved buildpacks or the design system (the policies and custom-rules rows, `approved_buildpacks` above; amended 2026-10-10, #1966, the owner) — undeploying production is the undeploy row's · **`db_sessions`** (design/DATABASE-SESSIONS.md §5; **added 2026-11-12**, #2835) ∈ `off \| on`, `off` by default, answered to every reader: whether a person may open a database session at all; an admin's alone; a PUT of `on` from an `agent` is `403 person_required`, and with sign-in on, from any session but a browser's, `403 dashboard_required` with one `denied` audit row, both before anything is written; a PUT of `off` is taken from any of an admin's sessions, and closes every open database session `turned_off` and ends its relays (*Database sessions*); audited before and after with the rest; never `overridden` · **`patching`** (design/PATCHING.md §4.5; amended 2026-10-18, #2086) ∈ `off \| ask`, `ask` by default: whether Canyon patches its run image under the apps, to preview on its own and to production when a person approves (*Patch runs*, *Production approvals*) — the org's switch, answered as saved, and read `off` while sign-in is off, when nobody can decide a patch approval: a PUT of `ask` there is refused `409 {error:"signin_required", message}` before anything is written, as `prod_approval`'s is; a PUT moving it from `off` to `ask` is `409 licence_required` (*Licence*), `feature` `platform_patching`, while no valid or grace licence names it, before anything is written, and `ask` as it is keeps patching whatever the licence (**added 2026-11-14**, #2879); a new install starts `off` without a licence (*Licence*, Patching at upgrade) (**amended 2026-11-14**, #2881's review); a PUT naming it from a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}`, before anything is written, with one `denied` audit row, whatever `prod_approval` says; never `overridden`; `off` stops every patch run before its `/run` and files no patch approval; **an app is patched only while its own `platform.known-vuln` is on** (amended 2026-10-29, #2102, the security review): one condition, read live wherever Canyon plans a patch, starts a patch run's `/run`, files a patch approval, reads one and decides one — `CANYON_PATCHING` `on`, sign-in on, this switch `ask`, and the app's effective `platform.known-vuln` (its additions included) not `off` — so an app whose own rule is on is patched while the org's is off, and one whose rule turns off has its patch approval read `outdated`, as `off` here does · **`rebuilds`** (design/PATCHING.md §5; added 2026-11-02, #2117) ∈ `off \| ask`, `off` by default: whether Canyon also rebuilds an app on the builder it names now when a counted finding is in a runtime or the app launcher, to preview on its own and to production only when the app's team approves (*Patch runs*, *A rebuild patch*) — answered as saved, read `off` while `patching` reads `off`, and changed as `patching` is: an admin's alone, from a browser session, a PUT of `ask` with sign-in off `409 {error:"signin_required", message}`, any other session `403 dashboard_required` with one `denied` audit row, both before anything is written; a PUT moving it from `off` to `ask` is `409 licence_required`, `feature` `platform_patching`, as `patching`'s is (**added 2026-11-14**, #2879); a PUT of `ask` while `patching` reads `off`, or one the PUT turns `off`, is `409 {error:"patching_off", message:"Turn on patching first: Canyon rebuilds apps only while it patches them."}` before anything is written, and a PUT that turns `patching` `off` turns `rebuilds` `off` in the same write and audit row (amended 2026-11-02, #2619's review); never `overridden`; `off` stops every rebuild patch before its `/run`, files no approval for one, and reads each open one `outdated`, so approving it ships nothing · **`db_prod`** (design/DATABASE-SESSIONS.md §5; design/PERMISSIONS.md, *Database sessions*; **added 2026-10-18**, #2106) ∈ `off \| read \| write`, `off` by default: whether production databases are reachable through *Database sessions* — `read` lets a contributor read them with a reason, `write` lets an owner also write them — read `off` while sign-in is off, and judged so by every open, attach and check whatever the row holds, when a PUT of `read` or `write` is refused `409 {error:"signin_required", message}` before anything is written, as `prod_approval`'s is; a PUT naming it from a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}`, before anything is written, with one `denied` audit row, whatever `prod_approval` says; never `overridden`; a value outside the three is `400 {error:"invalid_body"}`; narrowing it closes the database sessions it no longer allows at the next check, `setting_changed` · `onboarded_at` is RFC 3339 at whole seconds UTC, or `null` while the host is in the setup state (the paragraph above the Open group) · `overridden` is the keys the environment is setting: `org_name` (`CANYON_ORG`), `allowed_domains` (`CANYON_OIDC_ALLOWED_DOMAINS`), `allowed_emails` (`CANYON_OIDC_ALLOWED_EMAILS`), `admin_claim` and `admin_value` (`CANYON_ADMIN_CLAIM`, `CANYON_ADMIN_VALUE`, both or neither), `responder_claim` and `responder_value` (`CANYON_RESPONDER_CLAIM`, `CANYON_RESPONDER_VALUE`, both or neither), `groups_claim` (`CANYON_GROUPS_CLAIM`) — the read answers the environment's value for such a key, so the screen renders the field read-only and names the variable rather than letting somebody edit a value that will not take; `[]` when the environment sets none · PUT `{stacks?, approved_buildpacks?, design_system_id?, org_name?, allowed_domains?, allowed_emails?, admin_emails?, admin_claim?, admin_value?, groups_claim?, onboarded_at?, run_by?, access_grace?, prod_approval?, patching?, rebuilds?, push_approvers_linked?, db_prod?, db_sessions?}` writes only the keys present — an absent key is unchanged, `design_system_id: null` clears it, `admin_claim` and `admin_value` are named together, both strings or both `null` (clearing the pair), `groups_claim` a claim name or `""` to clear it — and answers the GET shape · `onboarded_at` accepts an RFC 3339 instant and is never cleared: the PUT that sets it from `null` is **`complete setup`** (design/ONBOARDING.md step 4), the write that ends the setup state, and its audit row's `action` says so · PUT is `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · **`admin_emails` is not among them: `CANYON_ADMINS` grants admin without pinning the key** (canyonv2-cr23) — admin is the union of the bootstrap list, the row's list and a matched claim, so the screen can add an admin on a host that has one, and editing the variable stays the documented recovery from a wrong list · four refusals, each judged on the row **as the PUT would leave it** and answered before anything is written: `400 {error:"no_members"}` when both membership lists would be empty (an open door or a brick — step 2's disabled Continue, said by the server) · `400 {error:"no_admins"}` when no admin would remain across `admin_emails`, `bootstrap_admins` and a set `admin_claim`, all three together — an organisation may empty its own `admin_emails` while `bootstrap_admins` is non-empty, because those bootstrap admins still administer the host · `400 {error:"admin_locked_out"}` when no admin address would be on either membership list, the message naming the stored address it could not place — never the request's, so somebody staring at their own typo is shown the address they were trying to match. At least one admin address, not every one: a stale `CANYON_ADMINS` entry must not refuse every routine membership edit. An `admin_claim` does not satisfy it — it names no address, and a claim matching nobody is indistinguishable from one matching everybody until somebody signs in. Without it a PUT naming a membership list the signer is not on, and `onboarded_at`, ends setup and locks that signer out on their next request, including on the PUT that would undo it · `409 {error:"set_by_operator", keys:[...]}` when the PUT names a key in `overridden`, `keys` those keys — without it a screen could accept an edit that silently does nothing · `400 {error:"invalid_stacks" \| "invalid_approved_buildpacks" \| "invalid_design_system" \| "invalid_body"}` — `invalid_body` also for a value outside the bounds above; a `design_system_id` no row carries → `404 {error:"not_found"}` · one audit row: `action` the route pattern — `complete setup` for the PUT that sets `onboarded_at` — `detail` the keys that changed with their before and after — typed values, never prose |
| `POST /api/v1/guardrails/setup` | `{messages: [{role, content}]}`, `role` ∈ `user \| assistant`, `content` 1–8,192 chars; ≤20 messages and ≤32 KB of body, else `400 {error:"invalid_messages"}` · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member · while `prod_approval` is on, a session whose `flow` is not `browser` is `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"change it in Canyon's dashboard"}` before the body is read, with one `denied` audit row (amended 2026-10-12): a turn can set the design system, which the preferences PUT refuses such a session · a plain request and response, **never streamed**: the client resends the conversation and the server stores none of it · `200 {message, writes[], proposal?}` — `message` is the assistant's line for the panel; `writes[]` is one `{tool, before, after}` per tool call that changed something, so the panel echoes every write and the form beside it re-reads `GET /api/v1/preferences` after every turn, success or failure — **the form is the record and the undo**; `proposal` appears only when the model proposed a policy change, `{rule, state, grace_days?, would_block: [{project, findings}]}`, the `would_block` that `PUT /api/v1/policies/:rule` answers without `?apply=true`, beside the `rule`, `state` and `grace_days` the client never sent — the model has no apply tool, applying is the admin's click and attributed to them, and it may name only a rule the snapshot carries and only the three states · ≤8 tool iterations per turn; past it the turn ends with the model's last line · `502 {error:"model_error"}` when the model cannot be reached or answers nothing — writes already committed stay committed, which is why the client re-reads · `429 {error:"busy"}` before anything is read when the host already has four turns in flight: a turn is up to eight model calls, and the cap is what bounds the bill from a session that loops · **not registered at all when the host has no model speaking the Claude API** (none, or `openai`): the `/api/v1/` catch-all answers `404 {error:"not_found"}` and GET /preferences says `setup_available: false` · one audit row per tool call, `detail {tool, before, after}` |
| `POST /api/v1/demo/fix/:app` | demo-mode hosts only; not routed when auth is configured |
| `GET /api/v1/github/app/new?org=&imports_new=&keeps_code=` | registers Canyon's GitHub App **on a GitHub organization** (M8; **amended 2026-10-09**, #1914: an App registered from github.com/settings/apps/new is owned by the admin's personal account, and GitHub installs a private App only on the account that owns it) · `200 text/html` — a form that submits itself, posting the App's manifest to `https://github.com/organizations/<org>/settings/apps/new` with a single-use state value held in a cookie built by the session cookie's builder · `org` is the organization's GitHub login (`^[A-Za-z0-9](-?[A-Za-z0-9])*$`, at most 39 characters, else `400 {error:"invalid_org"}`) · `imports_new` and `keeps_code` are each `true` or `false`, else `400 {error:"bad_request"}`: `imports_new` whether the organization holds only Canyon apps, `keeps_code` whether Canyon may create repositories there and commit to them · the manifest names the App `Canyon — <org>`, cut to GitHub's 34 characters, private, its webhook this host's `/webhooks/github`, its user authorization callback this host's `/api/v1/github/link/callback` (#2228), the events `push`, `pull_request` and `repository` (`repository` from #1917: renames, transfers, deletes and archives keep its links; `installation` and `installation_repositories` reach every App unsubscribed), and the permissions `metadata: read`, `contents: read`, `checks: write` and `security_events: read`; with `keeps_code` `contents: write` and `administration: write` instead of `contents: read`, and never `workflows` · **one App per organization**: `409 {error:"exists"}` when an App is registered on that organization · `409 {error:"keeping_org_exists"}` for `keeps_code=true` when another organization's App keeps code: one organization keeps code · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `GET /api/v1/github/app/callback?code=&state=` | where GitHub returns the admin's browser after registration · `302` to the App's installation page on GitHub, the App's credentials stored sealed — its client secret among them since #2228, which linking a GitHub account needs —, with the organization GitHub answers as its owner and the two answers the registration carried — installing the App on repositories is the admin's next step · one audit row `github app registered` `detail{app_id, slug, account, keeps_code, imports_new}` · `400 {error:"auth_failed"}` on a state that does not match the cookie's, a manifest conversion that fails, or one whose App is not owned by the organization the registration named (an `Organization` of that login, case aside) — no detail echoed; the reason goes to the log · `409 {error:"exists"}` when an App was registered on that organization while this admin was on GitHub, and `409 {error:"keeping_org_exists"}` when another organization started keeping code meanwhile: nothing is written · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, before the code is exchanged: the state cookie alone does not prove who is finishing the registration |
| `GET /api/v1/github` | the GitHub organizations Canyon's Apps are registered on (#1914) · `200 {reachable, orgs[]}` · `reachable` is false exactly when the public URL's host is `localhost`, a name under `.localhost` or a loopback address, where github.com can't deliver events: the dashboard says so in place of its register button, and the route itself refuses nothing on it, so a stack whose GitHub is a stub on the same machine registers (amended 2026-10-09, #1914) · each org is `{account, app_slug, keeps_code, imports_new, installed, last_event_at, links_accounts}`, oldest registration first: `links_accounts` whether Canyon holds the App's client secret, which linking a GitHub account needs (#2228), `account` the organization's login — for an App registered before #1914, on a personal account, the account it was first installed on, `""` while it has none, `installed` whether the App has a live installation, `last_event_at` the newest delivery accepted from it, `null` before any · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `GET /api/v1/github/link` | **links the caller's own GitHub account to them** (#2228), so a push by it is theirs: a run it starts is `author_cannot_approve` to them, and an approval they file covers none of its blocking findings (the approve rows) · any signed-in person, from a browser session · `302` to GitHub's `https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize` with the client id of the oldest App registered with a client secret, this host's `/api/v1/github/link/callback` as `redirect_uri` and a single-use state value held in a cookie built by the session cookie's builder · `403 {error:"dashboard_required", message:"link your GitHub account in Canyon's dashboard"}` from any session but a browser's · `409 {error:"not_configured", message:"no GitHub App here can link accounts: none was registered since Canyon could link them"}` when no App was registered with a client secret (none is, of those registered before #2228: Canyon receives an App's client secret only as it registers it), and the dashboard offers no link then (`github_link`, the fleet's `viewer`) · `404 {error:"not_found"}` with sign-in off, where no caller is a person |
| `GET /api/v1/github/link/callback?code=&state=` | where GitHub returns the person's browser · the code is exchanged for a user token with the App's client id and secret, `GET /user` answers the account's numeric `id` and `login`, and the token is dropped, never stored · the caller is linked to that account by its numeric id, the login kept for display alone, and `302` to `/` · a person may link more than one account and an account may be linked to more than one person: each link only ever binds the person who made it · one audit row `link github account` `detail{github_id, login}`, none when the account was already linked to the caller · GitHub's `error=access_denied` (the person cancelled) is `302` to `/` with nothing written · `400 {error:"auth_failed"}` on a state that does not match the cookie's, or an exchange or a `GET /user` that fails — no detail echoed; the reason goes to the log · the same `dashboard_required`, `not_configured` and `404` as the link, judged first |
| `PUT /api/v1/github/orgs/:account` | `{imports_new}` — whether new repositories in the organization become apps (#1923) · `200 {org}`, the org as `GET /api/v1/github` lists it · `account` matched as GitHub does, case aside · `400 {error:"bad_request"}` unless `imports_new` is a boolean · `404 {error:"not_found"}` when no App is registered on it, answered only after the admin check, so a member learns nothing of which organizations are connected · one audit row `set github org` `detail{account, imports_new}` naming the new value, none when nothing changes · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `PUT /api/v1/github/orgs/:account/client-secret` | `{client_secret}` — the App's client secret, for an App registered before Canyon kept one (#2228): its owner generates a new one on the App's settings page on GitHub (*Client secrets* → *Generate a new client secret*) and adds this host's `/api/v1/github/link/callback` as a *Callback URL* there | `200 {org}`, the org as `GET /api/v1/github` lists it, `links_accounts` true · the secret is sealed as the App's other credentials are, under the App's key version, and never answered, logged or audited · it replaces one stored before, so a secret regenerated on GitHub is supplied the same way · `400 {error:"invalid_secret"}` unless `client_secret` is 1 to 255 printable ASCII characters, no spaces · `404 {error:"not_found"}` when no App is registered on it, after the admin check · one audit row `set github client secret` `detail{account}` · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/auto-deploy` | `{auto_deploy}` — whether a push to the app's linked repository deploys to preview (#1923), off on every app until an owner or admin turns it on · `200 {auto_deploy}` · `400 {error:"bad_request"}` unless `auto_deploy` is a boolean · minimum level `owner` (the Level table) · `404 {error:"not_linked"}` when the app has no link to a repository · one audit row `set auto-deploy` `detail{auto_deploy}` when it changes, none when it does not · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted app · unlinking (`DELETE …/repository`) turns it off in the same transaction, with its `set auto-deploy` row, so a yes given for one repository never deploys another's pushes |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/repository/keeps` | `{keeps: false}` — hands the repository Canyon keeps the app's code in to developers (*Developers push from now on*, #1938): Canyon commits no more versions there, and the repository, its history and the link stay, so it feeds the app as any linked repository does, each push a version reviewed and never deployed unless the app's `auto_deploy` is on · `200 {repository}`, the project read's, `keeps` false · `400 {error:"bad_request"}` unless the body is exactly `{keeps: false}`: Canyon never takes a repository back from developers · minimum level `owner` (the Level table) · `404 {error:"not_linked"}` when the app has no link · `200` and no row when the link's `keeps` is false already · one audit row `hand over repository` `detail{repo_id, full_name}` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted app |
| `GET /api/v1/github/repositories` | the repositories Canyon's Apps can see, and the apps linked to them (#1917) · `200 {repositories[], unreachable[]}` · each repository `{repo_id, full_name, org, default_branch, apps[]}`, by `full_name`: `repo_id` GitHub's repository id, `org` the account of the installation that sees it, `apps[]` its links, each `{folder, id, name, state}`, root first, then by folder · read from GitHub on each request, with each live installation's token (`GET /installation/repositories`), never stored · `unreachable[]` the accounts whose installation GitHub did not answer for, whose repositories are left out · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `POST /api/v1/github/repositories/:repo_id/import` | `{folder?, name?}` — makes an app of a repository, or of one folder of it (#1917) · `folder` as a link's: `""` (the default) or a path of segments of letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, joined by `/`, no segment `.` or `..`, at most 255 characters, else `400 {error:"invalid_folder"}` · `name` the app's display name as `POST /projects` checks it (else `400 {error:"invalid_name"}`), by default the folder's last segment, or the repository's name for the root · the app is made as `POST /projects {name}` makes one, the host picking its id, **with no owner** — never claimed: an admin adopts it (`PUT …/owner`, design/PERMISSIONS.md) — and linked, in one transaction, then its default branch's newest commit is checked as a push of it is, by the actor `github:<org>` · `201 {project}` · `404 {error:"not_found"}` when no live installation sees the repository · `409 {error:"linked", app}` when an app is linked to that repository and folder, `app` its id · `502 {error:"github_unavailable"}` when GitHub did not answer: nothing is made · one audit row `import repository` `detail{repo_id, folder, full_name}` on the new app · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member |
| `PUT /api/v1/projects/:id/repository` · `DELETE /api/v1/projects/:id/repository` | links an app to a repository, or a folder of it, and unlinks it (#1917) · PUT `{repo_id, folder?}` → `200 {repository}`, the project read's `repository` · `folder` as the import's · nothing is packaged: the next push is · `404 {error:"not_found"}` on an unknown or deleted app, or a repository no live installation sees · `409 {error:"linked", app}` when another app has that repository and folder · `409 {error:"app_linked"}` when this app has a link: unlink it first · `502 {error:"github_unavailable"}` · DELETE → `204`, and the app keeps every version · `404 {error:"not_linked"}` when it has none · one audit row each, `link repository` and `unlink repository`, `detail{repo_id, folder, full_name}` · `403 {error:"admin_required"}` for a member, both |

### Integrations

Canyon posts to the org's chat channels, Slack's incoming webhooks and Teams' workflow webhooks, so a person
hears that production needs them without having Canyon open (**added 2026-11-09**, #2725; design/INTEGRATIONS.md,
the channels row above). A message carries names only: never a secret's name or value, a resource's value, a
finding's code, snippet or fix, an app's log line, a URL's userinfo, an approval's `code` or anyone's address.

**The summary** is what a channel's save and its test post: what needs a person in production now, so the test
says something true. It counts, as an admin's `needs[]` reads them (*What needs a person*), production's `held`,
`down`, `refused`, `refused_code`, `approval` and `patch` needs and the app's `secret` need: D5's `Canyon will
post here when production needs a person. Right now: <n> production needs, across <apps>.`, or with none
`Canyon will post here when production needs a person. Right now nothing in production needs a person.`, then a
link to the fleet. `<apps>` names the apps by id, in id order, at most five, the last two joined by `and`, then `and <n> more`;
`1 production need` for one. Its model is `integrations.schema.json`'s `summary`, `{kind: "summary", needs,
apps[], more, link}`, and each channel's `kind` renders it:

- `slack`: `{"text": <the line>, "blocks": [{"type": "section", "text": {"type": "mrkdwn", "text": <the line>}},
  {"type": "actions", "elements": [{"type": "button", "text": {"type": "plain_text", "text": "Open in Canyon"},
  "url": <link>}]}]}`, each app name with `&`, `<` and `>` escaped as Slack asks;
- `teams`: `{"type": "message", "attachments": [{"contentType": "application/vnd.microsoft.card.adaptive",
  "content": {"$schema": "http://adaptivecards.io/schemas/adaptive-card.json", "type": "AdaptiveCard",
  "version": "1.4", "body": [{"type": "TextBlock", "text": <the line>, "wrap": true}], "actions": [{"type":
  "Action.OpenUrl", "title": "Open in Canyon", "url": <link>}]}}]}`.

The link is the host's public address (`CANYON_PUBLIC_URL`, as the skill manifest hands it out) and
`/#/fleet`. A post is one `POST` with `Content-Type: application/json`, no redirect followed, 10 s in all.

**The messages** (**added 2026-11-09**, #2725). Once a minute the server's own tick reads every app's needs as an
admin reads them, in the fleet read's fixed statements, under an advisory lock so one server ticks, whoever has
the dashboard open. It posts the needs the summary counts, and the org's `notify_failing` (the fleet read's
`org_needs`) to every channel but the one failing. A need's key is its app, `env` and `kind`, and for
`refused` and `refused_code` its `destination` and `port`; `notify_failing`'s is its `channel_id`. A channel
is told a need **once**, as it opens, and again only when it changes in a way a person acts on: `down`'s
`state`, the `version` of `approval`, `patch` or `secret`, `refused`'s `held`; a count going up is no change.
When a need has stayed gone 30 minutes, one line says it cleared, and one back within the 30 minutes posts
nothing. A `down` need of an app whose production state the tick could not read is neither posted nor
cleared. A channel's save makes what its summary just said what the channel was told, so none of it posts
again one by one. At most 20 messages a channel a minute: past 19, one says `<n> more messages about
production: open Canyon.` with the fleet's link. A message is `integrations.schema.json`'s `message`,
`{kind: "need", need, state, app, env?, fields, since?, link}`, `state` `open` or `cleared`, `app` absent on
`notify_failing`, `fields` at most `destination`, `port`, `version`, `state` and `channel` and never another
field the need adds, `since` on an open one alone; or `{kind: "overflow", more, link}`. It renders as the
summary does, its line D5's for its kind and state, an open one's ending ` Since <d Mon HH:MM> UTC.`, its
link the app's page, `/#/apps/<app>`, or `/#/apps/<app>/outbound` for `refused` and `refused_code`, or
`/#/settings/integrations` for `notify_failing`.

**The outbox.** Each message is a row the sender leases, so two servers never post one at the same time; a
channel's post a second apart, each in 10 s. **Delivery is at least once** (**amended 2026-11-12**, #2725): a
message can be posted twice when the server stops between the channel's `2xx` and marking the row sent, as the
row's lease then lapses and it is posted again, and when a post Slack or Teams took answers too late, as no answer
is retried. Their incoming webhooks take no idempotency key, so nothing on Canyon's side can tell. A `2xx` is sent. A `429`, a `5xx` or no answer is
retried, 30 s doubling to 15 minutes and never sooner than the answer's `Retry-After` in seconds, until the
message is an hour old, then dropped. A `404` or `410` drops it and marks the channel failing, audited
`integration.channel.failing` once, with the channel's id, name, kind and host and the answer's status, and
nothing more posts to it until an admin saves it again. Any other answer drops it. The rows are kept 30 days,
what was posted and what Slack or Teams answered: how an admin checks a send, not the audit read.

### The deploy repo

Under `delivery: repo` (design/HAND-OFF.md §3b; #2470) a release is a commit to the company's deploy
repo (the platform-repo row, above), not a webhook. Its row is the webhook's release row — the same id,
states, `sent_at`, pending read and report route — with `via: repo`. `contracts/testdata/release/repo/`
is meridian's preview release exactly as Canyon commits it, the templates it read, and the routes'
answers; the gitops package's test renders it byte for byte.

**The files**, under `<root>/<app>/<env>/`, every object's `metadata.namespace` `canyon-<app>-<env>` (at
most 63 characters, else the release is refused before any git call):

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `deployment.yaml` | `apps/v1` Deployment `<app>-<env>`, labels `canyon.app`, `canyon.target`; the pod template annotated `canyon.dev/release`, `canyon.dev/gate-run`, `canyon.dev/package`; the sidecar a native init container (`canyon-identity`, the release's sidecar image) with the release's sidecar env, `CANYON_ACCESS_TOKEN` from Secret `<app>-<env>-access-<hex>` key `CANYON_ACCESS_TOKEN` (`<hex>` the release id after `rel_`), an emptyDir at `/var/cache/canyon`, and, with Canyon's CA set, `SSL_CERT_DIR=/etc/canyon/ca` over ConfigMap `canyon-ca`; the app (`app`, the release's image) with the release's `app_env`, each variable from Secret `<app>-<env>-vars` key `<name>` and each resource's `gives` from Secret `<app>-<env>-res-<name>` key `<give>`, none optional; the executor's security contexts, resources and readiness probe on 3000 (`executor/kube`, the hand-off renderer); the pod pinned to `nodeSelector: {kubernetes.io/arch: <the release's platform.architecture>}`, as a `/run` pins its own (#2528); a body built by the release before (#2528), in flight across the upgrade, names no platform, and its Deployment carries no `nodeSelector` at all, as before — the one release that may (release.schema.json's `platform`) |
| `service.yaml` | `v1` Service `<app>-<env>`, 4180 to the pod's 4180 |
| `networkpolicy.yaml` | `networking.k8s.io/v1` NetworkPolicy `<app>-<env>`: ingress to TCP 4180 alone; no egress rule — the org's |
| `externalsecret.yaml` | `<root>/.canyon/externalsecret.yaml` rendered when the repo has it; else `external-secrets.io/v1` ExternalSecret `<app>-<env>-access-<hex>`: `refreshInterval: 1h`, the ClusterSecretStore named `store`, target the same name with `creationPolicy: Owner`, and `data: [{secretKey: CANYON_ACCESS_TOKEN, remoteRef: {key: <release>}}]` — the store's webhook reads `GET /releases/<key>/access-token` with the fetch-only token |
| `route.yaml` | `<root>/.canyon/route.yaml` rendered: one or more objects, one annotated `canyon.dev/address` |
| `resources/<name>.yaml` | `<root>/.canyon/resources/<type>.yaml` rendered for each resource the release lists: one or more objects, one annotated `canyon.dev/endpoint`, which make Secret `<app>-<env>-res-<name>` |
| `ca.yaml` | ConfigMap `canyon-ca`, key `ca.crt` Canyon's CA, only when it is set |
| `canyon.json` | `{canyon, app, env, release, gate_run, package, version}` (`release.schema.json`'s `canyon_json`): what the reference ApplicationSet's files generator finds; it binds nothing — the project and namespace are the ApplicationSet's own from the path |

Each file is the objects serialized from typed or parsed values in YAML, keys sorted, documents joined by
`---`; no file holds a secret value.

**Templates.** YAML, at most 64 KiB and 10 objects (documents counted), parsed as nodes. Inside scalar
values alone — never a key — `${app}`, `${env}`, `${namespace}` (`canyon-<app>-<env>`), `${name}` (the
resource's name; the app's in `route.yaml` and `externalsecret.yaml`), `${secret}` (the Secret the
template must make: `<app>-<env>-res-<name>`, or `<app>-<env>-access-<hex>`; `""` in `route.yaml`),
`${release}`, `${store}` and `${service}` (`<app>-<env>`) are replaced, the result always a string; any
other `${…}` is refused. Refused too, `template_invalid` with its reason (D5), the first in this order: over 64 KiB; not YAML;
over 10 objects (documents counted, an empty one too); an anchor or alias; a merge key `<<`; a duplicate key; a tag
spelled outside `!!str`, `!!int`, `!!float`, `!!bool`, `!!null`, `!!map`, `!!seq`; `${…}` in a key; an unknown or
unclosed variable; an object without `apiVersion`, `kind` or `metadata.name`; a `metadata.namespace` other than the
release's (absent is set to it); a kind Canyon knows is cluster-scoped (`Namespace`, `Node`, `PersistentVolume`,
`StorageClass`, `ClusterRole`, `ClusterRoleBinding`, `CustomResourceDefinition`, `APIService`, `PriorityClass`,
`ValidatingWebhookConfiguration`, `MutatingWebhookConfiguration`, `ClusterSecretStore`, `ClusterIssuer`,
`ClusterExternalSecret`); an object of a kind and name Canyon or another template makes, or named `<app>-<env>-vars`,
or `<app>-<env>-access-…` outside `externalsecret.yaml`; `route.yaml` without exactly one `canyon.dev/address`, the
report row's `address`; a resource without exactly one `canyon.dev/endpoint`, the report row's `endpoint` for its
type — both read from the committed bytes. A type an app declares with no template is `template_missing`; `route.yaml` absent is too. A namespace over 63
characters is `name_too_long`, before any template is read.

**The commit.** Author and committer `Canyon <canyon@<public host>>`, dated the release's `created_at`;
the message `<version> · <app> · <env>`, a blank line, `Canyon reviewed it: <public>/#/apps/<app>/runs/<gate_run>`,
a blank line, and the trailers `Canyon-Release: <release>`, `Canyon-Review: <gate_run>`,
`Canyon-Version: <package>`, `Canyon-Templates: <tree of <root>/.canyon, or none>`; a remove's message is
`remove · <app> · <env>` (`delete · <app> · <env>` for a project delete's), its trailers
`Canyon-Release` alone. One parent, the branch's head; never a merge, never a force. Before the push,
`git diff-tree` of the parent and the commit names only paths under `<root>/<app>/<env>/`, else nothing
is pushed and the release is `refused`. A directory with no `canyon.json`, or whose `canyon.json` names
another `canyon`, is `directory_taken`: Canyon never writes over a directory it did not write. A pull
request's branch is `canyon/<app>/<env>/<hex>`, its title `<version> · <app> · <env>`, into production's
branch; a branch already there is reused only when its head is one commit whose tree at the directory is
the render, whose diff from its parent stays under the directory, and whose parent is an ancestor of
production's head (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`), else `protected_branch`. Canyon lists the open pull requests first and keeps
the one whose `head.ref` is that branch, `head.repo.full_name` this repo's `<owner>/<repo>` (never a
fork's) and `base.ref` production's branch, and creates one only when none is; a create answered `422` or
`409` is looked for in the list again, and none such there is `pulls_api_unavailable`, tried again, never
another pull request kept as Canyon's. Opening one closes, through the API, every
other open pull request of this repo's own branch (never a fork's: it is never Canyon's) into production's branch whose `head.ref` starts `canyon/<app>/<env>/`. The pulls API is GitHub's REST
subset — `POST`, `GET` and `PATCH /repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls[/<number>]`, `GET …/pulls?state=open&per_page=100&limit=100&page=<n>`
(GitHub reads `per_page`, Gitea and Forgejo `limit`, capped at their own page size), page after page until one
is empty, at most 10 pages, open pull requests past that `pulls_api_unavailable` — with
`Authorization: token <token>` and no redirect followed; a pull request's `html_url` is kept only when it is
`https` on the repo's or the API's host, else `pulls_api_unavailable`. Under `api: gitlab` the calls are GitLab's
merge requests API (v4) at `api_url`, the project named by `url`'s path below GitLab's own root (`api_url`'s path
less `/api/v4`) without `.git`, URL-encoded whole (`group%2Fproject`): `GET /projects/<path>/merge_requests?state=opened&per_page=100&page=<n>`, read as the pulls
list is, `POST /projects/<path>/merge_requests` `{source_branch, target_branch, title, description}`, and `GET`
and `PUT /projects/<path>/merge_requests/<iid>`, the close `{state_event: "close"}` — with `PRIVATE-TOKEN: <token>`
and no redirect followed. A merge request is read as a pull request: `iid` its number, `web_url` its address,
`source_branch` and `target_branch` its `head.ref` and `base.ref`, `sha` its head, `state` `merged` merged, `closed`
closed unmerged and `opened` or `locked` open, and this repo's own branch only when `source_project_id` is
`target_project_id`. GitLab documents no answer to a second merge request of one branch, so looking first is what
finds it; a create that fails is `pulls_api_unavailable`, tried again. An attempt holds the repo's lock at
most 3 minutes, git's fetch, commit and push together, and git gives up a transfer slower than 1024 bytes a
second for 30 seconds; past either it is `unreachable`.

**Outcomes and problem codes.** Each attempt is one step, retried as a webhook delivery is (§3 of
design/HAND-OFF.md: a minute apart for five minutes, then fifteen, an hour in all), each retry a
`build.log` line naming its code: `unreachable` (no connection, a refused or timed-out dial, the host's
5xx) retries; `tls_untrusted` (a certificate Canyon does not trust: no retry fixes it), `auth_refused`
(401, or 403 or 404 on the repo), `no_write` (a refused push of the token), `protected_branch` (the remote
rejected the ref: a rule or a hook), `branch_missing`, `template_missing`, `template_invalid`,
`directory_taken` and `name_too_long` are `refused` at once; the hour's end is
`undelivered`, its last line naming the last code; `pulls_api_unavailable` is a pull request call that
failed and retries. A push that is not a fast-forward is fetched and rendered again, five times within
the attempt, then `unreachable`. A pushed release, or one whose pull request is open, is `accepted`,
recording `commit`, the branch, the tree Canyon rendered and the pull request; a commit whose tree at
the path is already the render pushes nothing and is accepted on the branch's head. A remove that
pushed is `removed` at once, with its audit row `release removed by commit` `detail{release, env,
commit}`, actor `{kind: canyon, subject: commit}`; one through a pull request is `removed` when merged,
unedited, into production's branch from this repo's own branch (the workflow asks once a minute for 30 minutes; an undeploy or a delete asked again asks once more) and
`refused` when closed unmerged, or merged edited, into another branch or from a fork's.

**Audit rows** (actor `{kind: canyon, subject: commit}` unless said): `commit release`
`detail{release, env, commit, branch, pull_request?}` once a release is accepted; `release live by
check-in`, `release tree mismatch`, `pull request closed` and `pull request changed` (the access row);
`release removed by commit`. None carries the token, the user name or a template's contents.

### Kept in GitHub (#1926)

**When one connected organization keeps code** (`GET /api/v1/github`'s `keeps_code`; #1926), Canyon keeps every app no repository feeds in a repository of its own there, and commits to it each version a review lets through. Canyon never syncs, merges, rebases or forces: it fast-forwards `main`, or it refuses and says why.

- **The repository.** An app with no link gets one the first time a version of it is made current by an upload (`POST …/packages`, never a check's) while a keeping organization is set: a new app at its first deploy, an app from before the keeping organization at its next. It is made in the background by the keeping organization's App, so an upload never waits on GitHub or fails because of it: a private repository named after the app's display name — lowercase, each run of characters other than letters and digits one `-`, trimmed of dashes, cut to 50, `app` when nothing is left — then `-` and four lowercase letters or digits from a cryptographic source (`claims-app-x7k2`), picked again when GitHub says the name is taken, and never a person's name or handle. The app is linked to it at its root, `keeps` true, with one audit row `create kept repository` `detail{repo_id, full_name}` by Canyon. When GitHub refuses, nothing is linked, the project read says why (`repository.kept_error`, below), and the next version made current tries again.
- **What is committed.** A version is committed once its first run after it was made current ends and its review let it through — `clean`, or `blocked` with every blocking finding covered by an unexpired approval, the rule a deploy goes on by — and it is still the app's current version. A pushed version is GitHub's already and never committed; a check's and a pull request's never are. The commit is the App's, `<name>[bot]`, its tree the version's, its message `<version> · <app id>`, a blank line, `Deployed with canyon deploy by <display>.` (`Uploaded by <display>.` when its run was a review alone), `Canyon's review passed: <n> blocking, <m> advisory.`, a blank line, `Canyon-Version: <package>`, `Canyon-Review: <public URL>/#/apps/<app id>`, and `Co-authored-by: <display> <work email>` of the person whose upload it was, left out when the upload carried no address. One audit row `keep version in github` `detail{package, commit}` by Canyon.
- **Fast-forward only.** The commit's parent is the commit `main` was at when the version was made current — the link's `kept_commit` at that moment (the last commit Canyon kept, or the last push GitHub delivered), recorded with the version and never moved after — and it is sent with a plain `git push` of that one commit to `main`, never forced, with an installation token of the keeping organization's App, which no log, error or answer carries. A push GitHub delivers for a repository Canyon keeps moves `kept_commit` to its head; a push of Canyon's own commit starts no check. When `main` is no longer at that recorded commit — someone pushed after the version was made, even if Canyon has since heard of the push — nothing is pushed, so a later push is never reverted by an older version, and `kept_error` reads *<version> is deployed but not in GitHub: someone pushed to main after it was made, and Canyon never overwrites a push. Run canyon pull, then canyon deploy.*; when GitHub refuses the push, `kept_error` is GitHub's reason, after *GitHub refused <version>:* — a rule on `main` (*Add Canyon's App to the rule's bypass list on GitHub, or hand this repository to developers.*), a file over 100 MB, or `.github/workflows/`, which the App has no permission to write. Either way the version stays current and deployed, Canyon keeps every version, and the next version tries again; `kept_error` is `""` again once one goes in.
- **Handing it over.** `PUT …/repository/keeps {keeps: false}` (#1938) ends Canyon's commits: a version made current after it is never committed, and a keep under way when it lands commits nothing, since a commit is made only while the link's `keeps` is true. The link, the repository and its history stay.
- **The read.** The single read's `repository` gains `keeps` (Canyon commits this app's versions), `kept_commit` (`null` before the first) and `kept_error` (`""` while nothing is wrong). An app no repository feeds while a keeping organization is set reads `repository` `{repo_id: null, full_name: "", folder: "", state: "pending", keeps: true, kept_commit: null, kept_error}` until its repository is made.

### Audit categories

Every action on the trail is in one category, read from this table at the read and never stored: a
screen filters on it (`GET /api/v1/audit?category=`) and shows it, and keeps no copy of this map.
`access` is who came in, who was turned away, and what they read. `outcome` is what a run's steps did: Canyon's verdict and
failures, the provisioner's resources, a preview deploy, the org's platform reporting a release made
or removed, Canyon committing a release to the deploy repo and a pod's check-in putting it live or a commit removing it (#2470), and Canyon moving an app's sign-in sidecar to its own image after an upgrade (`update sidecar`,
by Canyon, on the app, `detail{env, image, roll}`, one row per roll of an environment: executor.md, `POST /sidecars`;
**amended 2026-10-17**, #2305), and Canyon filing an egress request (`egress.request`, by Canyon, on the app,
`detail{env, destination}`: *The check*; **amended 2026-10-19**, #2116). `governance` is every change a person or an agent makes, the production
change record (`deploy prod`), and the org's platform reporting a release it could not make or an image
Canyon did not review. **An action this table does not name is `governance`**, so a new action is never
filtered out of an auditor's view.

|---|---|

| `sign out` | access |
| `sign in refused` | access |
